- **Past tense plus one remedy** in toasts: “Archived · Undo”, “Moved to Trash · Undo”, “Moved to Inbox · Undo”, “Unstarred · Undo” (in Starred, where the row leaves the view), “Marked 3 as unread · Undo”, “Snoozed until Tue 09:00 · Undo”, “Marked as spam · Undo”. Coalesced: “Archived 3 conversations”. Only actions that take a row out of view, or change several rows at once, get a toast, and so does time set aside on a calendar or removed from it (“Blocked Fri 10:00 - 10:30 · Undo”, §5.54), which no row shows and which may land on a day not on screen; starring or marking one conversation unread does not, because the row itself shows the change. Such a change is still undoable: Z reaches it for the grace period, and ⌘Z for as long as the undo history holds it (§6.1). A copy gets an inline mark, “Copied”, not a toast: nothing leaves the view and nothing can be undone (§5.14).
- **What cannot be undone comes first.** An unsubscribe's toast names the request, then the archive Undo takes back: “Unsubscribe sent · Archived · Undo” (§5.30).
- **An undo confirms itself** with an action-less toast naming the undone command as Edit › Undo does: “Undid Archive”, “Undid Move to Trash”, “Undid Mark as Unread”, “Undid Snooze” (`arrow.uturn.backward`, {{motion.duration.toastConfirm}} ms, §5.30).
- **Errors say what happened and what happens next:** “Couldn't reach Gmail · retrying in 30 s”, “Sign in required · Reconnect”, “Couldn't send · Open draft”, “Couldn't archive. Restored.” Never blame the user.
- **The separator** is the middle dot with spaces ( · ). The ellipsis is the single character (…), for commands that ask for more (“Add Account…”) and for ongoing work (“Sending…”).
- **Numbers** use the locale's grouping (“3,412 of 8,120”) and a space before units (“240 KB”, “4 s”). Unread counts are the exception: wherever one shows (the rail, the title menu, the Dock) it reads “{{layout.badge.cap}}+” above {{layout.badge.cap}}, and nothing at zero (§5.28).
- **Times** follow the system 12/24-hour setting and the locale's day and month order. Rows under running heads show what the head leaves out:

| Running head | Row time |
|---|---|
| Today, Yesterday | time of day: “23:39” |
| Earlier this week | weekday: “Tue” |
| Last week, a month (“October”), a month of an earlier year (“October 2025”) | day and month: “27 Oct”, “30 Oct” (the year is in the head) |
| No heads (search results, views under {{layout.runningHead.minConversations}} conversations) | today “23:39”, yesterday “Yesterday”, this week “Tue”, this year “30 Oct”, older “30 Oct 2025” |
| Later (no heads: its times are ahead) | when it comes back: today “Back 15:00”, within the next six days “Back Tue 09:00”, this year or less than eleven months ahead “Back 16 Nov 09:00” (a day and month ahead can only mean the coming one), later “Back 16 Nov 2027 09:00” |

  The reader shows “Yesterday 23:39”, “Fri 30 Oct”, “30 Oct 2025”.
- **Sender lines by view:** Drafts “Draft to Ben Carter”, Sent “To Ben Carter” (the prefix in `ink2`).
- **Empty states** are one serif sentence and one hint: “Nothing left to read.” / “New mail will land here.” Every view and reader state has its pair in §5.9.
- **Privacy copy** is literal: “Your mail is stored on this Mac and travels only between this Mac and Google or Microsoft.”
- **VoiceOver announcements** repeat the toast: “Archived. Press Z to undo.”, “Undid Archive.”
- **Demo copy** says what is made up and where it lives: “Demo mode: these mailboxes are made up and live only on this Mac.”

### 4.14 Accessibility summary

- Contrast: every text pair ≥ 4.5:1 and every essential non-text pair ≥ 3:1, in all four modes, over every surface and state overlay the components produce (§7). Disabled controls are exempt and use `inkDisabled`.
- Minimum text size {{platform.minTextSize}} pt; avatar initials never below {{typography.monogram.minSize}} pt.
- Hit targets: at least {{layout.button.minHit}} × {{layout.button.minHit}} pt for every pointer target, enlarging the hit rect where the visual is smaller (search clear, attachment and token remove, small buttons, link buttons, header hover actions, the notice dismiss button, Settings +/−); rail tiles {{layout.rail.tileHit}} pt, rows full width.
- Full keyboard operation; every focusable element shows the ring (§4.1).
- VoiceOver rows: “Unread. Starred. Priya Raman. Lisbon in March? Booked the flat… 22:10. 3 messages. Gmail account.” Queued rows add “Waiting to sync”; failed rows add “Couldn't archive”. A row's actions are its swipe's, Archive first, where they apply (“Archive”, “Mark as Read” or “Mark as Unread”, §5.7). Running heads are headings. Custom glyph buttons carry the action name as `accessibilityLabel`.

| State | Colour cue | Non-colour cue |
|---|---|---|
| Unread | the ink's pip and time | the pip's presence, Semibold sender, subject and time |
| Selected | `selWash` | {{border.marginRule.width}} pt margin rule, always drawn |
| Autocomplete highlight | `selWash` | {{border.marginRule.width}} pt margin rule (never a solid accent block) |
| Window not key | `selRuleIdle` | grey traffic lights, rule colour, idle wash |
| Starred | `star` | filled star vs outline |
| Queued locally | `ink3` glyph | `ss.queued` shape, VoiceOver “Waiting to sync” |
| Operation failed | `danger` glyph | triangle shape, toast text |
| Account | ring ink | kicker words, monogram, optional initial tile, compact square, tooltip, VoiceOver |
| Invalid address | `danger` wash and edge | triangle glyph inside the token |
| Error | `danger` | glyph and words |
| Toggle on | `accentFill` track | knob position: the knob carries a {{layout.toggle.knobEdge}} pt `ruleStrong` edge, ≥ 3:1 against the off track on a group and on the desk; nothing ever washes the track or the knob (a press stretches the knob), so the cue holds in every state |
| Checkbox / radio on | `accentFill` (hover `accentFillHover`, pressed `controlOnPressed`) | the checkmark, mixed bar or dot shape |
| Quick-reply mode | `ink` label | {{border.tabUnderline.width}} pt underline, Semibold (`labelStrong`) |
| Target message (R, A, F, Space) | — | R · A · F keycaps beside its footer buttons; a `ruleStrong` sheet edge when several messages are open |
| Missing subject or sender | `ink3` | italic, the words “No subject” / “No sender” |
| Demo mode | — | “Demo” tag text, window title, Settings banner words |
| Active tab | `ink` text | {{border.tabUnderline.width}} pt underline, Semibold |
| Focus | `focus` ring | ring shape, {{border.focusRing.widthHC}} pt in IC; inset ring on borderless composer rows; the insertion point in the composer body (§4.1); a Quire ring on links inside message bodies (§8.4) |
| Link button | `accent` label | position and verb; the underline on hover and press, and at rest under Graphite and where the theme's flag says so (§3.1, §4.16) |

### 4.15 Text size (Q2.6)

Settings › Appearance › **Text size**: a popup of Small, Default, Large and Larger (`textSize.names`) that moves every role where mail is read and written by {{textSize.deltas}} pt (`textSize.deltas`), and ⌘+ / ⌘− for the reader alone. macOS gives apps no text-size preference; this is the one Quire has, and the 11 to 14 pt the list and the reader set by default are small for some eyes.

- **The step.** A role's size and its line height move together, so each role keeps its leading; weight, tracking and family stay. No text goes below {{platform.minTextSize}} pt: at Small the {{platform.minTextSize}} pt roles (`meta`, `runningHead`, `tag`, `badge`, `kbd` and their variants) keep their size and line height. Twins move with their roles, so a twin still shares its role's line (§4.4) and a Font switch still moves nothing. Monograms follow the face's diameter, which no Text size changes (§5.29).
- **Where it applies.** The mail: the list's rows, running heads and skeleton rows (§5.7, §5.8, §5.10); the reader, whose headline, sheets, collapsed rows, chips, invitation card and plain and unstyled bodies take the step through its CSS (§5.12, §8.4), the boxes a sheet header's lines sit in growing with them (`--q-step-<role>`), while styled HTML keeps its own sizes as it keeps its own fonts; the quick reply (§5.18: its field gains the step per line, so the bar at rest is {{layout.quickReply.rest}} pt at Default and a point less or up to two more, and the face, the R keycap and the send button stay centred on the field at rest); a composer's subject and body (§5.20: its rows keep their heights) and the folded original under them. Everything drawn around a line of chrome keeps Default, because its bands and controls are sized for that line: the rail, the list header and its masthead, the sync line, the list's tail footer and server row, the reader's kicker and actions, buttons, fields, popups, tags, badges and keycaps, toasts, the undo panel, the HUD and the cheat sheet, empty states, the welcome screen and Settings (its 140 pt label column holds “Keep mail on this Mac” at `body` and no more), and what the system draws (menus, tooltips, alerts).
- **The features beside it.** Each follows the rule above. Send Later (§5.46): a waiting message's Drafts row is a row, its “Sends Mon 09:00” on the time line, and the draft sheet's “Sends Mon 09:00 · Cancel” is a sheet header line; Send's drop-down with its Send Later times is a menu. Attachments (§5.17): a chip's name and size and the “3 attachments · Download all” line take the reader's step, the Download button is a glyph and keeps its 24 pt target. Find in conversation (§5.50): the marks sit in the bodies at their text's size; the find bar is the reader's chrome, at Default as the list's search field is. A reader window (§5.49) is the reader: it takes the reader's own size with the main window's, and ⌘+ and ⌘− work there. Mute (§5.51): the muted glyph is a mark. A printout (§5.48) is paper and keeps Default. The Senders sheet (§5.52) and At login (§5.40) are Settings.
- **Rows.** Each line of a row moves by its role's step, the lines below it by the lines above, and the row's top pad by the step once more; a compact row's bottom pad too. Standard rows are {{textSize.rowHeights}} pt (`textSize.rowHeights`), compact rows {{textSize.rowCompactHeights}} pt (`textSize.rowCompactHeights`). The marks keep their sizes and stay centred on what they mark: the pip on the sender line, the face and its ring in the row, the time cluster (the tile, the Back tag, the queued glyph) on the sender line, the glyph cluster and the count badge on line 2. The margin rule keeps its inset ({{layout.row.marginRuleY}} pt, compact {{layout.rowCompact.marginRuleY}}) and takes the row's height. Nothing moves horizontally.

| Standard row | Small | Default | Large | Larger |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| height | 64 | 68 | 72 | 76 |
| sender line y, h | 9, 15 | 10, 16 | 11, 17 | 12, 18 |
| subject line y, h | 25, 17 | 27, 18 | 29, 19 | 31, 20 |
| snippet line y, h | 43, 14 | 46, 15 | 49, 16 | 52, 17 |
| avatar box y | 13 | 15 | 17 | 19 |
| pip y | 12.5 | 14 | 15.5 | 17 |
| margin rule y, h | 8, 48 | 8, 52 | 8, 56 | 8, 60 |

| Compact row | Small | Default | Large | Larger |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| height | 44 | 48 | 52 | 56 |
| sender line y, h | 6, 15 | 7, 16 | 8, 17 | 9, 18 |
| line 2 y, h | 23, 17 | 25, 18 | 27, 19 | 29, 20 |
| pip y | 10 | 11.5 | 13 | 14.5 |
| margin rule y, h | 6, 32 | 6, 36 | 6, 40 | 6, 44 |

- **Running heads** keep their heights ({{layout.runningHead.height}} pt, compact {{layout.runningHead.compactHeight}}) and their label's place; the label's line takes `runningHead`'s step (none at Small) and the hairline stays on its centre, on the device grid. **Skeleton rows** take the row's height; each bar stays centred on the line it stands for and the circle on the row, at their own sizes.
- **The reader's own size.** ⌘+ (View › Make Text Bigger) and ⌘− (View › Make Text Smaller) step the reader alone through the same sizes, from the one Settings chose: its headline, sheets and bodies, never the list, the quick reply or a composer. ⌘+ is disabled at Larger and ⌘− at Small, so the keys never pass a size the design does not draw; there is no ⌘0, which is All Inboxes (§6), and the other key comes back. The reader keeps its size across launches; choosing a Text size in Settings sets the reader to it too.
- **Live.** A change applies at once, with no relaunch and no reselection. The list re-measures its rows, keeping the selection and the scroll position, as a density change does: the row at the top stays at the top, as far into it as before, and only a selection the taller rows push out of view is scrolled back to. Every reader shell, the pre-rendered ones and one still loading included, takes the size and measures its bodies again; the headline is set again. Every open composer sets its subject and its body again, mid-edit included: bold, italic, underline, links, lists and quotes stay, and the caret stays where it was. The quick reply does the same. A change is not an edit: the undo stack is kept, and what an undo or a redo brings back takes the size shown then.
- **Budgets.** A change re-measures rows once; moving the selection still builds nothing (`RowContentCache` keys on the Text size, §5.7). The list is re-measured and drawn within 50 ms, and every pooled reader shell has measured its open bodies again within 100 ms, after a change in Settings and after ⌘+ or ⌘− alike (the Text size row of the budgets in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, read with the `list.remeasure` and `conversation.textSize` signposts).

### 4.16 Themes (1.8)

Five themes, chosen in Settings › Appearance › Theme (§5.40). **Study** is the default and the palette of §4.1–§4.3 value for value: its table in `tokens.json` is `color` itself, so nobody's app changes until they choose another. **Gallery**, **Rain**, **Grove** and **Dusk** are each named for the light the window reads like, as Quire names its own parts. A theme is a few seeds (a paper hue and strength, an ink per mode) and one rule applied to Study's table: every token keeps Study's luminance and changes only its hue and chroma, so every contrast ratio of §7 stays Study's. The few targeted adjustments are rules too, and no value is set by hand. `docs/design/source/themes_def.py` holds the seeds, `themes_lib.py` the rule and `theme_rules.py` the theme rules; `tokens.json` carries every theme's table under `themes` (Study first), with its Graphite values, both link flags, `focusOnPage` and `sheetIsPage` per mode.

{{TABLE:themes}}

Each caption says only what holds in every mode its tile is drawn in. No name is a brand, a paint-chip name, or a name an account ink or an avatar tint already uses.

**What never changes.** `originalPage` (white in every theme and mode), the light reading sheet (it stays the white page), the ten account inks, the eight avatar tints, every semantic mark (`success`, `warning`, `danger`, `info`, `star`, `findWash`, `findCurrent`), `onDanger`, `slipEdge`, and the elevation and toggle-knob shadows. Nor does the brand mark: the welcome screen draws it and the wordmark in the brand's black and white and inbox zero in the system's secondary label grey (§4.11), and the app icon is black and white in every theme (§5.42).

**What a theme can and cannot change.**

1. **Contrast reads only luminance.** A token at exactly its Study luminance, in any hue and chroma, keeps every ratio it is in, composited pairs included: an alpha wash over a surface moves only by 8-bit rounding. So each theme inherits Quire's pair-by-pair tuning in all four modes instead of redoing it. R4 holds every neutral token's `Y + 0.05` within 3 % of Study's. The only exceptions are tokens a theme declares deeper, and only Dusk declares any; even then the hairline, the idle selection and a quiet toggle's on state are not scaled with the surfaces but keep Study's ratio against the deeper surface they are drawn on (the ratio lock, R4b), so declared depth never weakens structure.
2. **The account palette leaves the ink only the blue-to-teal band.** An account's colour is its identity and does not change with the theme. With the ten account inks fixed, “no account ink within {{accountRules.minFromAccentNormal}} ΔE00 of the ink” (§4.2) leaves only LCh hue 190–256 open in all four modes. R5 also keeps the ink at least {{themeRules.accentVsInfoMinDE00}} from `info` (at least {{themeRules.accentVsInfoMinAnyVision}} under every simulation) and at least Study's own floor from every account ink under every simulation, and R10 asks it to stay at least {{themeRules.linkUnderlineMinDE00}} from the text ink (at least {{themeRules.linkUnderlineMinAnyVision}} under every simulation) so links need no underline. In Light and Light IC that leaves blue-black (Iron Gall), petrol and a deep teal. **So in light modes the paper carries a theme's identity, and the ink carries it at night.** Gallery is the exception: it has no coloured ink at all.
3. **The light sheet is the white page.** A styled message that declares its own colours is shown as sent on the light sheet (§5.14), because the sheet is the white page its sender designed for. Every theme's Light sheet is therefore no farther from white, and no more chromatic, than Study's own `{{color.sheet.light}}` (R1); the Light IC sheet is `#FFFFFF`, `raised` stays `#FFFFFF` in Light and Light IC, and `originalPage` is `#FFFFFF` everywhere. A link its sender left uncoloured on that page is the page's link, not the theme's: it keeps Study's colour in every theme (`--q-linkOnPage`, R11). The build computes `sheetIsPage` per theme and mode and requires it to be exactly “not dark”, which is what the reader's offer of Original colors assumes (§5.14): a future theme that tints its light sheet fails the build until the reader learns to adapt in light.
4. **A light paper has a floor of L\* 94.4** (92.0 in Light IC). Lilac, the lightest account ink, must keep 3:1 on the selected row, so `selWash` needs Y ≥ 0.679, and the selected row must sit 1.25:1 below the paper: together, paper Y ≥ 0.861. Every theme today keeps Study's L\* 98.2; the floor binds a future theme that declares light depth.
5. **Shadows are not themed.** The elevation shadows (`e1`–`e3`) and the toggle knob's shadow are Study's ink at low alpha in every theme. In a theme's own ink they would differ by at most 1.5 ΔE00, less than the step between two adjacent greys, so they stay one set and R0 says so.

#### Study (default)

“{{themes.0.caption}}” Daylight on a writing desk: Quire 1.7's `color` table in every mode, with Iron Gall. Its reader stylesheet is empty.

{{TABLE:themeKey:study}}

#### Gallery

“{{themes.1.caption}}” An evenly lit white room, with everything printed in black. The neutral ramp at chroma 0 (greys at Study's luminances), and **no coloured ink**: its accent family is the Graphite substitution of §3.1 resolved against its own greys, in all four modes. The unread pip and time, links, the margin rule, focus rings and primary buttons are the text ink; toggle and checkbox on-fills are the text ink with a paper knob; quiet toggles take the idle wash. Links are underlined at rest in every mode, since colour cannot mark them. Colour that carries meaning stays: the account inks, the star, success, warning, danger, info and find. It is the only theme whose marks have no hue, so its window differs from Study's as much in Dark as in Light.

- **The one departure from Graphite: the key selection wash.** The system Graphite accent makes the selected row the idle wash and lets the rule carry the difference, and the build stops asserting the two 1.25 scan cues there. A theme may not drop pairs, so Gallery's `selWash` is a grey at Study's selection luminance: the selected row sits as far from the list as in Study, and every pair is asserted.
- **What the build reads as under Graphite** (“inkless”): the account rule “no account ink within {{accountRules.minFromAccentNormal}} ΔE00 of the ink” (the ink is the text ink; R5 holds the palette's own floor between inks, {{themeRules.inklessAccentVsAccountMinDE00}}, and the closest, Fern in Dark IC, is what Study shows under Graphite); R3 (no coloured fill to keep near the asset); R8 (key and not-key selection differ by the rule, `ink` against `ink3`, and by luminance); and R9's floor on the selected row (Study's selection under Graphite).
- **Native chrome.** The compiled accent asset stays Iron Gall (§3.1). With the Multicolor system accent, an alert's default button, menu highlights and the Settings toolbar's selected tab stay Iron Gall in Gallery; with Graphite chosen in System Settings, native chrome is grey too and Gallery looks the same everywhere.

{{TABLE:themeKey:gallery}}

#### Rain

“{{themes.2.caption}}” An overcast afternoon indoors: the coolest paper of the five, the rail wet slate and the desk pale sky. The ink is a deep petrol by day and a clear aqua at night, and the slip is blue-black. In Light IC the ink moves to a bluer petrol, because no ink at Rain's own hue inside Light IC's luminance clears both R10 and R5. In Light the selection wash resolves to Study's own, because R9 keeps the Sea and Slate avatar faces off it. Rain's ink sits close to its blue-grey `ink2`, so links inside quotes are underlined at rest in every mode.

{{TABLE:themeKey:rain}}

#### Grove

“{{themes.3.caption}}” Shade under trees. The green lives in the paper; the ink cannot be green, since success, the star and the Jade and Fern accounts own that space. By day the ink is a deep teal, chromatic enough that the pip, the unread times and the margin rule read as ink rather than black and links need no underline; at night it is a soft sea-glass. The slip is green-black. In Light the selection wash is Study's own, as in Rain.

{{TABLE:themeKey:grove}}

#### Dusk

“{{themes.4.caption}}” The blue hour: the theme people choose for its night. It **declares depth** in Dark and Dark IC: the surfaces and the idle selection take 0.7 × Study's luminance, so the window is violet-black and 30 % dimmer than Study's. The hairline, the idle selection and the quiet-toggle washes keep Study's ratio against the deeper surface they sit on (the ratio lock), and Dark declares a **night ink**: body text keeps Study's ratio on the deeper sheet instead of the higher one it would reach, and keeps at least {{themeRules.nightInkMinOnFindCurrent}}:1 on `findCurrent` after 8-bit rounding. Its marks are sky-blue. By day it is a faint heather paper with an ink close to Iron Gall, since in light modes the band leaves no other blue (point 2 above): the paper carries it, as the caption says.

{{TABLE:themeKey:dusk}}

Tokens each theme keeps from Study in all four modes: Gallery {{THEME_SAME:gallery}}. Rain {{THEME_SAME:rain}}. Grove {{THEME_SAME:grove}}. Dusk {{THEME_SAME:dusk}}. Every value of every theme is in `tokens.json` (`themes[].color`).

**How far apart they are.** Mean CIEDE2000 over the tokens that carry identity (`spine`, `canvas`, `paper`, `sheet`, `slip`, `selWash`, `accent`); about 2 is a visible tint across a pane, 4 and up reads as another window:

{{TABLE:themeDistance}}

`paper` alone, `spine` (the rail) alone, and the ink (`accent`) alone:

{{TABLE:themeDistance:paper}}

{{TABLE:themeDistance:spine}}

{{TABLE:themeDistance:accent}}

In light modes the papers and rails carry the coloured themes and the coloured inks cluster, as point 2 predicts: Dusk sits close to Iron Gall, and Rain and Grove close to each other. At night the four coloured inks spread apart, and Gallery's colourless ink is far from every other theme's in every mode. Under the system Graphite accent every theme is inkless, so there Study and Gallery differ only by their paper.

**Reading comfort.** Every theme but Dusk keeps Study's light levels and text contrast to within 2 %. Dusk's night is 30 % dimmer, with body text dimmed so it stays near Study's contrast instead of glaring, and the `ink` / `ink2` ratio keeps names and snippets apart:

{{TABLE:themeComfort}}

**The derivation.** The seeds:

{{TABLE:themeSeeds}}

The rule, per mode:

1. **Neutral ramp** ({{themeRules.neutralTokens}}). Each takes Study's relative luminance, multiplied by the declared depth for `spine`, `paper`, `canvas`, `sheet` and `raised` in Dark and Dark IC. Hue is the paper hue. Chroma is min(7, max(floor, k × Study's chroma)), where k is the paper factor (0.8 of it for the seven ink tokens) and the floor applies only to the six surfaces in dark modes. Out of gamut, chroma steps down by 0.1. Alpha tokens keep their alpha; pure white and pure black stay.
2. **Ratio lock** (declared depth only). `rule` takes the luminance that gives Study's `rule`-on-`sheet` ratio on the theme's deeper `sheet`, `selWashIdle` Study's ratio on the deeper `paper`, and `accentWash`, `accentWashHover` and `accentWashPressed` Study's ratio on the deeper `sheet`, on the same side of it, at the theme's hue and chroma.
3. **Night ink** (declared; Dusk, Dark only). `ink` takes the luminance that gives Study's `ink`-on-`sheet` ratio on the deeper sheet, never below {{themeRules.nightInkMinOnFindCurrent}}:1 on `findCurrent`, its tightest pair. After 8-bit rounding the build takes the first value that meets the floor, not the nearest.
4. **The light sheet is the page.** In Light and Light IC, `sheet` gives up chroma until it is no farther from white than Study's sheet, with C\* ≤ {{themeRules.sheetMaxChroma}}.
5. **Fixed and derived.** `slipEdge` is Study's; `originalPage`, `onAccent` and `onSwipe` are `#FFFFFF` (Gallery's `onAccent` is its paper, as under Graphite; `onSwipe` is white everywhere, the swipe button's icon and label, §5.7); `selRuleIdle` = `ink3`; `scrimSolid` = `scrim` over `canvas`. The semantic tokens are Study's, except as rule 10 says.
6. **The ink as a mark.** `accent` = `focus` = `selRule` = the seed.
7. **Fills** (`accentFill`, `accentFillHover`, `accentFillPressed`, `controlOnPressed`). Study's luminance in the ink's hue, chroma × min(1.25, seed C / Iron Gall's C); then all four are pulled together toward Iron Gall until `accentFill` is at most {{themeRules.fillVsAccentAssetMaxDE00}} ΔE00 from the compiled accent asset (R3). In dark modes each on-state fill is then lifted until it keeps 3.03:1 on `sheet` and `canvas`, with white at least 4.6:1 on it. `swipeArchive` is `accentFill` as finally lifted.
8. **Washes.** The quiet-toggle washes take Study's luminance (or rule 2's) in the ink hue, chroma × min(1.3, that ratio). `selWash` takes Study's luminance, never deepened, so the 1.25 scan cue holds, and is never paler than Study's; its hue steps toward Study's selection hue until every avatar face clears its floor on it (R9). `selWashIdle` then gives up chroma until it is at least {{themeRules.selectionKeyVsIdleMinDE00}} ΔE00 from `selWash` and every avatar face clears its floor on it.
9. **Inkless** (Gallery). After rules 1–5, the accent-family tokens of `graphite` are resolved against the theme's own final neutrals, exactly as the system Graphite accent resolves them; `selWash` alone keeps Study's selection luminance in the theme's neutral.
10. **Banners stay banners.** A semantic wash closer than {{themeRules.bannerVsPageMinDE00}} ΔE00 to the theme's `sheet` or `paper`, or closer to its `canvas` than the smaller of that and Study's own distance, gains chroma at its own hue and luminance until it clears.
11. **Derived flags and page colours.** Both link flags (R10), `--q-focusOnPage` (the theme's light focus, Light IC's under Increase Contrast, in both appearances, since the page is white in both) and `--q-linkOnPage` (Study's accent, R11). Nobody sets any of these by hand.

Because the adjustments are rules, a later change to Study's table re-derives every theme and the build re-checks it. The banner washes rule 10 moved (hue and luminance kept to 8-bit rounding, so every banner text pair keeps its ratio):

{{TABLE:themeBanners}}

Dusk's ratio lock, against Study:

{{TABLE:themeRatioLock}}

**Graphite and Increase Contrast.** The Graphite substitution of §3.1 is unchanged and resolves against **the active theme's** neutrals: a Rain user with a Graphite system accent gets Rain's blue-grey ramp and an ink margin rule. The build regenerates the Graphite pairs for each theme, and under Graphite links are underlined at rest in every theme (§3.1). Gallery's own table already is its Graphite table, except `selWash`, which under the system accent becomes the idle wash as in every theme. Key Graphite values (all of them are in `tokens.json`, `themes[].graphite`):

{{TABLE:themeGraphite}}

Each theme's Light IC and Dark IC tables are derived from **Study's IC values** by the same rule, so Increase Contrast raises contrast in every theme by the steps Study takes: darker inks, stronger `rule` and `ruleStrong`, a white light sheet, Dusk's ratio-locked hairline included. The IC-only geometry (3 pt focus rings, the `ruleStrong` outline on sheets, 1 pt hairlines, the stronger `slipEdge`) is untouched, and the account inks keep their 3.5:1 IC floor on every theme's surfaces.

**The theme rules.** Asserted for every theme beside the pairs, the Graphite pairs and the account rules (`theme_rules.py`; the constants are `themeRules` in `tokens.json`). “Inkless” says how a rule reads Gallery, the way the build reads the system Graphite accent.

| Rule | Asserts | Why |
|---|---|---|
| R0 | every theme has every token in all four modes; the default theme equals `color` byte for byte; the elevation and knob shadows are one set | completeness; nobody's app changes until they choose |
| R1 | the Light and Light IC `sheet` no farther from white than Study's, with C\* ≤ {{themeRules.sheetMaxChroma}}; `raised` white in the light modes; `originalPage` white in every mode | the page under styled mail stays the page its sender designed for (§5.14) |
| R2 | the theme's light `focus` at least {{themeRules.focusOnPageMinContrast}}:1 on white (it is `--q-focusOnPage`) | the focus ring on the Original colors page |
| R3 | `accentFill` at most {{themeRules.fillVsAccentAssetMaxDE00}} ΔE00 from the accent asset in each mode. Inkless: not asserted | native chrome cannot follow a switch (§3.1) |
| R4 | no neutral token's `Y + 0.05` moves more than 3 % from Study's, except tokens the theme declares deeper (inkless: `knob`, accent family under Graphite) | the luminance lock: depth is declared, never drifted into |
| R4b | declared depth: `rule` on `sheet`, `selWashIdle` on `paper` and the three `accentWash` tokens on `sheet` keep 99 % of Study's ratio; a declared night ink keeps {{themeRules.nightInkMinOnFindCurrent}}:1 on `findCurrent` after rounding | depth never weakens hairlines, quote bars, the idle selection or a toggle's on state |
| R5 | the ink at least {{themeRules.accentVsInfoMinDE00}} ΔE00 from `info` (at least {{themeRules.accentVsInfoMinAnyVision}} under protan, deutan and tritan), at least the smaller of {{themeRules.accentVsAccountMinAnyVision}} and Study's own from every account ink under every simulation, and at least {{themeRules.accentVsMeaningMinDE00}} from success, warning, star and danger. Inkless: at least {{themeRules.inklessAccentVsAccountMinDE00}} from every account ink | the unread pip, links and an account dot never look like the info glyph or each other |
| R6 | `ink`, `ink2` and `ink3` at least {{themeRules.textVsMeaningMinAnyVision}} ΔE00 from success, warning, star and danger under every simulation | a meaning never reads as text |
| R7 | each banner wash at least {{themeRules.bannerVsPageMinDE00}} ΔE00 from `sheet` and `paper`, and at least the smaller of that and Study's own from `canvas` | a banner never sinks into a tinted page, or into Settings' desk |
| R8 | `selWash` at least {{themeRules.selectionKeyVsIdleMinDE00}} ΔE00 from `selWashIdle`. Inkless: not asserted (the rule tells key from not key, as under Graphite) | key and not-key selection stay apart |
| R9 | each avatar face at least the smaller of {{themeRules.avatarFaceVsSurfaceMinDE00}} ΔE00 and its Study distance from `paper`, `sheet`, `canvas`, `selWash` and `selWashIdle`. Inkless: on `selWash`, the Study distance under Graphite | no face sinks further than it does in Study |
| R10 | two flags. **links** (`linkUnderlineAtRest`): every link is underlined at rest where the ink is under {{themeRules.linkUnderlineMinDE00}} ΔE00 from `ink`, or under {{themeRules.linkUnderlineMinAnyVision}} under any simulation. **quoteLinks** (`quoteLinkUnderlineAtRest`): links inside quotes, where the same holds against `ink2` | colour must not be the only cue, beside body text or beside quoted text |
| R11 | `--q-linkOnPage` (a link its sender left uncoloured, in a body neither Quire-set nor adapted) is never closer than Study's accent to the text a sender's page sets (`#000`–`#444` light, `#FFF`–`#CCC` dark), normal or under any simulation | the sender's text, not the theme's, is what such a link sits beside |

**Results.** {{THEMES_SUMMARY}}. Per theme and mode, the tightest pair is the smallest margin over its need among pairs needing 3 or more; they are Study's own, because every luminance is Study's:

{{TABLE:themeChecks}}

¹ Inkless: “no account ink within {{accountRules.minFromAccentNormal}} ΔE00 of the ink” is read as under Graphite, and R5 holds {{themeRules.inklessAccentVsAccountMinDE00}} instead; Study under the Graphite system accent shows the same distance.

The theme rules' values:

{{TABLE:themeRules}}

**Links, both flags.** Every link: link buttons (`.ss-link`), invitation links (`.ss-ilink`) and every link in adapted and Quire-set bodies (`ss-links`, §8.4). Links in quotes: links inside `blockquote` and `.ss-quote` in Quire-set bodies (`ss-qlinks`). Under Graphite both are on in every theme. Study needs neither flag in any mode, so Study draws its links as before.

{{TABLE:themeLinks}}

**Switching.** Live, with no relaunch, Settings included and keyboard focus kept (§5.40). The theme is one field in the environment snapshot every colour provider already reads (`QuireEnvironment.theme`), one SwiftUI environment value (`quireTheme`), one redraw sweep of every window, and one replaced `<style id="ss-theme">` in each reader document (§8.2–§8.4). Colour never changes a height, so a switch measures nothing: the list is redrawn within 50 ms and every pooled reader shell restyled within 100 ms, and nothing runs at idle (the Theme row of the budgets in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`). Study's reader stylesheet is empty; another theme's is at most about 7.7 KB per shell and 2.2 KB per body document, holding only what differs from Study.

**Limits the check makes visible.**

- Study's own Stone avatar face sits close to Study's `selWashIdle` in Light and Light IC. That is today's look, and R9 measures every other theme against it; changing it would change the default, so it waits for a change made on its own terms.
- Study's ink is close to Lilac under deuteranopia in Dark IC; R5 takes Study's value as the floor there.
- The new inks sit near their floors by construction: each is the quietest that clears the rules, and the build re-checks them on every change to Study's table.
- In Light and Light IC the coloured inks cluster (point 2). The papers carry those themes by day and the inks separate at night; a wider band would mean changing account inks per theme, which would change an account's colour with the theme.
- Links inside quotes are underlined at rest in more modes than every link, because a blue or teal ink sits near a blue-grey or tinted `ink2`. Only links inside quotes in Quire-set bodies are affected.
- Under the system Graphite accent, a link its sender left uncoloured stays the ink on the page, as before 1.8, so it can vanish beside dark text when the sender also removed its underline.

### 4.17 Pointer (1.8.1)

The pointer tells what a click will do before it is made.

| Over | Pointer |
|---|---|
| Anything a click acts on: buttons of every kind (icon, text, link-style, Download and Download all, toolbar, header and footer actions, banner actions and dismiss crosses, invitation answers, Send and its drop-down, a toast's Undo), every link (in message bodies too, the ones the reader linked in plain text included, a phone number, which opens in FaceTime, and a link to a place in its own message, which scrolls the reader there, whatever cursor the sender's CSS asks for), chips, tiles (rail folders and accounts, theme tiles), rows (conversation rows, Settings' account rows, a collapsed message, the fold), each recipient of the open list and the sender's address (§5.14), the recipients toggle, popup and menu buttons, checkboxes, radios, toggles, tabs, disclosure controls, a scrim that a click closes | the pointing hand |
| Text that takes typing or selection: a field's whole face (its padding, glyph and keycap too, since a click there puts the caret in the field), the Later sheet's time digits, the composer, message bodies (a body's link that a click does nothing with, to `#` or to an inline picture, included), the reader's headline | the I-beam |
| A control that is disabled, or that a click would leave as it is: a loading button, the pending account tile, the chosen radio, theme tile or tab; words, keycaps, running heads, the open list's labels, commas and gaps, an overlay scroller while it shows, and every other surface | the arrow |
| Rows, files and pictures being dragged (§5.2, §5.7, §5.20, §5.22) | the system's move and copy pointers, unchanged |
| A window's edge, a split divider | the system's resize pointers, unchanged |
| AppKit's own chrome, which the app neither draws nor can lay a view on: a window's title-bar buttons (close, minimise, zoom), the menu bar and every menu (context menus, More, Later, Send options, a popup's list), the Settings window's toolbar tabs, the open panel (Attach, Insert Picture), Quick Look | the system's own pointer, unchanged |

- A control's pointer covers its hit rect (§4.14), never the focus-ring margin, and one region alone lies under any point: a control inside another (a field's clear button, the sync line's link, the find bar's buttons) keeps its own, and its container leaves that area out.
- A surface that lies on something clickable shows the arrow, so nothing under it shows through: the find bar and the quick reply on the reader's page, a toast on the list, the composer's suggestions and link popover on its fields and body, the cheat sheet's panel on its scrim. The G-sequence HUD takes no clicks, which go to the rows under it, so it shows theirs.
- A text view's or field's I-beam is its own bounds, cut out of what lies on it, never AppKit's over its visible rect, which reaches past them now that views no longer clip to their bounds: the composer's editor leaves the quote's chip and attribution and the failed-send banner their own pointers.
- An overlay scroller takes the click only while it shows: the rows and the page under it leave it the arrow then, and have its strip back once it fades.
- **AppKit.** Every `QuireControl` is a `QuirePointerTarget`: it adds `pointerCursor` over its hit rect as a cursor rect (a loading button, a field and a group's chosen radio override it), and `pointerRegions` lists the rects with the controls inside cut out (`QuirePointerRegion`). Another view that acts on a click adopts `QuirePointerTarget`; a control AppKit makes, such as an alert's buttons, gets a `QuirePointerOverlay`; the Later sheet's calendar gets one over all of it, its caption, weekday letters and past days included, since AppKit tells nothing of where they lie and its cell's hit test calls every point of it trackable. A surface laid over others adopts `QuirePointerCover`, and the targets and text views under it leave its frame out (`pointerCoverAreas()`), asked again once per turn of the run loop when it comes, goes, hides or its frame changes. A text view or field adds its I-beam with `addTextPointerRects()`, and a field being edited leaves the field editor's clip without the arrow AppKit gives it. Cursor rects apply in the key window only, so the undo-send panel's action, in a panel that never becomes key, also sets the pointer from its tracking area (§5.31), and takes it back when it goes, hides or changes under a resting pointer.
- **SwiftUI.** Every Quire style and control applies `.quirePointer()`, which is `.pointerStyle(.link)` while the control is enabled and acts; a `QuireButtonLabel` that is loading tells its button's style, which then shows the arrow; a field or text area takes `.horizontalText` over its whole face. SwiftUI shows no hand over a link inside text, so the inline link (§5.38, §5.40) lays a box with the hand over its own words.
- **Reader.** The shell has two rules: `button, [role="button"]` take `cursor: pointer`, and `:disabled` or `aria-disabled` ones `default`, as does the invitation's chosen answer, which a click leaves as it is (§5.14). Body documents set every element back to `auto` (the I-beam over text, the arrow elsewhere), then `cursor: pointer` on every `a[href]` and `area[href]`, and on a sender's `summary`, which opens its `details` with no script, then `text` on a link to `#` or to an inline picture, which a click leaves as it is; each is `!important` and as specific as the one before it or more, so a sender's sheet never wins (§8.4). The sanitizer drops a sender's `cursor` declarations. A click on a phone number opens it in FaceTime; one on a link to a place in its own message scrolls the reader to that place.

---
## 5. Components

Conventions: metrics in points; x and y from the component's top-leading corner; a row height includes its bottom hairline. Unless a component says otherwise its states are: **hover** = `hover` wash, glyph and text to `ink`, no animation; **pressed** = `pressed` wash (link buttons, checkbox and radio on fills and toggles have their own hover and press, §5.24 and §5.26, so that no wash ever pulls a state mark under its floor); **focused** = the focus ring (§4.1); **disabled** = `inkDisabled` text and glyph, no wash, no hover feedback, the arrow (§4.17); **window not key** = only the marks listed in §3.4 change (selection wash and rule, the quick reply's mode underline, primary buttons, focus rings); every other mark, the ink's included, stays. Every metric table below is rendered from `layout.<component>` in `tokens.json`.

### 5.1 App window and title bar

Anatomy: rail ({{layout.rail.width}}; {{layout.rail.width26}} from macOS 26 on, §3.2) · list pane ({{layout.list.defaultWidth}} default, {{layout.list.minWidth}}–{{layout.list.maxWidth}}, user-resizable, autosaved; its maximum gives up {{layout.calendarPane.stripWidth}} pt while the day column is on, §3.2) · reader (flexible) · day column ({{layout.calendarPane.columnWidth}}, fixed, on by default, folding to a {{layout.calendarPane.stripWidth}} pt strip while the reader would fall under {{layout.calendarPane.readerMinWithColumn}} beside it, §5.54). Pane dividers are hairlines with a {{layout.window.dividerHit}} pt `NSSplitView` hit area. The split view keeps its three items: the reader's holds the reader and, at its trailing edge, the day column, which has no divider to drag (one width, nothing to remember), only its own leading hairline. Each pane paints its own background up to the top edge: rail `spine`, list, both headers and the day column `paper`, reader desk `canvas`, the strip `canvas`.

The default width ({{layout.window.defaultWidth}}) is the macOS 26 rail, the default list, the narrowest reader that keeps its keycaps ({{layout.window.readerKeycapsMin}}, §5.36) and the day column, so a new window's reader keeps its keycaps beside the column (§3.2).

{{TABLE:layout:window}}

| State | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Key | coloured traffic lights; the ink's selection, mode underline `ink`, primary buttons filled |
| Not key | system grey lights; exactly the §3.4 list changes (idle selection, `ink3` underlines, primary buttons as secondary, no focus ring); the unread pip and unread time, badges and every other mark stay |
| Full screen | traffic lights hidden by the system; layout unchanged, title-bar zone keeps {{layout.window.titlebarZone}} pt |
| Minimal mode (⌃⌘S, View › Hide Account Rail) | rail width 0, **instantly** in every mode (the split view snaps in one frame, so no row re-truncates and no iframe re-measures over frames); the masthead moves to x = rail width + {{layout.listHeader.mastheadMinimalGap}} ({{layout.listHeader.mastheadMinimalX}} on macOS 15, {{layout.listHeader.mastheadMinimalX26}} from macOS 26 on, §3.2) and becomes the title menu's button again (chevron, §5.4), with the views and an Accounts section, so every folder and account the rail held is still a click away; search and compose keep the header line and search takes the width that is left (§5.3); `⌘0…⌘9` and the G-sequences keep working |
| Live resize | the list keeps its width; the reader takes the change, the day column keeping its {{layout.calendarPane.columnWidth}} pt until the reader would fall under {{layout.calendarPane.readerMinWithColumn}}, when it folds to its strip in one frame (and back); the reader re-fits wide HTML (§5.14) once, when the resize ends |
| Day column (on by default; D, View › Hide Day, the reader header's Day button; §5.54) | the column at the reader's trailing edge, the window's full height, its header sharing the horizon; hidden, the reader takes its {{layout.calendarPane.columnWidth}} pt back **instantly**, as the rail; the window keeps the choice across launches, as it keeps a hidden rail |
| Day column folded (window width − rail − list − {{layout.calendarPane.readerMinWithColumn}} < {{layout.calendarPane.columnWidth}}) | the {{layout.calendarPane.stripWidth}} pt strip in the column's place; a click on it or D opens the whole column over the reader's trailing edge until Esc, D or a click in the reader (§5.54) |
| Week (W, View › Show Week, the Week button) | the week takes the reader's place and the column's; W or D brings both back (§5.54) |
| Narrow reader (< {{layout.window.readerNarrow}} pt) | column margins {{layout.reader.narrowColumnMargin}}, sheet padding x {{layout.reader.narrowSheetPadX}} (§5.14); below {{layout.window.readerKeycapsMin}} pt the reader's keycaps hide (§5.36) |

### 5.2 Account rail and its items

Anatomy, top to bottom: title-bar zone (traffic lights) · **the folders**, one tile per view in the title menu's order: Inbox at y = {{layout.rail.folderTop}}, then Starred, Sent, Drafts and Trash at a {{layout.rail.folderPitch}} pt pitch, a {{layout.rail.dividerWidth}} pt hairline at y = {{layout.rail.folderDividerY}}, and All Mail at y = {{layout.rail.allMailTop}}; while something in the scope is snoozed the **Later** tile sits directly below Inbox, at y = 106, and Starred to Trash, the hairline and All Mail each move down one pitch (Starred at 150 … Trash at 282, the hairline at 329, All Mail at 338); while a message in the scope is on its way the **Outbox** tile sits between Drafts and Trash, at y = 238, and Trash, the hairline and All Mail each move down one pitch (Trash at 282, the hairline at 329, All Mail at 338); with both, each shown tile takes its pitch: Later at 106, Starred at 150, Sent at 194, Drafts at 238, the Outbox at 282, Trash at 326, the hairline at 373 and All Mail at 382 · flexible space · **the accounts**, anchored just above Add: the All Inboxes tile, a {{layout.rail.dividerWidth}} pt hairline {{layout.rail.dividerGap}} pt below it, then the account tiles from {{layout.rail.accountGap}} pt below the hairline at a {{layout.rail.accountPitch}} pt pitch · Add · Settings. Opaque `spine`, trailing hairline. All y values are top edges: Add's {{layout.rail.utility}} pt button has its **top** at bottom − {{layout.rail.addTopFromBottom}}, Settings' top at bottom − {{layout.rail.settingsTopFromBottom}}. The account stack's clip ends {{layout.rail.stackBottomGap}} pt above Add's top and the last tile ends {{layout.rail.stackInset}} pt inside it, so in the default {{layout.window.defaultHeight}} pt window with three accounts the last tile spans 652–690, the first starts at 552, the hairline is at 538 and All Inboxes starts at 484.

- **Folder tiles**: {{layout.rail.tile}} pt, radius `r.tile`, the view's symbol (`{{icon.viewInbox}}`, `{{icon.viewStarred}}`, `{{icon.viewSent}}`, `{{icon.viewDrafts}}`, `{{icon.viewTrash}}`, `{{icon.viewAllMail}}`) {{layout.rail.folderGlyph}} pt in `ink2`, with the All Inboxes tile's washes, raised selection and focus ring, but no margin rule: the rail carries two selections, the view (the raised tile) and the scope (the margin rule), and they must read apart. A click switches the view as the title menu does (§5.4); the G-sequences are unchanged. Inbox carries the unread count badge of the current scope's Inbox (all accounts, or the narrowed one; the count the title menu's Inbox item carries); the other folders carry none. Tooltips name the folder and its sequence: “Inbox  G I”, “Starred  G S”, “Sent  G T”, “Drafts  G D”, “Trash  G #”, “All Mail  G A”.
- **Later tile** (§5.45): `{{icon.viewLater}}` {{layout.rail.folderGlyph}} pt, the folder tiles' looks and states, shown only while at least one conversation in the current scope (all accounts, or the narrowed one) is snoozed, and gone the moment the last one comes back or ends, or the scope moves to an account with none; its list returns to the Inbox then (a search typed there stays, §5.45). The tiles move instantly, as everything in the rail does. Tooltip “Later  G L”; no badge (what is snoozed is not waiting to be read); it takes no drop (a snooze needs a time, which a drop cannot give). ↑/↓ step over it while it is hidden; if it has the keyboard as it goes, the Inbox tile takes it.
- **Outbox tile** (§5.53): `{{icon.viewOutbox}}` {{layout.rail.folderGlyph}} pt, the folder tiles' looks and states, shown only while at least one message in the current scope (all accounts, or the narrowed one) is on its way, that is listed in the Outbox, and gone the moment the last one has gone (taken by the provider, kept in Drafts as failed, cancelled, continued or taken back by its Undo), or the scope moves to an account with none; its list returns to the Inbox then (a search typed there stays, as from Later). A failed send may show it for the moment before it is kept in Drafts; a send leaving as another arrives never hides it, since the rail reads the whole outbox at once. Tooltip “Outbox  G O”; no badge (what is on its way is not waiting to be read); it takes no drop (a message on its way is not mail a drop could move there), and rows dragged out of it drop nowhere. ↑/↓ step over it while it is hidden; if it has the keyboard as it goes, the Inbox tile takes it.
- **Dropping rows**: conversation rows dragged from the list (§5.7) drop on four folders: Inbox moves them to the Inbox, Trash trashes them, Starred stars them and All Mail archives them, exactly as ⇧I, #, S and E do on those conversations, as one change for every dragged row with that key's toast and Undo (in Drafts, Trash is Discard Draft…, asked first, as # is there; §5.12), except that Starred only ever stars. Sent, Drafts, Later, the Outbox, All Inboxes, the account tiles and the utilities take no drop; rows dragged out of Later onto Inbox move back now and end their snooze, as ⇧I does there. A folder refuses a drop that would change nothing the list shows: the selected folder outside a search (its rows are already there; a search lists the rows of the view it looks through, All Mail or the one an `in:` term picks (§5.6), so then only the rows decide, except that Trash refuses `in:trash` results, as # does nothing there), Inbox for rows all in the Inbox, Starred for rows all starred, All Mail for rows none of which is in the Inbox; a refused drag lights nothing and ends where it began. Only the list's own rows are taken: files, text and another app's drags are refused. A drag counts over the {{layout.rail.tileHit}} pt hit square, as a click does, and nowhere else: the folders' squares abut, so a drag at one square's rim never lands on its neighbour. The cursor shows a move, or a copy while ⌥ is held, following the modifiers as they change over a folder.
- **All Inboxes tile**: {{layout.rail.tile}} pt, radius `r.tile`, `ss.quire` {{layout.rail.unifiedGlyph}} pt in `ink2`; the first tile of the account group.
- **Account tile**: a {{layout.rail.face}} pt face (the account ink at {{layout.rail.faceAlpha}} opacity over `sheet`), the domain monogram in New York Semibold {{layout.rail.monogram}} pt ({{layout.rail.monogramTwoLetter}} pt for two letters; `monogramSans` under the SF Pro font, §5.29) in `ink`, and a {{border.accountRing.width}} pt ring in the account ink outside a {{border.accountRing.gap}} pt `spine` gap ({{layout.rail.tile}} pt overall).
- **Badge**: unread count badge (§5.28) at top {{layout.rail.badgeTop}}, trailing {{layout.rail.badgeTrailing}} relative to the tile, with a {{layout.badge.cutout}} pt `spine` cut-out ring. Inbox, All Inboxes and the account tiles read their counts in the same words as the Dock (“{{layout.badge.cap}}+” above {{layout.badge.cap}}, no badge at 0), and the tile's VoiceOver help says the real number (“1,234 unread”).
- **Utilities**: {{layout.rail.utility}} pt icon buttons, `plus` / `gearshape` {{layout.rail.utilityGlyph}} pt. Add opens the add-account menu (§5.44).
- **Margin rule**: {{border.marginRule.width}} × {{layout.rail.marginRuleHeight}} pt at x = 0, centred on the selected account tile or All Inboxes; never on a folder.
- **Capacity.** The account group sits as low as it can and rises as accounts are added, until the All Inboxes tile would come closer than {{layout.rail.groupGap}} pt to All Mail's tile (its top at {{layout.rail.allMailTop}} + {{layout.rail.tile}} + {{layout.rail.groupGap}} = 352). From then on All Inboxes and its hairline stay there and the account tiles scroll inside their own clip view, from {{layout.rail.stackInset}} pt above the first tile (room for the badge overhang) down to {{layout.rail.stackBottomGap}} pt above Add's top. Without scrolling, one account fits at the {{layout.window.minHeight}} pt minimum height, five at the default {{layout.window.defaultHeight}} pt and seven at 900 pt; while the Later tile or the Outbox tile shows All Mail sits a pitch lower, so All Inboxes stops at 396 and each figure is one fewer (none at the minimum height, four at the default, six at 900 pt), and while both show two pitches lower, so All Inboxes stops at 440 and each figure is two fewer (three at the default, five at 900 pt; at the minimum height the account tiles' clip has no room left at all, so the accounts are reached with ⌘1…⌘9 until the window is taller, one fitting from 656 pt). While tiles are clipped a `rule` hairline marks the clipped edge (bottom, and top once scrolled); the scroller shows only while scrolling; ⌘1…⌘9 and ↑/↓ scroll the chosen tile into view instantly. Tiles never overlap the folders or the utilities.

{{TABLE:layout:rail}}

| State | Folder tiles and All Inboxes | Account tile |
|---|---|---|
| Rest | glyph `ink2`, no fill | face + ring + monogram |
| Hover | {{layout.rail.tileHit}} pt `hover` wash, radius `r.xl`; tooltip “Inbox  G I”, “All Inboxes  ⌘0” | same wash (it sits under the ring; `spine + hover` is an asserted ring surface); tooltip “Contoso · alex@contoso.test  ⌘1” |
| Pressed | {{layout.rail.tileHit}} pt `pressed` wash | the hover wash stays and a `pressed` wash darkens only the {{layout.rail.face}} pt face, never the gap or the ring, so every account ink keeps ≥ 3:1 (≥ 3.5:1 in IC) against what is beside it |
| Selected | `raised` fill, `rule` edge, `e1`, glyph `ink`; All Inboxes adds the margin rule `selRule`, a folder never does | margin rule `selRule` |
| Selected, window not key | All Inboxes: margin rule `selRuleIdle`; a folder: unchanged | margin rule `selRuleIdle` |
| Drop target (§5.7 rows over a folder that takes them) | Inbox, Starred, Trash, All Mail (never Later or the Outbox): the {{layout.rail.tileHit}} pt `pressed` wash and glyph `ink`, the look of a click, while the drag is over the hit square; it never takes focus (no ring) and never switches the view (no spring-loading). A folder refusing the drag shows nothing | — (takes no drop) |
| Focused (Tab enters the folders on the selected folder and the accounts on the selected account; ↑/↓ move within each group and choose) | ring outside the tile, {{border.focusRing.gapAroundSheets}} pt gap | ring outside the account ring |
| Unread | count badge (“{{layout.badge.cap}}+” above {{layout.badge.cap}}, none at 0, §5.28): Inbox and All Inboxes only (never Later or the Outbox) | same, the account's Inbox |
| Needs sign-in | — | attention badge (`dangerFill`, `exclamationmark`) replaces the count; tooltip “Sign in required” |
| First sync | — | {{layout.progress.spinner.0}} pt spinner replaces the badge |
| Reconnecting (§5.44) | — | the attention badge **stays**: the account still needs sign-in until the browser finishes, and an abandoned sign-in must not look like progress. The tooltip reads “Finish signing in in your browser…”; the tile gets no spinner (the sync line carries the wait and its Cancel) |
| Signing in (pending tile, §5.44) | — | a placeholder at the end of the stack: {{layout.rail.face}} pt `fill` face with a 1 pt dashed `ruleStrong` ring and a {{layout.rail.pendingSpinner}} pt `ink3` spinner; tooltip “Finish signing in in your browser…”; not selectable |
| Collision monogram | — | two letters at {{layout.rail.monogramTwoLetter}} pt |
| Drag to reorder | — | tile lifts with `e2`; others shift instantly |

Add and Settings: rest `ink2`; hover `hover` wash (radius `r.md`), glyph `ink`; pressed `pressed` wash; Add shows `pressed` while its menu is open; focused ring with a {{border.focusRing.gap}} pt `spine` gap.

### 5.3 List header

One row, {{layout.listHeader.height}} pt: the title-bar zone itself, `paper`, its bottom hairline at y = {{layout.listHeader.height}}. Everything in it is centred on y = {{layout.listHeader.mastheadCenterY}}, the traffic lights' centre line, in this order:

- **Masthead** at x = {{layout.listHeader.mastheadX}} (rail width + {{layout.listHeader.mastheadMinimalGap}} in minimal mode, §5.1): the view's name in `masthead` (New York {{typography.masthead.size}}/{{typography.masthead.lineHeight}}). With the rail showing it is a plain title, not a control: the folders are in the rail, so it has no chevron, no hover, no menu and no Tab stop, VoiceOver reads it as a heading, and a click on it drags the window like the rest of the zone. In minimal mode it is the title menu's button (§5.4): `chevron.down` {{layout.listHeader.chevron}} pt Semibold `ink2` after {{layout.listHeader.chevronGap}} pt, hit area text + chevron + {{layout.listHeader.mastheadHitPad}} pt padding, radius `r.md`. After it, in this fixed order and only when they apply: the minimal-mode new-mail dot ({{layout.listHeader.minimalDotSize}} pt `accent`, {{layout.listHeader.minimalDotGap}} pt after the chevron; shown in minimal mode when another account has new mail), the “Demo” tag ({{layout.listHeader.demoTagGap}} pt after the previous item, §5.43), and, narrowed to one account, “· alex@contoso.test” in `snippet` `ink3` ({{layout.listHeader.addressGap}} pt after the previous item).
- **Search** (§5.6): from {{layout.listHeader.searchGap}} pt after the masthead cluster to {{layout.listHeader.searchComposeGap}} pt before compose.
- **Compose**: {{layout.listHeader.composeSize}} pt icon button, `ss.nib` {{layout.listHeader.composeGlyph}} pt, trailing {{layout.listHeader.composeTrailing}}, top {{layout.listHeader.composeTop}}.

**Who gives way.** The search field takes the free width between the cluster and compose, at rest never starts before the cluster ends (while it has the keyboard it covers the cluster, §5.6), and keeps at least {{layout.listHeader.searchMinWidth}} pt while anything else on the line can make room for it, in this order:

1. **The address** truncates (tail), and drops out whole when less than {{layout.listHeader.addressMinWidth}} pt of it would show (a lone “· a…” names nothing). While it is off the line the placeholder names the account instead (“Search Contoso”, or “Contoso” alone where that would be cut, §5.6), so the narrowed scope is always on screen; in minimal mode nothing else shows it.
2. **The Demo tag** steps off the line whole, and out of the Tab order, when the field beside it would be narrower than its minimum. The window title and the sync line still say Demo, and its menu's commands are in the File menu (§5.43). The address, which gave way first, stays off while the tag is.

The masthead text, the chevron and the dot never give way. When they alone leave less than {{layout.listHeader.searchMinWidth}} pt the field narrows to what is left, drops its keycap before its placeholder and ends the placeholder with an ellipsis. That happens only in minimal mode, where the masthead starts at {{layout.listHeader.mastheadMinimalX}} ({{layout.listHeader.mastheadMinimalX26}} from macOS 26) and carries the chevron: at the default list width for Starred and All Mail (and Drafts from macOS 26) and whenever the dot shows, and at the {{layout.list.minWidth}} pt list minimum in every view, down to 51 pt (All Mail with the dot; 43 pt from macOS 26). Beside the rail the masthead alone always leaves the minimum, so there the field never goes below {{layout.listHeader.searchMinWidth}} pt; on a list at its minimum width the Demo tag is off the line.

Worked numbers, with the rail, in Inbox, at the default list width (the header is 363 pt, the pane less its divider): the field runs from x = 82 to 315 (233 pt); with the Demo tag, which ends at x = 132, from 144; narrowed to alex@contoso.test, the address shows 87 of its 111 pt and the field is 175–315, exactly the minimum. In minimal mode (masthead at {{layout.listHeader.mastheadMinimalX}}), narrowed, with the dot, the address is off the line and the field runs from x = 182 to 315 (133 pt), a point short of the 134 pt “Search Contoso” needs, so it reads “Contoso”; without the dot it is 149 pt and reads “Search Contoso”.

{{TABLE:layout:listHeader}}

States of the masthead in minimal mode: rest; hover (`hover` wash behind text and chevron); pressed / menu open (`pressed`); focused (ring around the hit area); window not key (unchanged). With the rail showing it has none.

### 5.4 Title menu

A system `NSMenu` popped from the masthead in minimal mode only: with the rail showing, the folders and accounts are in the rail and the masthead is a plain title (§5.3). Left-aligned to the masthead text, {{layout.menu.titleMenuOffsetY}} pt below it.

| Item | Symbol | Shortcut column |
|---|---|---|
| Inbox | `{{icon.viewInbox}}` | G I |
| Later, only while something in the scope is snoozed (the rail's rule, §5.2) | `{{icon.viewLater}}` | G L |
| Starred | `{{icon.viewStarred}}` | G S |
| Sent | `{{icon.viewSent}}` | G T |
| Drafts | `{{icon.viewDrafts}}` | G D |
| Outbox, only while a message in the scope is on its way (the rail's rule, §5.2, §5.53) | `{{icon.viewOutbox}}` | G O |
| Trash | `{{icon.viewTrash}}` | G # |
| — separator — | | |
| All Mail | `{{icon.viewAllMail}}` | G A |
| — separator — | | |
| All Inboxes, then each account | monogram | ⌘0, ⌘1…⌘9 |

The current view has a checkmark. Later and the Outbox are listed exactly while their rail tiles show, in the same places, since the menu stands in for the rail. G-sequences are an attributed title with a right tab stop in `ink3` (`keyEquivalent` cannot express a sequence). Highlight and item states are the system's; under Multicolor the highlight is Iron Gall through the accent asset, in every theme (§3.1).

**Unread counts** are system menu-item badges, never custom fills: Inbox carries the unread Inbox count of the current scope (all accounts, or the narrowed one; the rail's Inbox tile shows the same count, §5.2), and All Inboxes and each account item carry theirs. The menu stands in for the rail, so it says what the rail's tiles say (§5.28): a string badge (`NSMenuItemBadge(string:)`) reading “{{layout.badge.cap}}+” above {{layout.badge.cap}}, where a count badge would print the whole number. Numerals only (and the cap's plus), drawn and coloured by the system, so they stay legible on the highlighted row. Zero shows no badge. Later, Starred, Sent, Drafts, the Outbox, Trash and All Mail carry none.

### 5.5 Filter tabs (removed in 1.4)

Removed: the Inbox always shows all of its mail. Focused and Other split one inbox into two places to check, and the header now has a single line for the masthead, search and compose. The providers' categories are still stored (`inbox_section`) to round-trip provider state, and nothing filters or marks rows by them (§5.7). The section number stays so that references to the sections after it keep their meaning.

### 5.6 Search field

| Part | Spec |
|---|---|
| Size | height {{layout.listHeader.searchHeight}}, top {{layout.listHeader.searchTop}} (centred on y = {{layout.listHeader.mastheadCenterY}}); the width fills the header line from the masthead cluster + {{layout.listHeader.searchGap}} to compose − {{layout.listHeader.searchComposeGap}}, at least {{layout.listHeader.searchMinWidth}} pt and never under the masthead cluster (§5.3 says what gives way). While it has the keyboard it widens over the masthead cluster to where the masthead text starts ({{layout.listHeader.mastheadX}}, or the minimal-mode x), gliding `dur.standard` `ease.glide` while the masthead and the Demo tag fade under it and its face covers the new-mail dot and the address (Reduce Motion: the width snaps and the masthead and Demo tag cross-fade in `dur.reducedFade`, as the motion table says); faded, the cluster takes no click, Tab stop or VoiceOver focus; the placeholder then names the account when the view is narrowed to one, as it does when the address is off the line. Focus leaving gives the cluster back the same way. Compose and the field's height never move, so the list does not move |
| Shape | radius {{layout.search.radius}}, `sheet`, {{border.strong.width}} pt `ruleStrong` resting edge |
| Leading | `magnifyingglass` {{layout.search.glyph}} pt `ink3` at x = {{layout.search.glyphX}}; text and placeholder start at x = {{layout.search.textX}} |
| Placeholder | “Search” (`body`, `ink3`); “Search Starred” etc. in other views; “Search Inbox” when narrowed; while the header line has no room for the narrowed account's address (§5.3), the account's name in every view: “Search Contoso” (search looks through all of that account's mail but the Trash, whatever the view), or the name alone (“Contoso”, “Demo Gmail”) in a field too narrow to show that whole, since the magnifier already says search and a cut name may not tell two accounts apart |
| Trailing | {{layout.search.trailingInset}} pt from the trailing edge: keycap “/” ({{layout.kbd.inField}} pt) when empty and not focused, and only while the whole placeholder fits beside it (a field the header line has narrowed drops the keycap before the word, and a placeholder that has given up its “Search”, the bare account name, never gets the keycap in its place); while results show, the clear button ({{layout.search.clearVisual}} pt `fill` disc, `xmark` {{layout.search.clearGlyph}} pt, {{layout.search.clearHit}} pt hit) with “38 results” in `meta` `ink3` {{layout.search.resultsGap}} pt before it; the query truncates before the count does |

| State | Look |
|---|---|
| Rest | as above |
| Hover | face `sheet + hover` |
| Focused | edge kept, focus ring outside a {{border.focusRing.gap}} pt `paper` gap |
| Typing | query `ink`, caret `accent` |
| Searching | a {{layout.progress.spinner.0}} pt spinner replaces the magnifier, only when the first page takes more than {{motion.duration.spinnerDelay}} ms |
| No results | list shows the empty state (§5.9) |
| Query kept, focus elsewhere | keeps its query, count and clear button with no ring, because the list below is still the result set, until the query is cleared |
| Disabled (no accounts) | `fill`, no edge, `inkDisabled` |

**Leaving.** Focus leaving the field returns it to the free width beside the cluster, a kept query and its count included (truncated as above). Esc in the field clears the query; Esc in an empty field returns focus to the list (§6.1). The clear button clears and keeps focus in the field. ↓ gives the keyboard to the list with the query kept; ↩ does too, after asking the mail server for more (below).

**Searching the server.** ↩ in the field asks the mail server for more while the list offers it, once the local results are all listed (§5.10, Edit › Search Gmail for More), and gives the keyboard to the list, so the results can be walked while the server answers; while the field has the keyboard the offer says so with a “↩” keycap: words typed too recently to have reached the list go to it first, and the search runs as soon as their results are in. After a search, and after a failure, ↩ only gives the keyboard to the list, with no keycap, so coming back to the results never searches every account again: Try again is the row's link and the Edit item. ↓ never asks the server. The server is asked the field's text as typed, operators included, so its own search rules decide what it finds (Gmail reads its own operators, Outlook its search words); the operators below that test the row (`is:`, `has:`, `before:`, `after:`, `in:`) and the account scope still narrow its finds, as they narrow the local results. The field itself shows nothing of it: no spinner, no change to its count until the found rows arrive; the list's last row carries the state.

**Operators.** The field takes a few operators besides words, and nothing else changes: no chips, no suggestions, no syntax colouring, no extra empty state. The cheat sheet's search line lists them (§5.32). Operator names and their keywords ignore case, a value may be quoted (`from:"Ben Carter"`), and several terms all apply. Nothing typed is ever an error: an unknown operator (`label:work`) or a value an operator does not take (`before:yesterday`) is searched as words.

| Term | Finds |
|---|---|
| a word | as before: the word in the subject, the people or the text, the last one typed as the start of a word |
| `"a phrase"` | the words side by side, in order; still being typed while the quote is open |
| `from:` | the sender's name or address (`from:ben`, `from:ben@example.com`, `from:"Ben Carter"`) |
| `to:` | any recipient's name or address: To, Cc and Bcc |
| `subject:` | the subject |
| `is:unread`, `is:read`, `is:starred`, `has:attachment` | what the row shows: its unread dot, its star, its paperclip |
| `after:2026-01-31`, `before:2026/01/31` | the row's date (its latest message) on or after that day, or before it, from local midnight |
| `in:inbox`, `in:sent`, `in:drafts`, `in:trash`, `in:all` | the conversation in that view. The first `in:` picks the view searched in place of the one on screen, Later included (Later and the Outbox have no `in:` of their own: a search typed in either looks through All Mail, as from any view): `in:sent` in the Inbox lists sent mail, and `in:trash` reaches the Trash, which search otherwise leaves out. The account scope still applies |
| `-` before any of these | leaves out what it describes: `-from:ben`, `-"weekly metrics"`, `-is:starred` |

Words, phrases, `from:`, `to:` and `subject:` must all hold in one message, and a minus word or address must not be in it, as Gmail and Outlook read the same query; the other operators test the row. While the last thing typed is the start of a value an operator takes (`is:un`, `before:2026-0`) it waits instead of searching for those letters. Opening a result of an `is:unread` search reads it, as anywhere, and the row stays (read) until another conversation is opened, so the selection never jumps to the next result. Keys, menus, drops on the rail's folders and the reader treat results as rows of the view searched: `in:trash` results restore with ⇧I and # leaves them be, as in the Trash (§5.12), and the Trash folder refuses them (§5.2); L snoozes All Mail results wherever the search was typed, the Trash and Drafts included, and beeps for `in:trash` and `in:drafts` ones (§5.45). An action that takes a result out of the list toasts as it would in that view: archiving in an `in:inbox` search, unstarring in an `is:starred` one.

### 5.7 Conversation row

Fixed **{{layout.row.height}} pt** (compact {{layout.rowCompact.height}}) at the Default Text size; the other sizes step the row and its lines (§4.15), and every metric below is Default's. Drawn by hand in `layout()` / `draw(_:)`: no Auto Layout, no shadow, no blur, no per-row animation, no layer per sub-element beyond the text labels and the avatar image.

| Element | Frame (x, y, w, h) | Style |
|---|---|---|
| Margin rule (selected) | 0, {{layout.row.marginRuleY}}, {{border.marginRule.width}}, {{layout.row.marginRuleHeight}} | `selRule` / `selRuleIdle` |
| Unread pip | {{layout.row.pipX}}, {{layout.row.pipY}}, {{layout.row.pip}}, {{layout.row.pip}} (centred on the sender line) | `accent` circle |
| Avatar box | {{layout.row.avatarBoxX}}, {{layout.row.avatarBoxY}}, {{layout.row.avatarBox}}, {{layout.row.avatarBox}}: face {{layout.row.avatarFace}} centred; {{border.accountRing.gap}} pt gap; {{border.accountRing.width}} pt ring | face tint, monogram (New York, or `monogramSans` under the SF Pro font), ring = account ink (unified views only). The gap is **not drawn**: it is the row's own fill showing through, so the same face serves every row state |
| Sender line | {{layout.row.textX}}, {{layout.row.senderY}}, to the time − {{layout.row.timeGap}}, {{layout.row.senderHeight}} | `sender`; Semibold when unread; participants joined by “, ”, the user as “me”; Drafts/Sent prefixes in `ink2`; no sender: “No sender” in the placeholder style (§4.1) |
| Time cluster | trailing {{layout.row.trailing}}, on the sender line | from the trailing edge: time (`meta`, tabular, `ink3`; unread: `metaStrong` in `accent`; format per running head, §4.13) · {{layout.row.tileGap}} pt (`tileGap`) · optional account initial tile ({{layout.row.accountTile}} pt) · {{layout.row.tileGap}} pt · the “Back” tag (§5.28) on an unread Inbox row back from Later · {{layout.row.queuedGap}} pt (`queuedGap`) · `ss.queued` or the failed glyph ({{layout.row.queuedGlyph}} pt). An absent tile or tag takes its leading gap with it: with neither the glyph sits {{layout.row.queuedGap}} pt from the time. Rows carry no category tag: the Focused/Other category is not shown anywhere (§5.5) |
| Subject line | {{layout.row.textX}}, {{layout.row.subjectY}}, to the glyph cluster − {{layout.row.clusterGap}}, {{layout.row.subjectHeight}} | `subject` (New York; `subjectSans` when New York lacks the script, and always under the SF Pro font); Semibold when unread; no subject: “No subject” in the placeholder style (italic `ink3`, regular weight even when unread) |
| Glyph cluster | trailing {{layout.row.trailing}}, centred on the subject line, {{layout.row.glyphGap}} pt gaps, fixed order from the trailing edge: star, muted, attachment, count | `star.fill` {{layout.row.glyph}} pt `star`; `{{icon.mute}}` {{layout.row.glyph}} pt `ink3` (§5.51); `paperclip` {{layout.row.glyph}} pt `ink2`; count badge {{layout.badge.height}} pt, neutral |
| Snippet | {{layout.row.textX}}, {{layout.row.snippetY}}, to trailing {{layout.row.trailing}}, {{layout.row.snippetHeight}} | `snippet`, `ink2` |
| Separator | from x = {{layout.row.separatorX}} to the trailing edge, bottom | hairline `rule`; hidden on the selected row and the row above it |

{{TABLE:layout:row}}

| State | Fill | Marks | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Read | `paper` | — | Regular weights, time `ink3` |
| Unread | `paper` | pip | Semibold sender, subject and time; time in `accent` |
| Hover | `paper + hover` | — | pointer only, instant; never on a selected row; no hover action buttons in rows; suspended while the list scrolls (below) |
| Pressed | — | — | selection applies on mouse-down |
| Selected, window key | `selWash` | margin rule `selRule` | text unchanged; pip, ring, badge, star keep ≥ 3:1 / 4.5:1 |
| Selected, window not key | `selWashIdle` | margin rule `selRuleIdle` | always drawn |
| Multi-selection | each selected row as above | | ⇧-click, ⌘-click, ⇧J / ⇧K; the reader shows §5.19 |
| Keyboard focus with no selection | `paper` | {{border.focusRing.width}} pt ring inset | Full Keyboard Access only |
| Starred | — | filled `star` | VoiceOver “Starred” |
| Has attachment | — | `paperclip` | |
| Muted (§5.51) | — | `{{icon.mute}}` `ink3` beside the star | in every view the conversation is listed in; `ink3`, the time's ink, whose pairs on every row fill are asserted, so it adds no pair; VoiceOver “Muted” |
| Message count ≥ 2 | — | neutral count badge | |
| Queued locally | — | `ss.queued` `ink3` before the time | full contrast, never faded; VoiceOver “Waiting to sync” |
| Operation failed permanently | — | `exclamationmark.triangle.fill` `danger` replaces `ss.queued` | the row is restored locally; toast “Couldn't archive. Restored.”; tooltip and VoiceOver “Couldn't archive”. The triangle stays until the conversation is opened (selected for {{motion.duration.spinnerDelay}} ms, which acknowledges it), a later operation on it succeeds, or 24 hours pass (`failed_op_kind`, §8.5) |
| Account (unified views) | — | ring; optional initial tile | single-account views drop both; face size unchanged |
| Drafts / Sent views | — | “Draft to Ben Carter” / “To Ben Carter” | |
| Back from Later (Inbox) | — | “Back” tag before the time while unread | the row sorts and shows the time it came back (the later of its newest mail and the return), so last week's mail returns under Today; read, it keeps its place and loses the tag; leaving the Inbox or newer mail ends both (`returned_at`, §8.5), and an undo of the E, # or L that took it out puts both back (§5.45). VoiceOver adds “Back from Later” |
| In Later | — | the time slot reads “Back Tue 09:00” (§4.13) | soonest first, no running heads |
| Send Later (Drafts) | — | the time slot reads “Sends Mon 09:00”, “Sending…” or “Not sent” (§5.46) | the row of a message waiting in the outbox, in place of its draft; the time's tooltip and VoiceOver add “while SuperSparrow is open” |
| Outbox (§5.53) | — | “To Ben Carter”; the time slot reads “Sending…”, “Sends Mon 09:00”, “Not sent”, “Retrying shortly” or “Couldn't send”, the last in `danger` | a message on its way, built from the outbox; soonest to go first, no running heads |
| Dragging | — | system drag image | the row itself is unchanged; Inbox, Starred, Trash and All Mail in the rail take it (§5.2) |
| Swiped | the row's own, moving with the fingers | the button it uncovers: the row's full height in `swipeArchive` or `swipeRead`, its icon and label white (`onSwipe`) | Archive on the trailing edge, Mark as Read or Mark as Unread on the leading one; Swipe, below |

**Swipe.** A two-finger sideways swipe on the trackpad slides a conversation row's content under the fingers and uncovers a button on the edge it leaves: moving left, **Archive** on the trailing edge; moving right, **Mark as Read** on a row with unread mail, else **Mark as Unread**, on the leading edge. Let go with the button open and click it, or carry the swipe across the row to do it at once; one button per edge, so a full swipe has one thing to do. The list draws all of it itself, as Apple Mail does (`RowSwipeController`, `QuireSwipeButton`, §8.2), and never uses AppKit's row actions: AppKit drew their title in a colour the app cannot set (black on the near-black Archive fill in a key window) and moved it out of the capsule under an icon.

- **What is a swipe.** A gesture whose first movement is more sideways than up or down, over a conversation row that offers a change on some edge.
