Everything else is the table's and scrolls as before: up and down, sideways over a running head, the list tail or the server row, a mouse's wheel, and a scroll's momentum. A swipe's own momentum is swallowed, so a flick's tail neither scrolls the list nor moves the row, and momentum never starts a swipe. A gesture that begins without moving waits for its first movement, then goes whole to the side it turns out to be. The content goes the way the fingers go, with natural scrolling or without.
- **The key's change on that one row.** Archive is E and Mark as Read or Unread is U, applied to the swiped conversation whatever the selection, through the key's own path: store first and queued, E's toast “Archived” with Undo and Z (none for a read change the row itself shows, as with U), swipes within {{motion.duration.toastCoalesce}} ms coalescing into one toast and one undo (§5.30), the next row taking a removed row's place in the same frame, the selection moving on as after E when the swiped row was the selected one and staying put otherwise, and Undo selecting the conversation again.
- **Offered only where the key would change the row**, so a swipe never uncovers a button that beeps: Archive where the row has Inbox mail, and in Later on every row (E there ends the snooze and leaves the mail archived, §5.45); no Archive in Trash, Sent, Drafts, Starred or All Mail on a row with no Inbox mail, nor on a snoozed conversation a search found. A search's rows follow the view it looks through, as the keys do. A draft kept on this Mac offers neither button, nor does an Outbox row (§5.53); running heads, the list tail and the server row offer nothing. Toward an edge that offers nothing the content does not move.
- **The button.** The whole area the content uncovers, the row's full height, in the action's fill, with the action's icon above its label, both white (`onSwipe`) and centred in it. The icons are the menus' and the toast's own (§4.11): Archive's `{{icon.archive}}`, Mark as Read's `{{icon.markRead}}`, Mark as Unread's `{{icon.markUnread}}`, at {{layout.swipe.glyph}} pt medium; the label is the menu's command (“Archive”, “Mark as Read”, “Mark as Unread”), a verb (§4.13), in `label` at the list's Text size, {{layout.swipe.glyphGap}} pt under the icon. Icon over label fits every row the swipe can uncover, the compact row at Small included, with room above and below. While the content slides, the button is as wide as what it uncovers: the label shows only where it fits whole with {{layout.swipe.padX}} pt either side, fading in over the last {{layout.swipe.padX}} pt, never truncated, and the icon rises from the centre to its place above it as the label comes; narrower, the icon shows alone, centred, fading in the same way; narrower still, the fill alone. Open, the button is the label's width with {{layout.swipe.padX}} pt either side, never under {{layout.swipe.minWidth}} pt. The pointing hand shows over it (§4.17); a click released on it, or VoiceOver's press, makes the change; it takes no keyboard focus. Each fill holds white at 4.5:1 in every theme and mode and under Graphite (the swipe pairs, §7):

| Button | Fill | Icon and label | Study: Light · Dark · Light IC · Dark IC |
|---|---|---|---|
| Archive | `swipeArchive`: the theme's `accentFill`, as on a primary button | `onSwipe` | `{{color.swipeArchive.light}}` · `{{color.swipeArchive.dark}}` · `{{color.swipeArchive.lightHC}}` · `{{color.swipeArchive.darkHC}}` |
| Archive under Graphite, and in Gallery | `swipeArchiveNeutral`, because `ink` turns light at night and white would not hold on it: the `slip` by day, and at night a grey of the ramp light enough to stand out from the row, which the slip sinks into, and still dark enough for white | `onSwipe` | `{{color.swipeArchiveNeutral.light}}` · `{{color.swipeArchiveNeutral.dark}}` · `{{color.swipeArchiveNeutral.lightHC}}` · `{{color.swipeArchiveNeutral.darkHC}}` |
| Mark as Read, Mark as Unread | `swipeRead`: a grey of the ramp in the theme's paper hue, mid by day and low at night, so it is the quiet button beside Archive on every row; unchanged under Graphite | `onSwipe` | `{{color.swipeRead.light}}` · `{{color.swipeRead.dark}}` · `{{color.swipeRead.lightHC}}` · `{{color.swipeRead.darkHC}}` |

The two fills stay at least 12 ΔE00 apart, and 9 under every simulated colour-vision deficiency, in every theme and mode and under Graphite, so the edges never look alike, though only one shows at a time and the word names it. Each fill is held against the row it is uncovered beside, at rest, hovered and in both selections, and Archive stands out from it more than Read does on every one of them, in every theme and mode and under Graphite (the fill-beside-the-row pairs, §7, and the build). White at 4.5:1 keeps a fill below about L\* 50, so at night no fill reaches 3:1 beside a selected row: the pairs carry floors of their own, Archive's above Read's. Both are live tokens, never the system accent: an accent the user picked (Yellow, Pink) does not reach them (§3.1). A button that is out follows every change: it draws from the live tokens, and draws again on an appearance change and on every theme, Graphite and Increase Contrast change.
- **Thresholds** (`layout.swipe`). The content follows the fingers one to one. Let go past {{layout.swipe.fullSwipe}} of the row's width (and never closer than half a button past the open width, so a narrow list still leaves room to open it), the change is made. Short of that, past the whole button, or past {{layout.swipe.snapOpen}} of the open width while still opening, the button stays open; otherwise, and whenever the last movement was back toward rest short of the whole button, the row closes. Past the full-swipe distance the row rubber-bands, slowing toward the row's width and never reaching it ({{layout.swipe.rubberBand}}, the scroll view's constant), so the point past which letting go makes the change can be felt. A gesture the system cancels never makes the change. Swiping the open row again carries on from where it is.

{{TABLE:layout:swipe}}

- **Closing.** One row is open at a time. An open button closes, settling back, on a scroll of the list (trackpad or wheel), another row's swipe, a click anywhere but on it (at once when the click is in the list, which acts on its rows in the same moment), Esc (which then goes no further), a selection change and a refresh of the list (rows may have moved under it). A row the fingers are still moving when a close rule comes closes, and the rest of that gesture does nothing.
- **Motion.** The row follows the fingers, as a scroll does, whatever Reduce Motion says. Let go, it settles open or closed over `dur.standard` `ease.settle` from where it was let go of, and a close rule settles it closed the same way. A full swipe, or a click, that archives slides the content the rest of the way out over `dur.quick` `ease.leave`, the fill covering the row, and the row leaves in the frame the change lands, the next row taking its place as after E (§4.10); if the change leaves it listed (Archive in Starred), it settles back. A read change settles back and the row shows it. Under Reduce Motion nothing settles or slides: the row is at once where it settles. The settle steps once a frame (`QuireFrameAnimation`) and a keystroke finishes it (`Quire.Motion.finishAll()`).
- **VoiceOver.** The row's custom actions are the same buttons, Archive first, offered where the swipe offers them (§4.14); a button that is out is a button named by its words. A mouse has no swipe: E, U, the context menu and the Message menu do the same.

**Compact density** ({{layout.rowCompact.height}} pt): pip at y {{layout.rowCompact.pipY}}; sender line at x {{layout.rowCompact.textX}}, y {{layout.rowCompact.senderY}}; a {{layout.rowCompact.accountSquare}} pt account square (radius {{layout.rowCompact.accountSquareRadius}}, filled with the account ink) before the time, replaced by the {{layout.row.accountTile}} pt initial tile when **Show account initials** is on (never both); line 2 at y {{layout.rowCompact.line2Y}}: subject, then “ - ” and the snippet in `snippet` `ink2`; glyph cluster as above; margin rule y {{layout.rowCompact.marginRuleY}}, height {{layout.rowCompact.marginRuleHeight}}; separator from x {{layout.rowCompact.separatorX}}.

{{TABLE:layout:rowCompact}}

**Performance contract.**

- All attributed strings are built once per (conversation id, version, appearance, contrast, Font setting, Text size, day) in `RowContentCache`, including the script-coverage decision and the running-head time format. Window key state is not part of the key: no text colour depends on it (§3.4).
- **Avatars: faces are cached, rings are drawn.** The face bitmap (tint disc + monogram) renders off the main thread, cached by (initials, tint, size, appearance, contrast, Graphite, Font setting) only. The account ring is stroked in `draw(_:)` with `NSBezierPath` (a circle of radius face/2 + gap + ring/2, width {{border.accountRing.width}}, in the account ink), and the gap is left transparent so the row's fill (`paper`, `paper + hover`, `selWash`, `selWashIdle`) shows through. Hover and selection therefore never miss the cache or flash a wrong-coloured gap. Until a face is ready the row draws the tint disc and initials synchronously from the cached glyph run; it never shows an empty circle.
- **Hover is suspended while scrolling.** On `NSScrollView.willStartLiveScrollNotification` (and for scroll-wheel momentum) the list stops updating hover; rows that slide under a still pointer draw no wash. On `didEndLiveScrollNotification` the row under the pointer, if any, takes the hover wash once.
- Selection changes redraw only the fill and the margin rule inside `CATransaction.setDisableActions(true)`. Row views are opaque and layer-backed.

### 5.8 Running heads (date dividers)

Rows are grouped under local-day heads: Today, Yesterday, Earlier this week, Last week, then month names (“October”), then “October 2025” for earlier years. Each head sets the row time format below it (§4.13): Today and Yesterday rows show the time, Earlier this week the weekday, every later head day and month.

{{TABLE:layout:runningHead}}

- Label at x {{layout.runningHead.labelX}}, y {{layout.runningHead.labelY}}: `runningHead` (SF Pro {{typography.runningHead.size}}/{{typography.runningHead.lineHeight}} Semibold, sentence case, the system's own tracking), `ink3`. A hairline runs from the label end + {{layout.runningHead.ruleGap}} to trailing {{layout.list.inset}} at y {{layout.runningHead.ruleY}}.
- **Hidden** in search results (ranked), in Later (its times are ahead) and in views with fewer than {{layout.runningHead.minConversations}} conversations. The Inbox groups by the date it sorts by, so a conversation back from Later is under Today.
- **Compact density**: {{layout.runningHead.compactHeight}} pt, label at x {{layout.runningHead.compactLabelX}}, y {{layout.runningHead.compactLabelY}}, rule at y {{layout.runningHead.compactRuleY}}.
- **Text size** (§4.15): the head keeps its height and its label's place; the label's line takes `runningHead`'s step and the rule stays on its centre.
- **Not sticky**: plain group rows, no `floatsGroupRows` (no extra compositing during scroll). `heightOfRow` returns two fixed values per density and Text size (row and head), plus the tail footer's height (§5.10). `j`/`k`, next-unread and selection restore skip them; they are not selectable or focusable; VoiceOver reads them as headings.

### 5.9 Empty states

Centred in the pane, max width {{layout.emptyState.maxWidth}}. Line 1 is `aside` (New York Italic {{typography.aside.size}}/{{typography.aside.lineHeight}}) in `ink`; line 2 is `snippet` in `ink3`, {{layout.emptyState.lineGap}} pt below. A line 2 that carries keycaps is a row of commands: it may run past {{layout.emptyState.maxWidth}} pt, up to the pane's width, and when it must wrap it breaks only before a keycap, so a keycap never ends a line apart from the words that follow it.

| Case | Glyph | Line 1 | Line 2 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Inbox zero | `ss.paperPlane` {{layout.emptyState.glyph}} pt in the system's secondary label grey (§4.11), {{layout.emptyState.glyphGap}} pt above | Nothing left to read. | New mail will land here. |
| Empty Starred | — | No starred conversations | Press S to star one. |
| Empty Sent | — | No sent mail | Messages you send appear here. |
| Empty Drafts | — | Nothing in Drafts | Drafts you start here are kept on this Mac. |
| Empty Trash | — | Trash is empty | Conversations you move to Trash appear here. |
| Empty All Mail | — | No mail | Every conversation in this account appears here, archived ones too. |
| Empty Later (only for the moment before the view returns to the Inbox, §5.2) | — | Nothing snoozed | Press L to bring a conversation back later. |
| Empty Outbox (§5.53; only for the moment before the view returns to the Inbox, §5.2, whatever the accounts' sync states, which say nothing of what this Mac is sending) | — | Nothing waiting to send | Messages you send wait here until they have gone. |
| First sync finished with no mail in the view (the older mail listed too, not just the first page) | — | No mail in Contoso yet | Sync finished. New mail will land here. |
| Older mail still being listed, nothing in the view yet (any view but search and offline) | — | the view's line 1, as above | Older mail is still downloading. |
| Search, no results | — | No results for “lisbon flat” | Not on this Mac? **Search Gmail** (a link; “Search Outlook”, or “Search all accounts” in All Inboxes with several accounts, as the row of §5.10 names them), then “Searching Gmail…”, then “Nothing on Gmail either.” or “Couldn't search Gmail ·” **Try again**. Found conversations replace the empty state with their rows, and the row of §5.10 ends them. With no account to search: “Search looks at subjects, people and message text.” |
| Reader, nothing selected | — | No conversation selected | keycaps J K to move · ↩ to open |
| Reader, conversation emptied while open (its last message deleted or moved elsewhere) | — | No messages to show | They were deleted or moved on another device. |
| Offline, nothing cached | `wifi.slash` {{layout.emptyState.offlineGlyph}} pt `ink3`, {{layout.emptyState.offlineGlyphGap}} pt above line 1 (`emptyState.offlineGlyphGap`) | You're offline | Mail will appear when you reconnect. |
| No accounts | — | the welcome screen (§5.39) | |

**A link in line 2.** Only the empty search's line 2 acts: its last words are a link, `snippet` in `accent` (`ink2` while pressed, underlined on hover, and at rest under Graphite, §3.1), with a hit area at least {{layout.button.minHit}} pt tall. The empty state is then a group to VoiceOver with the link as a button inside it; ↩ in the search field does the same, and while the field has the keyboard a “↩” keycap follows the link.

In the conversation-emptied state the reader header keeps the subject (placeholder style if missing), every action but Trash is disabled, and the quick reply is hidden (§5.18); the list removes the row as usual and the selection moves on with the next keystroke.

### 5.10 Skeleton and loading

Only on the first sync of an account, before its first page lands, and only after {{motion.duration.skeletonDelay}} ms. Static: no shimmer.

{{TABLE:layout:skeleton}}

- Skeleton row: {{layout.row.height}} pt; a {{layout.skeleton.circle}} pt circle and four bars (sender, time, subject, snippet: frames above as x, y, w, h), widths varying by ±{{layout.skeleton.widthJitter}} from a fixed table; colour `fill`, radius {{layout.skeleton.barRadius}}.
- At another Text size a skeleton row takes that row's height, its bars centred on the lines they stand for and its circle on the row (§4.15).
- Compact skeleton row: {{layout.rowCompact.height}} pt, no circle; sender bar {{layout.rowCompact.skeletonSenderBar}}, time bar {{layout.rowCompact.skeletonTimeBar}} (trailing {{layout.row.trailing}}), line-2 bar {{layout.rowCompact.skeletonLine2Bar}} (x, y, w, h), same jitter, colour and radius.
- Body not cached yet: the sheet shows its header at once and three bars at {{layout.skeleton.bodyBars}} of the measure, {{layout.skeleton.bodyBarHeight}} pt high, {{layout.skeleton.bodyBarGap}} pt apart. In the reader these are CSS (`.ss-skel { background: var(--q-fill); border-radius: var(--q-r-xs) }`), never a full-pane spinner.
- Skeleton to content is an instant swap, in the list and in a sheet (no cross-fade: nothing in the list animates).

**List tail (paging).** The list loads older pages as the user scrolls within two screens of the end. When the next page is not in hand, a fixed {{layout.listFooter.height}} pt footer row ends the list: after {{motion.duration.spinnerDelay}} ms a {{layout.listFooter.spinner}} pt `ink3` spinner and, {{layout.listFooter.gap}} pt after it, “Loading older mail…” in `meta` `ink3`, centred; before the delay the row is empty `paper`, so fast pages never flash. Failure: `exclamationmark.triangle.fill` `danger` + “Couldn't load older mail” + link “Retry” (`metaStrong`, `accent`). At the true end of a view there is no footer. The footer is not selectable, `j`/`k` skip it, and it is the same height in both densities.

**The end of a search.** A search's results end, once every local result is listed (the tail has nothing left to load), with one row that asks the mail server for more: during a first download the server holds mail this Mac does not have yet, and its own search reads the field's text as typed by its own rules (Gmail's operators, Outlook's search words), which decide what it finds; the operators this Mac tests on the row (`is:`, `has:`, `before:`, `after:`, `in:`, §5.6) and the account scope still narrow the finds. It is the tail's row in the tail's type: {{layout.listFooter.height}} pt, `paper`, the same height in both densities, a centred cluster of a {{layout.listFooter.spinner}} pt glyph and, {{layout.listFooter.gap}} pt after it, the words in `meta`. It is never selectable and `j`/`k` skip it; its link joins the Tab order after the list, and ↩ or Space on the focused link, a click on it, or ↩ in the search field runs the search. The search never blocks the list, never puts a spinner over it, and never raises an alert; the local results stay on screen while it runs.

| State | Glyph | Words |
|---|---|---|
| Offer | `magnifyingglass` `ink3` | “Search Gmail for more” as a link (`metaStrong`, `accent`, underlined on hover; tooltip “Search Gmail for More  ↩ in the search field”), then, while the search field has the keyboard, a “↩” keycap {{layout.listFooter.gap}} pt after it. One account in scope: the provider's name, Gmail or Outlook; All Inboxes with several accounts: “Search all accounts for more” |
| Searching | `magnifyingglass`, replaced by a {{layout.listFooter.spinner}} pt `ink3` spinner after {{motion.duration.spinnerDelay}} ms, as in the search field (§5.6) | “Searching Gmail…” in `ink3`, at once; it stays until the rows the search adds are listed |
| Found | `magnifyingglass` `ink3` | “Found 12 more on Gmail” (“in your accounts” for all accounts) in `ink3`: the conversations the search added to the list. They take their places among the results in date order; the row stays last |
| Nothing | `magnifyingglass` `ink3` | “Nothing more on Gmail” (“in your accounts”) in `ink3` |
| Failed | `exclamationmark.triangle.fill` `danger` | “Couldn't search Gmail” in `danger` + {{layout.listFooter.gap}} pt + link “Try again” (`metaStrong`, `accent`), as the failed tail sets Retry. In All Inboxes the account that failed by name (“Couldn't search Contoso”), “2 accounts”, or “your accounts” when every one failed; the others' finds still list |

New words, or another scope, start again: a search still running is cancelled and the row offers the server for the new words. A finished search is not offered again for the same words. What it found stays on this Mac, so a later search lists it like any mail by its subject, people and preview (by all its text once it has downloaded). VoiceOver reads the row's words (and its link as a button), and hears the result once when the search ends and the list holds all it found, since the row may be off screen. With no local results at all, the empty state offers the same in its own words (§5.9).

### 5.11 Sync status line

{{layout.syncLine.height}} pt at the bottom of the list pane, top hairline, padding {{layout.syncLine.padX}}, gap {{layout.syncLine.gap}}, glyph {{layout.syncLine.glyph}} pt. Text `meta` `ink3`; the lead phrase `metaMedium` `ink2`; trailing actions `metaStrong` `accent`. Always present, so the list never changes height. Clicking it opens Settings › Accounts; hover shows the per-account detail as a tooltip.

| State | Glyph | Copy | Extras |
|---|---|---|---|
| Up to date | `checkmark` `ink3` | **Up to date** · 3 accounts · checked 23:41 | — |
| Up to date, downloading older mail | `checkmark` `ink3` | **Up to date** · Contoso · older mail 3,412 of 8,120 (“older mail 3,412” while the total is unknown, “older mail…” before the first count) | determinate contrail along the top edge while the total is known (never the indeterminate sweep: new mail is current); “checked 23:41” leaves the line for the tooltip's last line (“Checked 23:41”) |
| Up to date, older mail paused | `checkmark` `ink3` | **Up to date** · Contoso · older mail paused until 10:42 | the provider is limiting the older-mail download while it is still being listed; new mail still arrives, so this is never Slow / retrying. No contrail. Once the older mail is listed, a pause of its body downloads is not older mail and never takes the line |
| Downloading | {{layout.progress.spinner.0}} pt spinner | **Downloading mail** · Contoso · 3,412 of 8,120 | determinate contrail along the top edge. Only a new account's first page of newest mail; the older mail after it shows under Up to date |
| Checking | {{layout.progress.spinner.0}} pt spinner | **Checking for mail…** | only for a check the user asked for (View › Check for New Mail ⇧⌘N, or reconnecting), and only once it has run {{motion.duration.spinnerDelay}} ms. The background checks, every 5 s while SuperSparrow is active and every 30 s otherwise, and the checks on app activation, on waking from sleep and when the network returns, never change the line or its tooltip (an account checked in the background reads as Settings shows it, Up to date) except to update “checked 23:41” |
| Sending | {{layout.progress.spinner.0}} pt spinner | **Sending 1 message** | after the undo window, while the send is in flight |
| Signing in (§5.44) | {{layout.progress.spinner.0}} pt spinner | adding: **Finish signing in in your browser** · Gmail (the provider: no account exists yet); reconnecting: **Finish signing in in your browser** · Fabrikam (the account's name) | trailing link “Cancel” |
| Offline | `wifi.slash` `ink2` | **Offline** · changes will sync when you're back | — |
| Slow / retrying | `clock` `warning` | **Gmail is slow to respond** · retrying in 30 s | — |
| Sign-in required | `exclamationmark.triangle.fill` `danger` | **Sign in required** · Fabrikam (line in `danger`) | trailing link “Reconnect” |
| Error | `exclamationmark.triangle.fill` `danger` | **Couldn't sync Contoso** · details | trailing link “Retry” |
| Demo (§5.43) | `info.circle` `ink3` | **Demo** · made-up mail, nothing syncs | — |

**Several accounts: precedence.** The line shows one state. It is chosen from every account's status by the first rule that matches, top to bottom; the tooltip lists every account's own state (“Contoso: Downloading 3,412 of 8,120 · Fabrikam: Sign in required · Gmail: Offline”), the same words Settings › Accounts uses (§5.40).

| # | State | Wins when |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Signing in | a sign-in the user started is waiting for the browser (it answers the user's own action, and its Cancel lives only here in the main window) |
| 2 | Sign-in required | any account needs sign-in |
| 3 | Error | any account failed with a non-network error |
| 4 | Offline | macOS reports no network path, or every account that is not idle failed with a network error |
| 5 | Slow / retrying | some accounts are waiting to retry new mail or sends: a network error while others are online, or the server asked to slow down (rate limited). A limit on the older-mail download only pauses that download, and reads as paused under Up to date (rule 10) |
| 6 | Sending | a send is in flight after its undo window |
| 7 | Downloading | a new account is fetching its first page of newest mail (its first download, before it can check for changes). The older mail downloaded behind it never takes the line (rule 10), nor do body downloads (hydration); Settings shows both |
| 8 | Checking | a check the user asked for has run {{motion.duration.spinnerDelay}} ms |
| 9 | Demo | demo mode (it replaces Up to date) |
| 10 | Up to date | otherwise; while any account downloads older mail, the line says how far it has come in place of “checked 23:41”, or, once every such account is paused, until when |

**Copy for several accounts.** One account in the winning state is named; two or more are counted, never listed: “**Sign in required** · Fabrikam” / “**Sign in required** · 2 accounts”; “**Couldn't sync Contoso** · details” / “**Couldn't sync 2 accounts**”; “**Gmail is slow to respond** · retrying in 30 s” / “**2 accounts are slow to respond** · retrying in 30 s” (the soonest retry); “**Downloading mail** · Contoso · 3,412 of 8,120” / “**Downloading mail** · 2 accounts · 3,412 of 8,120” (summed over the downloading accounts, “3,412 messages” while a total is unknown); “**Up to date** · Contoso · older mail 3,412 of 8,120” / “**Up to date** · 3 accounts · older mail 3,412 of 8,120” (named as Up to date names the accounts shown; the figures summed over the accounts downloading older mail, a paused one included so the figure does not jump when it pauses, and “older mail 3,412” while a total is unknown), and “**Up to date** · 3 accounts · older mail paused until 10:42” once every one of them is paused (the soonest resume). The trailing action follows the count: with one account “Reconnect” and “Retry” act on it; with two or more the link reads “Reconnect…” and opens Settings › Accounts, where each row has its own Reconnect, and “Retry” retries every failed account. Numbers use the locale's grouping (§4.13).

**Keyboard.** The line is a button (Space or ↩ opens Settings › Accounts) with the focus ring inset {{border.focusRing.width}} pt inside its bounds. While it carries an action (Reconnect, Retry, Cancel) it joins the Tab order after the list, always, because no other surface offers that action in place; Tab lands on the action link (ring around the link, radius `r.sm`) and ↩ runs it. Without an action it is reachable only with Full Keyboard Access. VoiceOver reads the whole line, then the action.

### 5.12 Reader header and action buttons

A **native** view (it replaces `ConversationHeaderView`), {{layout.readerHeader.height}} pt, `paper`, bottom hairline at the horizon, always visible. The subject updates in the same frame as the selection, before the web view renders, so key repeat feels instant.

- **Row 1** (title-bar zone): the kicker at the reading column's leading edge, centred on y = {{layout.readerHeader.kickerCenterY}}: a {{layout.readerHeader.kickerDot}} pt account-ink dot, {{layout.readerHeader.kickerDotGap}} pt, the account name, “·”, the message count, in `runningHead` `ink3` (single-account setups omit the account; several selected shows “Selection”). The kicker ends at least {{layout.readerHeader.kickerActionGap}} pt before the first visible action (`readerHeader.kickerActionGap`); when it does not fit, the account name tail-truncates and the dot, the “·” and the count never do; if fewer than {{layout.readerHeader.kickerNameMinChars}} characters of the name would remain, the name is dropped (dot and count stay, and the kicker's tooltip names the account). Trailing at {{layout.readerHeader.actionsTrailing}}, top {{layout.readerHeader.actionsTop}}: Star · Archive · Trash, a {{layout.readerHeader.actionGroupGap}} pt gap, Reply · Reply All · Forward, as {{layout.readerHeader.actionSize}} pt icon buttons with {{layout.readerHeader.actionGlyph}} pt symbols, {{layout.readerHeader.actionGap}} pt apart (190 pt for all six); then, in the main window, another {{layout.readerHeader.actionGroupGap}} pt gap and the **Day** and **Week** buttons, the same icon buttons {{layout.readerHeader.actionGap}} pt apart (262 pt for all eight; §5.54). The set changes per view and selection (below); Day and Week end every set. At the {{layout.window.readerMinWidth}} pt minimum reader the kicker has 400 − 16 − 262 − 16 − 16 = 90 pt: the dot and “· 12 messages”, the account name dropped (the kicker's tooltip names it). In a reader window, which has no Day or Week button (§5.49), it keeps 400 − 16 − 190 − 16 − 16 = 162 pt: the dot, “· 12 messages” and the first letters of the account name.
- **Row 2**: the headline at the reading column, top {{layout.readerHeader.headlineTop}}: `headline` (New York {{typography.headline.size}}/{{typography.headline.lineHeight}} Semibold, `headlineSans` when New York lacks the script), at the reader's Text size (§4.15: at Larger 26/32, still inside the band), one line, tail truncation, full subject in the tooltip and VoiceOver label, selectable. No subject: “No subject” in the placeholder style (`headline` italic, `ink3`).
- The reading column x = max(margin, (reader width − {{layout.reader.columnMax}}) / 2), where margin is {{layout.reader.columnMinMargin}}, or {{layout.reader.narrowColumnMargin}} when the reader is narrower than {{layout.window.readerNarrow}} pt; recomputed in `layout()`; the kicker, headline, sheets and quick-reply field share it. In a reader window the kicker starts no nearer the leading edge than the minimal-mode masthead, so it clears the lights (§5.49).
- **Per view and selection.** Each set is drawn from the trailing edge; hidden actions close up, they never leave gaps.

  | Reader state | Actions | Notes |
  |---|---|---|
  | One conversation in Inbox, Starred, Sent, All Mail | Star · Archive · Trash, gap, Reply · Reply All · Forward | — |
  | One conversation in Later | Star · Archive · Trash, gap, Reply · Reply All · Forward | Archive ends the snooze and leaves the conversation archived, as E does there (§5.45) |
  | One conversation in Trash | Star · Move to Inbox, gap, Reply · Reply All · Forward | Archive becomes Move to Inbox (`tray.and.arrow.down`, ⇧I); **Trash is removed**: the conversation is already there and v1 has no Delete Forever (§9). # does nothing in Trash and Message › Move to Trash is disabled |
  | Several selected (any view but Drafts, Trash and the Outbox) | Star · Archive · Trash | the reply group is **hidden**: a reply answers one conversation, so R, A and F do nothing. Archive and Trash act on every selected conversation (toast “Archived 3 conversations”). Star shows the selection's state: on (`star.fill` in `star`, tooltip “Unstar All  S”) only when **every** selected conversation is starred, and then it unstars all; otherwise, none or mixed, it is off (outline `star`, tooltip “Star All  S”) and it stars all. S does the same |
  | Several selected in Trash | Star · Move to Inbox | as above, with Move to Inbox for all |
  | Drafts (one draft) | Continue Editing (`ss.nib`, ↩) · Discard Draft (`trash`, #) | Star, Archive and the reply group are hidden; Discard confirms with the “Discard this draft?” alert; the kicker reads “Draft · Contoso” (§5.19). Several drafts selected: Discard Draft only, which asks “Discard 3 drafts?” (§5.33) |
  | Outbox (one message, §5.53) | Continue Editing (`ss.nib`, ↩) while it is a Send Later message still held (waiting, missed, or waiting to go again); nothing otherwise | no mail action applies to a message on its way; the kicker reads “Outbox · Contoso”. Several selected: nothing, and the headline reads “3 messages selected” |
  | Nothing selected | the full set, disabled | — |
  | Conversation emptied while open | the view's set, every action but Trash disabled | in Trash, every action disabled |

- **Day and Week** (main window only, after every set above; §5.54). Day: `{{icon.dayColumn}}`, Week: `{{icon.week}}`, quiet toggles (`isOn` in the quiet style: `accentWash` with the glyph in `accent`, hover `accentWashHover`, pressed `accentWashPressed`, §5.15) that act on the window rather than the conversation, so they stay enabled whatever is selected, nothing and a draft included. Day is on while the day column shows, in full or opened over the reader's edge, and off while it is hidden or folded to its strip; a click does what D does. Week is on while the week shows (both then end the week's own header, which takes the reader's place); a click does what W does. Tooltips “Hide Day  D” / “Show Day  D” and “Show Week  W” / “Hide Week  W”; VoiceOver “Day, toggle button” and “Week, toggle button” with their pressed state. Reader windows have neither (§5.49).

{{TABLE:layout:readerHeader}}

| State | Glyph | Fill |
|---|---|---|
| Rest | `ink2` | none |
| Hover | `ink` | `hover`, radius `r.md`; tooltip “Archive  E” |
| Pressed | `ink` | `pressed` |
| Focused | `ink2` | ring |
| On (starred) | `star.fill` in `star` | none |
| Disabled (Reply All with one participant; nothing selected; every action but Trash when the conversation has no messages left) | `inkDisabled` | none |
| Loading | {{layout.progress.spinner.0}} pt spinner replaces the glyph | none |
| Window not key | unchanged | unchanged |

With nothing selected the header keeps its height, shows no kicker or headline, and its actions are disabled.

### 5.13 Message stack (collapsed messages)

Below the header the reader shell renders the participants line (`body`, `ink2`, {{layout.reader.participantsTop}} pt from the top, up to {{layout.reader.participantsMax}} names then “+3”), then the stack, {{layout.reader.participantsGap}} pt below.

All collapsed messages between expanded ones form one stack: one `sheet` with a hairline `rule` edge, radius {{layout.reader.sheetRadius}}, no shadow; collapsed rows are {{layout.reader.stackRow}} pt and separated by hairlines.

| Part | Spec |
|---|---|
| Row | padding 0 {{layout.reader.stackRowPadX}}, gap {{layout.reader.stackRowGap}} |
| Avatar | {{layout.reader.stackAvatar}} pt monogram, no ring |
| Name | `senderMedium`, `ink`, fixed {{layout.reader.stackNameColumn}} pt column; Semibold when unread; “No sender” in the placeholder style |
| Snippet | `sender`, `ink2`, fills, tail-truncated |
| Starred | `star.fill` {{layout.reader.starGlyph}} pt in `star`, {{layout.reader.starGap}} pt before the date, when that message is starred (Gmail stars messages; for Microsoft the flag is on the message too) |
| Attachments | `paperclip` {{layout.reader.starGlyph}} pt in `ink2`, as the list row draws it, left of the star (or {{layout.reader.starGap}} pt before the date when not starred), when that message has attachments: until its body downloads, the provider's flag the list row uses; after, the attachments its sheet shows, so inline pictures never earn it. VoiceOver adds “has attachments” to the row |
| Date | `meta` tabular `ink3`, trailing |
| Unread pip | {{layout.reader.stackPip}} pt `accent` circle at x = {{layout.reader.stackPipX}} (centred in the {{layout.reader.stackRowPadX}} pt leading padding), vertically centred on the row (y = 19 in the {{layout.reader.stackRow}} pt row) |
| Fold | more than {{layout.reader.foldAbove}} collapsed messages keep the first {{layout.reader.foldKeepStart}} and last {{layout.reader.foldKeepEnd}} and fold the rest into one row “4 more messages” (`label`, `ink2`, centred) |

States: rest; hover (`hover` across the row, end rows clipped to the radius); pressed; focused (`n`/`p` or Tab: a {{border.focusRing.width}} pt `focus` ring inset {{border.focusRing.gap}} pt inside the row); unread (pip + Semibold); starred (the mark above). Click or ↩ expands the message in place, by the provisional-height rule of §4.10 (`dur.standard`, `ease.settle`; Reduce Motion: instant).

**Fold row** (“4 more messages”): a {{layout.reader.stackRow}} pt stack row with the same states: hover (`hover`, label `ink`), pressed (`pressed`), focused (the inset ring; `n`/`p` land on it like a message). Click, ↩ or Space unfolds it into its collapsed rows (not into expanded messages), with the stack's height change (`dur.standard`, `ease.settle`; Reduce Motion: instant); focus moves to the first unfolded row. It does not fold again until the conversation is reopened. VoiceOver: “4 more messages, button”.

### 5.14 Message sheet (expanded) and message header

{{TABLE:layout:reader}}

| Part | Spec |
|---|---|
| Sheet | `sheet`, radius {{layout.reader.sheetRadius}}, hairline `rule` edge + `e1`; padding {{layout.reader.sheetPadTop}} {{layout.reader.sheetPadX}} {{layout.reader.sheetPadBottom}}; {{layout.reader.stackGap}} pt below the stack or the previous sheet |
| Header | avatar {{layout.reader.headerAvatar}} (no ring) · {{layout.reader.headerGap}} gap · who block · date |
| Name line | `bodyStrong`, `ink` (“No sender” in the placeholder style); a starred message shows `star.fill` {{layout.reader.starGlyph}} pt in `star` {{layout.reader.starGap}} pt after the name, at rest; a message that offers a way to unsubscribe shows the **Unsubscribe** link next ({{layout.reader.addressGap}} pt gap; see “Unsubscribe” below); the address follows on hover or focus (`meta`, `ink3`, {{layout.reader.addressGap}} pt gap) and copies with a click (“Copying an address”, below); a sender with no name apart from the address shows the address as the name, and the name is then what copies. Beside Unsubscribe, whose request no undo takes back, a copy target keeps {{layout.reader.unsubscribeHitGap}} pt of nothing between the two hits (`reader.unsubscribeHitGap`), so the text gap is 12 pt: a press that misses the address, which fades in under the pointer, lands on neither; unread shows a {{layout.reader.headerPip}} pt `accent` pip before the name. A right-click on the name, the address or the avatar opens the person menu (below) |
| Recipients | the toggle, on the line under the name. Folded, the summary “to me · cc Tom Hughes” (`snippet` `ink2`, tooltip “Show Addresses”); open, “Hide addresses” in the header's link style (`snippet` `ink2`, underlined {{layout.textStyle.linkUnderline}} pt at offset {{layout.textStyle.linkUnderlineOffset}}, as “and 24 more”; tooltip “Hide Addresses”). Click, ↩ or Space opens and folds. It keeps its line in both states, and open, its hit keeps the folded summary's width (the words are shorter, the ring hugs them), so the place clicked to open the list is the place that folds it, wherever along the summary that was. Hover and press `ink`; focus: the ring outside a 1 pt `sheet` gap; hit: its line and the {{layout.reader.recipientsToggleHitBelow}} pt below it (`reader.recipientsToggleHitBelow`), {{layout.button.minHit}} pt. VoiceOver: “Recipients, to me, cc Tom Hughes, collapsed, button” / “Hide addresses, expanded, button” |
| Recipients, open | {{layout.reader.recipientsTop}} pt below the toggle (`reader.recipientsTop`), inside the who block: one row per field in the order To, Cc, Bcc (Bcc only on messages the user sent), all on one {{layout.reader.recipientPitch}} pt pitch (`reader.recipientPitch`, `recipientsFieldGap` {{layout.reader.recipientsFieldGap}}). Each row starts with its label, “To”, “Cc” or “Bcc” (`snippet` `ink3`, level with its first line), in a {{layout.reader.recipientsLabelColumn}} pt column (`reader.recipientsLabelColumn`, to the recipients' text), then the recipients wrapped in the remaining width, each one a control that copies its address (“Copying an address”, below): “Ben Carter <ben@contoso.test>,” with the name in `snippet` `ink2` and the address in `snippet` `ink3`, a comma ending each but the field's last, the user as “me <alex@contoso.test>”, a recipient with no name as its address alone. Between two recipients, the {{layout.reader.recipientPadX}} pt padding of each one's face. A recipient never breaks across lines (a single over-long one tail-truncates, full text in its tooltip); one with no address (an empty group) is plain text. Past {{layout.reader.recipientsMaxLines}} lines (`reader.recipientsMaxLines`) the list stops at “and 24 more” (`snippet` `ink2`, underlined, a button that shows the rest; pressed from the keyboard, focus moves to the first recipient it shows, and clicked, it stays where a click on any button leaves it, with Tab reaching that recipient next). The labels, commas and the space around the recipients take no click (a click there only makes the message the target) and show the arrow; nothing in the list folds it. The open state lasts the session, per message. Opening and folding follow the expand rule of §4.10 (`dur.standard`, `ease.settle`; Reduce Motion: instant); the list is shell DOM, measured at once, so there is no provisional height. VoiceOver: the list is a group “Recipients”, each field a group “To”, “Cc”, “Bcc”. All inks are tested pairs on `sheet`, `sheet + hover` and `sheet + pressed` (§7) |
| Date | `snippet`, tabular, `ink3`, trailing: “Today 09:12”, “Yesterday 23:39”, “Fri 30 Oct” |
| Attachments | the stack row's `paperclip` (§5.13), {{layout.reader.starGlyph}} pt `ink2`, 4 pt before the date, after the hover actions; always shown, not only on hover |
| Hover actions | on hover or focus only (fade in `dur.quick`), before the date: {{layout.reader.headerAction}} pt icon buttons ({{layout.reader.headerActionGlyph}} pt symbols; {{layout.button.minHit}} pt hit), {{layout.reader.headerActionGap}} pt apart, the last one {{layout.reader.headerActionDateGap}} pt before the date: Star (`star` / `star.fill` in `star` when on), Reply, More; plus Original colors in dark mode for styled HTML mail that declares its own colours (off: `circle.lefthalf.filled` `ink2`, tooltip “Original Colors  ⇧⌘O”; on: the same glyph in `accent` on `accentWash`, hover `accentWashHover`, pressed `accentWashPressed`, tooltip “Adaptive Colors  ⇧⌘O”; the on state stays visible without hover) |
| More (`ellipsis`) | a system menu acting on **this message**; conversation actions stay in the header (Star Message, like the hover Star, and Mark as Unread from Here are mail actions as S and U are: the toast S or U would give in that view, so none where the row shows the change, and Z and ⌘Z undo them, §6.1): Reply · Reply All · Forward · — · Star Message / Unstar Message · Mark as Unread from Here · — · Load Images (when images are blocked) · Original Colors / Adaptive Colors (dark mode, styled, declares colours) · — · Unsubscribe ⇧⌘U (when the link shows; it acts on this message) · Always Archive Mail from This Sender · Block Sender (when someone other than the user sent it, §5.52; the rule the sender has is checked and disabled) · Copy Sender Address. Items that do not apply are hidden, not disabled |
| Invitation card | when the message carries a calendar invitation: {{layout.reader.bodyTop}} below the header, at the indent, inside the measure; {{layout.invitation.gapBelow}} below it, where the body would start, the message's elision mark, the body folded behind it (“The invitation card” and “The message under the card”, below) |
| Body | {{layout.reader.bodyTop}} below the header; under an invitation card, once unfolded, {{layout.reader.blockGap}} below the message's elision mark. Plain and unstyled: `reading` (or `readingSans`), indented {{layout.reader.bodyIndent}} to the name column, measure {{layout.reader.measure}} (narrower when the sheet is), paragraph gap {{layout.reader.blockGap}}. A text/plain body is stored as paragraphs: blank lines end a `<p>` and a run of them is one break, lines inside keep their `<br>`, so the gap spaces paragraphs instead of an empty line (bodies stored before 1.3.1 keep `<br><br>` until fetched again). Styled HTML: the sheet's full content width ({{layout.reader.htmlWidth}} at the widest column), fitted as below |
| Quoted-text toggle | {{layout.reader.blockGap}} below the body, at the indent (§5.15); under an invitation card it folds with the body |
| Attachments | {{layout.reader.attachmentsTop}} below, chips at the indent, wrapping with {{layout.attachmentChip.chipGap}} pt gaps (§5.17) |
| Footer | {{layout.reader.footerTop}} below, hairline above, {{layout.reader.footerPad}} pt padding; ghost buttons Reply · Reply all · Forward (sentence case, §4.13) with their symbols, at the indent, **regular** size ({{layout.button.regular.height}} pt, `bodyMedium`, {{layout.button.regular.glyph}} pt glyphs; `layout.reader.footerButtonSize`), {{layout.reader.footerButtonGap}} pt apart; on the target message each button is followed by its keycap (R, A, F), {{layout.reader.targetKeycapGap}} pt after the button and {{layout.reader.footerKeycapNextGap}} pt before the next one |

**Copying an address.** Each recipient in the open list, and the sender's address on the name line, copies with a click: the address alone (`ben@contoso.test`), as plain text, since that is what any field anywhere takes. ↩ or Space with keyboard focus copies too (the app's Open and Quick Look give way, as on the elision mark, §5.15), and so do ⌘C and Edit › Copy, whatever else the page has selected. A double-click copies once. The click also makes the message the target. A target is one control, name and address together: on hover a {{layout.reader.recipientFace}} pt face (`reader.recipientFace`, centred in the {{layout.reader.recipientPitch}} pt pitch, so the hit is the whole pitch), radius `r.sm` ({{layout.reader.recipientRadius}}, `reader.recipientRadius`), {{layout.reader.recipientPadX}} pt padding each side (`reader.recipientPadX`; at rest the text sits where it would without it); the sender's address has the name line's 18 pt face and a {{layout.button.minHit}} pt hit that reaches {{layout.reader.senderAddressHitAbove}} pt up into the sheet's padding (`reader.senderAddressHitAbove`), never down onto the toggle. Targets are not selectable text; the sender's name is, and is not a target. When the name line leaves the sender's address less than {{layout.button.minHit}} pt (Unsubscribe and a long name in a narrow reader), the address hides and is no target; the person menu and More › Copy Sender Address still copy it. Tooltip “Copy Address” (a truncated recipient keeps its full text instead).

| State | Face | Text |
|---|---|---|
| Rest | none | name `ink2`, address `ink3` (the sender's: `meta` `ink3`, shown on the sheet's hover or focus) |
| Hover | `hover` | name and address `ink` |
| Pressed | `pressed` | `ink` |
| Focused | the ring outside a 1 pt `sheet` gap, around the face | unchanged |
| Copied | as the pointer gives it | what was copied gives way to `checkmark` {{layout.reader.copiedGlyph}} pt · {{layout.reader.copiedGap}} pt · “Copied” in its own role, in `ink`, leading-aligned in the width it had (the checkmark alone where the mark is wider). Where a long name cuts the address short or out of sight, the mark stands over the whole recipient as far as it shows, and the face's ellipsis goes while it does; {{motion.duration.copiedConfirm}} ms (`dur.copiedConfirm`), then the text cross-fades back in `dur.quick` |
| Its menu open | `hover` until the menu closes | `ink` |
| Window not key | unchanged, no ring | unchanged |

The mark stands in for exactly what the pasteboard took: after Copy Address, the address with its brackets and comma (or the whole of a recipient with no name); after Copy Name and Address, the whole recipient. It shows only once the app has written the pasteboard. There is one at a time across the page, since the pasteboard holds one thing: a new copy ends the last mark, a copy of the same recipient restarts it, and a re-render of the header keeps it for its remaining time. A copy gets no toast and no sound: nothing leaves the view and there is nothing to undo. VoiceOver says “Copied ben@contoso.test.” (“Copied Ben Carter, ben@contoso.test.”). More › Copy Sender Address copies the same way, mark and announcement included.

**Keyboard in the open list.** The list is one Tab stop: Tab lands on the recipient last reached or clicked while it has been open, else the first. A click never gives a target keyboard focus: it leaves focus on the sheet, as a click on any of the reader's buttons does, since focus the pointer gave would show no ring and still take ↩, Space, ←, Esc and ⌘C from the app; a target the keyboard reached keeps its focus through a click. → and ← move along the recipients in reading order, To through Bcc, then onto “and 24 more”, and stop at either end; the app leaves ← to the page there, as on the attachment row (§5.17). ↑ and ↓ scroll the reader. Esc folds the list and moves focus to the toggle (§6.1). A re-render of the header keeps focus on the same recipient, or on the toggle when it has gone. VoiceOver names each recipient by what it shows, without the brackets: “Ben Carter, ben@contoso.test, button”, “me, alex@contoso.test, button”, and the sender's address “ben@contoso.test, button”; its help is the tooltip.

**The person menu.** A right-click (or VO-⇧-M) on a recipient, or on the sender's name, address or avatar, opens a system menu for that person: Copy Address · Copy Name and Address (“Ben Carter <ben@contoso.test>”, written as the composer's tokens are, the user's own name for “me”; only when the header gives a name) · — · New Message (a composer to that address from the conversation's account; only for a valid address) · Show Mail from This Person (the main window's search field takes `from:ben@contoso.test` and runs it as if typed, and the list takes the keyboard with the query kept, §5.6; from a reader window the main window comes forward). Items that do not apply are hidden. Labels, commas and “and 24 more” keep the conversation's menu.

**The copy is the app's.** The shell names the recipient by field and position (`copyAddress`, `cc:2`), never by its address; the app reads the address from the store, writes it to the general pasteboard as plain text, then shows the mark and announces it. The page writes no clipboard, and no address is logged above debug.

**The target message.** R, A, F and Space act on one message: the last expanded message when a conversation opens; the message last clicked anywhere inside its sheet; or the one last reached with `n`/`p` or Tab. It is always shown, without the keyboard ring: its footer carries the R · A · F keycaps, and when more than one message is expanded its sheet edge is {{border.strong.width}} pt `ruleStrong` instead of the `rule` hairline. When the reader is narrower than {{layout.window.readerKeycapsMin}} pt and the keycaps hide, the edge is drawn even with one message expanded. The keyboard ring (below) appears only after `n`/`p` or Tab and always sits on the target.

**Unsubscribe.** A message whose `List-Unsubscribe` header (RFC 2369) offers a mailto, https or http address shows “Unsubscribe” after the sender's name and star, before the address. It is drawn as a link, never as a button (VoiceOver still reads it as one): `snippet` in `ink2`, the recipients line's type and colour, underlined at rest ({{layout.textStyle.linkUnderline}} pt at offset {{layout.textStyle.linkUnderlineOffset}}) like the header's other link (“and 24 more”), since nothing else tells it from the name line's text; `ink` on hover and press; the Quire ring on focus ({{border.focusRing.width}} pt `focus` outside a 1 pt `sheet` gap). It never truncates: its room comes first from the address, which shows nothing at rest and gives up its share before the name gives any (the line still does not reflow on hover), then from the name. Tooltip “Unsubscribe  ⇧⌘U”; VoiceOver “Unsubscribe, button”; Space or ↩ acts on it like a click. It shows on every expanded sheet whose message offers it (a thread of newsletter issues can show it on each), never on a draft or a reply still in the outbox. Mail stored before 1.6.3 kept no list headers and shows none until it is fetched again. One click acts, with no alert, in this order of preference: an RFC 8058 one-click request when `List-Unsubscribe-Post` is exactly `List-Unsubscribe=One-Click` beside an https address (the app posts it from this Mac, with no cookies or credentials); else the mailto request, sent silently from the conversation's account to the link's one address, never to its Cc or Bcc (it appears in Sent; a link naming several recipients is not used); else the sender's page, opened in the browser. Then the conversation is archived as E would and the toast says both (§5.30): “Unsubscribe sent · Archived”, or “Opened in browser · Archived”. A snoozed conversation (in Later, or a search's result, §5.45) is archived already: its snooze ends instead and it stays archived, as E leaves it in Later, so the list's mail does not come back to the Inbox at its time; the toast reads the same. Asked again while that toast is up (a double-click, whose second click is ignored even where the next conversation's link has moved under the pointer; ⇧⌘U again where the archived row stays listed), the same request is not sent twice and the toast keeps its Undo. Undo (the toast, Z, ⌘Z) brings the conversation back (a snoozed one to Later, at its time as before); the request stays sent, which is why the archive is the toast's last word and why the undo confirms itself with “Undid Archive · Unsubscribe stays sent”. Message › Unsubscribe (⇧⌘U), enabled while one conversation is open and a message of it offers it, acts on that conversation's latest such message, as does the context menu's item (§5.35); the More menu's item acts on its own message.

**Tab order inside an expanded sheet.** Tab enters a sheet on the sheet itself (it takes the ring and becomes the target), then moves through, in this order: the Unsubscribe link, when it shows → the sender's address, when it shows apart from the name → the recipients toggle → the open list of recipients, one stop (“Copying an address”) → the header hover actions in visual order (Star, Reply, More, and Original colors when present; they show while anything in the sheet has focus) → the invitation card's controls in visual order (its links, Accept, Tentative, Decline, Open in Calendar, the notice's Reconnect) → the message's elision mark under the card (while the message is folded, its body's links and quoted-text toggle are no stops and come back with it; its banners stay) → banner actions in document order (Load images; the notice's Original colors / Adaptive colors, then its dismiss) → links in the body, in document order, **only with Full Keyboard Access** (the web view's `tabFocusesLinks` follows that setting; otherwise ⌥Tab reaches them, as in Safari) → the quoted-text toggle → the attachment chips, as one stop (← → move between chips, Space opens Quick Look on the focused one) → the footer buttons Reply, Reply all, Forward → the next stack row or sheet, and after the last message the quick reply. ⇧Tab reverses. Every stop draws the Quire ring; links in a body draw it through the injected `a:focus-visible` rule (§8.4: {{border.focusRing.width}} pt `focus`, {{border.focusRing.widthHC}} pt in IC, outside a 1 pt gap in the sheet colour), never WebKit's default ring, whose alpha falls below 3:1. In Original colors the link ring takes the light-mode `focus` value and an `originalPage` gap, because the page under it is white.

**The invitation card.** A message that carries a calendar invitation (an iCalendar REQUEST or CANCEL, read when its body was fetched, never when it opens: §8.5) opens with a card above its body. It answers the invitation and hands it to Calendar; it never becomes a calendar: no other events and no reminders. It does say what the invitation overlaps (the overlap line, below), from the events the day column reads (§5.54), so the answer is given knowing it.

{{TABLE:layout:invitation}}

| Part | Spec |
|---|---|
| Card | `sheet` with an inset hairline `rule` edge (`ruleStrong` in IC), radius `r.lg` ({{layout.invitation.radius}}), padding {{layout.invitation.padTop}} {{layout.invitation.padX}} {{layout.invitation.padBottom}}; at the body indent ({{layout.reader.bodyIndent}}), at most the measure ({{layout.reader.measure}}) wide, {{layout.reader.bodyTop}} below the header; the message's elision mark {{layout.invitation.gapBelow}} below, where the body (and its banners) would start, the body folded behind it (below). No shadow: it is content on the sheet, like a chip |
| Kicker | `calendar` {{layout.invitation.kickerGlyph}} pt, {{layout.invitation.kickerGap}} pt, “Invitation” in `runningHead` `ink3`, as the reader's kicker. Cancelled: `exclamationmark.triangle.fill` and “Cancelled”, both `danger` |
| Title | {{layout.invitation.titleTop}} pt below: `title` `ink`, wrapping (never truncated). No title: “No title” in the placeholder style (italic, `ink3`). Cancelled: `ink2` with a 1 pt line through it (the shape that says cancelled beside the word) |
| When | {{layout.invitation.whenTop}} pt below: `body` `ink`, in this Mac's zone: “Tue 11 Mar · 15:00 - 16:00”; “Tue 11 Mar · All day”; “Wed 12 Mar - Fri 14 Mar · All day”; “Tue 11 Mar, 22:00 - Wed 12 Mar, 02:00”; a date outside this year carries its year as the locale writes it. Times follow the system 12/24-hour setting (§4.13). All-day events and times written without a zone read the same everywhere and are never shifted |
| Elsewhere | when the event was written in a zone at another offset from this Mac's at its start: the same line in that zone and its name, `snippet` `ink3`: “Tue 11 Mar · 11:00 - 12:00 Eastern Time” |
| Repeat | {{layout.invitation.whenTop}} pt below, `snippet` `ink2`: the rule in words, never its occurrences: “Every week on Tuesday”, “Every 2 weeks on Monday and Thursday”, “Every month on the 3rd, until 3 March 2027”, “Every month on the last Friday, 10 times”, “Every year on the second Tuesday of March”; a rule whose parts cannot be said reads “Repeats” |
| Details | {{layout.invitation.detailsTop}} pt below: rows {{layout.invitation.rowGap}} pt apart, each a label in a {{layout.invitation.labelColumn}} pt column (`snippet` `ink3`) and its value (`snippet` `ink2`, wrapping): **Where** the location (a web address is a link, `accent`, underlined on hover and press as a link button, §5.24, and always under Graphite; it keeps to one line, cut with an ellipsis when longer, the whole address in its tooltip, since a Teams link runs to hundreds of characters), then a meeting link on its own row when the invitation names one apart from the location; **Organizer** the organizer's name, or “You”; **Guests** the user first as “You”, then the others by name, at most {{layout.invitation.attendeesMax}}, then “+3 more” (rooms and equipment are never listed; the organizer has their own row) |
| Overlap | {{layout.invitation.detailsTop}} pt below the details, while the event overlaps another on the user's calendars: `{{icon.warning}}` {{layout.invitation.answerGlyph}} pt `warning`, {{layout.invitation.kickerGap}} pt, then “Overlaps 1:1 with Sam, 13:30 - 14:00” (`snippet` `ink`, wrapping), the overlapping event's title and the time the two share; with more than one, the first by start and “and 2 more” (“Overlaps Hiring sync, 11:00 - 11:30, and 2 more”). Glyph and words carry it, never the colour alone (§1). Overlaps count every account's events the store holds for that day except the invitation's own (the same iCalendar UID), declined ones, free ones and all-day ones; an invitation's times without a zone are read in this Mac's zone first (§5.54). None on a cancelled card, an updated one, one not sent to this account or the user's own event; it goes the moment the user declines, and comes back if they answer again. VoiceOver reads it after the When line. No toast, no undo and no action: the answers below are unchanged |
| Actions | {{layout.invitation.actionsTop}} pt below, a hairline `rule` above and {{layout.invitation.actionsPad}} pt between: Accept · Tentative · Decline as **small** buttons ({{layout.button.small.height}} pt, §5.24), {{layout.invitation.answerGap}} pt apart, sentence case (§4.13); then, trailing, the link button “Open in Calendar” (Calendar is the app's name) |
| Answer, not chosen | small secondary: `raised`, `ink` `label`, {{border.strong.width}} pt `ruleStrong` inset; hover `raised + hover`, pressed `raised + pressed`; focus ring outside a 1 pt `sheet` gap |
| Answer, chosen | the user's answer: `accentWash` with `checkmark` {{layout.invitation.answerGlyph}} pt and the label in `labelStrong` `accent`, no edge; hover `accentWashHover`, pressed `accentWashPressed` (the quoted-text toggle's on state, §5.15: tested pairs). The checkmark and the weight carry the state, not the colour. Clicking another answer moves the mark at once; clicking the chosen one does nothing, so it shows the arrow (§4.17). An answer this Mac could not record goes back at once to the one it holds |
| Permission notice | {{layout.invitation.noticeTop}} pt below the actions, an info banner (§5.38: `infoWash`, `info.circle` `info`), multi-line: “Answer invitations: SuperSparrow needs permission to update your calendar.” and the link “Reconnect” (§5.44). Only on a card answered on this Mac whose account waits for that permission |

| State | Look |
|---|---|
| Open (not answered) | the three answers, none chosen; Open in Calendar; the overlap line while it overlaps something |
| Answered | the answer chosen (given here, or as the invitation or, for Microsoft, the event states it). It is recorded on this Mac at once and goes to the organizer in the background; the row shows the queued glyph (§5.7) until it has. An answer changed before the earlier one has gone out replaces it, so the organizer hears only the latest. A failed answer goes back to the last answer that went out (none, when none did), and the row shows the failed mark: “Couldn't answer the invitation” |
| Waiting for permission | as answered, plus the permission notice; the answer goes out once the account reconnects with the permission, and the notice leaves |
| Cancelled | kicker “Cancelled” in `danger`, the title struck through, no answers; Open in Calendar stays (Calendar removes the event from there) |
| The user's own event | “Organizer · You”, no answers |
| Not sent to this account | no answers, Open in Calendar stays: an invitation that names neither the account it came to nor a group it may be on (one forwarded to the user, a Bcc, an invitation to another of their addresses) is not that account's to answer; “You” among the guests is that account alone |
| Updated | no answers and none chosen, Open in Calendar stays: a later message in the conversation carries a later revision of the same event or occurrence (a higher SEQUENCE, or the same SEQUENCE stamped later), and only that card is answered, so an answer never repeats times or a revision the organizer has replaced. The card says nothing more: the update is a later message in the same conversation |
| Unreadable | no card at all: an invitation that could not be read whole (an unknown zone, a missing start, broken syntax) shows the message as it is rather than a card that might be wrong |

**Behaviour.** Accept, Tentative and Decline record the answer on this Mac in one transaction and queue it; nothing waits on the network, and there is no undo (an answer is mailed or sent to the organizer; the user changes it by answering again) and no toast (the card shows it). Gmail mails the organizer an iMIP reply from the user's address and marks the user's own Google Calendar copy; Microsoft answers the event. The first answer asks for permission to update the calendar: without it the answer waits, the card shows the permission notice, Settings › Accounts reads “Calendar permission needed” with Reconnect (§5.40), and Reconnect signs in again asking for that permission beside mail (§5.44). Open in Calendar hands the invitation to the Mac's Calendar, which shows it and offers to add it; SuperSparrow asks for no calendar access. A repeating Microsoft event in a zone the Mac does not know goes over as its first occurrence alone: Microsoft gives its times in UTC and its repeat in that zone, which cannot be joined without knowing the zone, and a repeat on the wrong days would be worse than none. Its card still says how it repeats.

**The message under the card.** The body under a card is most often the sender's own rendering of the same invitation (Google Calendar's When, Guests, Reply and Join with Google Meet; Outlook's equivalent), so it starts folded behind the reader's elision mark (§5.15), the quoted-text toggle's component, states and motion: at the indent, {{layout.invitation.gapBelow}} below the card, where the body would start, reading “Show message”; unfolded it reads “Hide message” (the on state) and stays where it is, the whole body {{layout.reader.blockGap}} below it exactly as it shows without a card, banners first and its own quoted text still folded under its own toggle. Every state of the card folds it: to answer, answered, waiting for permission, updated by a later revision, cancelled, the user's own event, not sent to this account. A message without a card is unchanged. The mark unfolds and folds on a click, ↩ or Space with focus (focus stays on the mark; the app's ↩ for Open and Space for Quick Look give way to a focused mark, §5.15), and “.” (§6), which on a message under a card folds and unfolds the message rather than its quoted text; there the quoted-text toggle's tooltip names no key. Folding with “.” while focus is in the body (its quoted-text toggle, a link) moves the focus to the mark, never leaving it on what the fold hides. Tooltip “Show Message  .” / “Hide Message  .”; VoiceOver “Show message, toggle button” and its pressed state. The fold is remembered as the quoted-text toggle's is: per message, through the store's renders (an answer, the permission notice, a read), while the reader keeps the conversation, and forgotten when it renders it afresh. Folded, only the body itself folds: its banners (§5.16) stay in view {{layout.reader.blockGap}} below the mark, where the body starts, as they stay over folded quoted text, so what reads the body works and answers where it can be seen, and leaves the fold as it is: Load images (the banner's link, ⇧⌘I, More) shows its progress and loads the images, Original colors (the notice's button, ⇧⌘O, the hover action, More) switches the rendering, and both show in the body once it unfolds. Attachment chips stay visible, {{layout.reader.attachmentsTop}} below the mark or the last banner, and the footer follows as usual. The fold is decided from the card alone, never from the HTML, and the body still loads when the message opens, out of sight and out of reach, so unfolding animates the sheet straight to its height (`dur.standard`, `ease.settle`; Reduce Motion: instant).

**Accessibility.** The card is a group named “Invitation: Design review” (“Cancelled event: Design review”); each answer is a toggle button with its pressed state (VoiceOver: “Accept, toggle button, selected”); the links and Open in Calendar are buttons; every stop draws the Quire ring and joins the sheet's Tab order (above). The card is drawn again when the store changes it (an answer recorded, the permission notice shown or gone); the control that had focus keeps it, and when that control is gone (Reconnect, once granted) focus goes to the sheet, so a keyboard or VoiceOver user never loses their place. All inks are tested pairs on `sheet`, `raised`, `accentWash` and `infoWash` (§7).

**Wide HTML.** Styled bodies render at the sheet's content width (column − 2 × sheet padding x). After an iframe loads, the shell reads the body's `scrollWidth` once (layout, not a scan of the HTML). If it is wider than the content width, the body is scaled with CSS `zoom` to fit, down to {{layout.reader.htmlMinZoom}}; if fitting needs less than {{layout.reader.htmlMinZoom}}, the body stays at {{layout.reader.htmlMinZoom}} and scrolls horizontally inside its iframe (scroller only while scrolling, `overflow-x: auto`); nothing is clipped. The fit is recomputed when a live resize ends, never per frame.

Worked example, a 600 px newsletter with the default {{layout.list.defaultWidth}} pt list, for both rail widths (§3.2):

| Window | Reader (macOS 15, rail {{layout.rail.width}} / macOS 26 and later, rail {{layout.rail.width26}}) | Column | Content width | Zoom needed | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Default {{layout.window.defaultWidth}}, beside the day column ({{layout.calendarPane.columnWidth}}, §5.54) | 624 / 616 | 560 / 552 | 504 / 496 | 0.840 / 0.827 | zoomed to fit on both |
| Default {{layout.window.defaultWidth}}, day column hidden (D) | 864 / 856 | {{layout.reader.columnMax}} / {{layout.reader.columnMax}} | {{layout.reader.htmlWidth}} / {{layout.reader.htmlWidth}} | — | fits unscaled |
| Minimum {{layout.window.minWidth}}, the day column folded to its {{layout.calendarPane.stripWidth}} pt strip (narrow reader: margins and sheet padding {{layout.reader.narrowColumnMargin}}) | 492 / 484 | 460 / 452 | 428 / 420 | 0.713 / 0.700 | stays at {{layout.reader.htmlMinZoom}} and scrolls |
| Minimum {{layout.window.minWidth}}, day column hidden (narrow reader) | 520 / 512 | 488 / 480 | 456 / 448 | 0.760 / 0.747 | zoomed to fit on both |
| Minimum, day column hidden, Original colors (dark) | 520 / 512 | 488 / 480 | 432 / 424 (the inset's inner width) | 0.720 / 0.707 | stays at {{layout.reader.htmlMinZoom}} and scrolls |
| {{layout.window.readerMinWidth}} pt minimum reader | 400 | 368 | 336 | 0.560 | stays at {{layout.reader.htmlMinZoom}} and scrolls |

`htmlMinZoom` is {{layout.reader.htmlMinZoom}}, set just under 0.747 so that the minimum window with the day column hidden fits a standard 600 px newsletter on every supported macOS instead of scrolling it by 2 px (beside the strip, 1.9, it scrolls by 16 or 24 px at 0.74; D gives the reader its {{layout.calendarPane.stripWidth}} pt back) (at 0.75 the macOS 26 case would overflow 448 pt by 2 pt); 0.74 keeps a sender's 12 px text at 8.9 px, the same legibility floor as before.

**Colour in dark appearances.** What a body looks like depends on two flags stored at sync time (`typesetting` and `declaresColors`, §8.5), never on a scan at open:

| Body | Light, Light IC (every theme) | Dark, Dark IC, Adapt on (the default): **Adapted** | Dark, Dark IC, Adapt off: **Original colors** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Plain or unstyled | the theme's inks on the theme's sheet (`reading`) | the same, on the theme's dark sheet | the same: nothing to adapt, so no notice and no hover action |
| Styled, declares no colours | as sent, on the sheet, which every theme keeps the white page (§4.16); a link its sender left uncoloured in `--q-linkOnPage` | `color-scheme: dark` on the body document: the browser defaults turn light-on-transparent, so the dark sheet shows through; a link its sender left uncoloured in `--q-linkOnPage` through a zero-specificity rule (`:where(a:link)`) | the same |
| Styled, declares colours | as sent, on the sheet (the sheet is already the white page the sender designed for); no notice, no inset | the shell puts `ss-adapt` on the body document's root: `color-scheme: dark`; every declared text colour becomes `ink` (links `accent`); declared background colours and background images are removed (transparent, so the dark sheet shows); declared border colours become `rule`. Layout, fonts, sizes and `<img>` elements are untouched; all of these are the active theme's. The notice says so: “This message uses its own colours.” · Original colors (§5.16). Switched to Original colors by hand: the white inset of the next column, with “Showing the sender's colours.” · Adaptive colors | nothing is overridden: `ss-adapt` is removed and the body renders exactly as sent on an `originalPage` inset (`#FFFFFF` in every mode) inside the sheet, radius `r.xs` ({{layout.reader.originalInsetRadius}}), padding {{layout.reader.originalInsetPad}} on every side (`layout.reader.originalInsetPad`), filling the sheet's content width; wide-HTML fitting measures the inset's inner width (content width − 2 × {{layout.reader.originalInsetPad}}). **No notice**, since Settings chose it; the header hover action, More and ⇧⌘O adapt this one message, which then shows “This message uses its own colours.” · Original colors |

Adaptation **replaces** colours rather than inverting them or relying on `color-scheme` alone: `color-scheme` changes only the browser's defaults, so a sender's declared black text would stay black on the dark sheet, and inversion cannot promise any contrast. With Quire's inks every adapted text pair is one of the tested pairs (§7). The cost is the sender's brand colour, and images drawn for a white page (a dark logo with a transparent background) can disappear; Original colors restores both in one keystroke. In Dusk, adapted text is the dimmed night ink and adapted borders the ratio-locked `rule` (§4.16).

**The Dark Mode default** (Settings › Appearance › Dark Mode, “Adapt mail that sets its own colours”, on) decides which of the two a styled message that declares colours opens in, in a dark appearance. A message's own switch is a choice on top of it, per message and per session: the shell reads one effective state, the switch made by hand if there is one and otherwise the default, and every way to switch (⇧⌘O, the notice's button, the header hover action, More) flips what the message **shows**, so with either default the first ⇧⌘O changes the message and a second restores it. A message switched by hand keeps its choice when the default changes. View › Original Colors carries a checkmark while the focused message shows the sender's colours, read from what is shown. Every body frame sits in a permanent page wrapper (`.ss-page`) whose `ss-inset-on` class is the white inset, so a switch, a change of the default and an appearance change toggle classes and refit one message, and never build a frame again (§8.4). The white page is never themed: a link on it takes the focus ring `--q-focusOnPage`, the theme's light focus.

| State | Treatment |
|---|---|
| Rest | as above |
| Hover | header actions and the sender's address appear; a recipient or the address under the pointer takes its face |
| Focused (`n`/`p`, Tab) | ring around the sheet outside a {{border.focusRing.gapAroundSheets}} pt `canvas` gap |
| Target (several expanded, or keycaps hidden) | {{border.strong.width}} pt `ruleStrong` edge; footer keycaps |
| Starred | `star.fill` after the name, at rest |
| Unread | pip before the name |
| Loading body | skeleton bars (§5.10), only while a body fetch is actually in flight (and after {{motion.duration.skeletonDelay}} ms); never as a placeholder for a fetch that is not running |
| Body not downloaded, offline | the stored snippet stands in for the body: `reading` (or `readingSans`) in `ink2`, at the indent, inside the measure, ending in “…”; {{layout.reader.offlinePreviewGap}} pt below it a neutral banner (§5.38, `fill`): `wifi.slash` `ink2` + “The full message will load when you're back online.” No action and no skeleton: when the connection returns the fetch starts by itself, the skeleton shows while it runs, then the body replaces the preview. Attachments known from the metadata still show as chips. VoiceOver reads the preview, then the notice |
| Body failed (online; the fetch itself failed) | “Couldn't load this message · Retry” as a danger banner (§5.38) in place of the body |
| Pending send | full contrast; the date reads “Sending…” with a {{layout.progress.spinner.0}} pt spinner. A reply in the outbox (scheduled or sending; not a forward, which starts its own conversation) shows as the newest sheet of the conversation it answers, built from the outbox row: its plain-text part set as a plain body, no header actions and no footer, never the target. It goes when the send leaves or is undone; the provider's copy arrives with the next sync |
| Dark mode, styled HTML that declares colours | **Adapted** with Adapt on (the default), with the notice; **Original colors** with Adapt off, with none, on the white `originalPage` inset; either switched for one message by ⇧⌘O, the hover action, More or the notice. Both renderings are specified above, under “Colour in dark appearances” |

### 5.15 Quoted-text toggle (the elision mark)

{{TABLE:layout:quotedToggle}}

`ss.elide` {{layout.quotedToggle.glyph}} pt + `label` “Show quoted text” / “Hide quoted text”; the same mark folds the message under an invitation card, “Show message” / “Hide message” (§5.14). The label says what the mark hides. With keyboard focus, ↩ or Space presses it: the reader's ↩ (Open) and Space (Quick Look, §6) give way to a focused mark, even on a message with attachments. Find in conversation unfolds the quote (or the message) holding its current match, and leaves it unfolded (§5.50).

| State | Fill | Text and glyph |
|---|---|---|
| Folded, rest | `fill` | `ink2`, “Show quoted text” (“Show message”) |
| Folded, hover | `fill + hover` | `ink` |
| Folded, pressed | `fill + pressed` | `ink` |
| Unfolded, rest | `accentWash` | `accent`, “Hide quoted text” (“Hide message”) |
| Unfolded, hover | `accentWashHover` | `accent` |
| Unfolded, pressed | `accentWashPressed` | `accent` |
| Focused (either) | ring outside a {{border.focusRing.gap}} pt `sheet` gap | unchanged |

The label carries the state; `accent` keeps 4.5:1 on all three unfolded fills in every mode (§7). In the composer the folded original is a glyph-only {{layout.quotedToggle.composerChip}} pt chip followed by the attribution the message will carry, with the author's name only: “On 1 Nov 2026 at 23:39, Ben Carter wrote:” (a forward: “Forwarded message from Ben Carter · 1 Nov 2026 at 23:39”), in `snippet` `ink3`; the date is absolute, as sent, never “Today”. Clicking either unfolds it. Unfolding animates the quote height (`dur.standard`; Reduce Motion: instant). Revealed quotes use the shared quote style (`layout.textStyle`): a {{layout.textStyle.quoteBar}} pt `rule` leading bar and a {{layout.textStyle.quoteIndent}} pt indent, text in `ink2`, in the reader and the composer alike.

### 5.16 Remote-images banner and Original colors

The first element of a body whose remote images are blocked. Neutral: privacy is not a warning.

{{TABLE:layout:banner}}

| Part | Spec |
|---|---|
| Banner | `fill` over `sheet`, radius {{layout.banner.radius}} |
| Glyph | `photo` {{layout.banner.glyph}} pt `ink2` |
| Copy | “3 remote images hidden to protect your privacy.” (`snippet`, `ink`); “3 remote images hidden.” below {{layout.banner.narrowBreakpoint}} pt |
| Action | link button “Load images” (`label`, `accent`), {{layout.button.minHit}} pt hit. Loading is per message, as `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` specifies; there is no per-sender allowlist |
| Dismissal | disappears when the images load; no close button |

States: rest; action hover (the link gains its {{layout.textStyle.linkUnderline}} pt underline; no wash: link buttons never take one, §5.24); action pressed (underline kept, label `ink2`: a `pressed` wash behind `accent` on this `fill` banner would fall to 4.22:1 in Dark); action focused (ring); loading ({{layout.progress.spinner.0}} pt spinner replaces the glyph, “Loading 3 images…”); partial failure (“2 images couldn't load” with `exclamationmark.triangle.fill` in `warning`).

**Original colors notice** (warning banner, dark appearances only) at the top of a styled body whose `declaresColors` flag is set. Its two states are the two renderings of §5.14 (“Colour in dark appearances”): **Adapted** (the theme's inks on the dark sheet, the sender's colours removed) and **Original colors** (the body exactly as sent, on the white `originalPage` inset with {{layout.reader.originalInsetPad}} pt padding).
