| Title row | {{layout.cheatSheet.titleRow}} pt: “Keyboard Shortcuts” (`title`) and a close button ({{layout.cheatSheet.close}} pt icon button, `xmark`, at {{layout.cheatSheet.closeInset}} pt from the top-trailing corner) |
| Sections | {{layout.cheatSheet.headerGap}} pt below the title: {{layout.cheatSheet.bands}} bands of {{layout.cheatSheet.columns}} columns, each {{layout.cheatSheet.columnWidth}} pt, {{layout.cheatSheet.columnGap}} pt apart: **Move · Act · Go** in the first band, **Write · Format · View** in the second, {{layout.cheatSheet.sectionGap}} pt between bands; each section opens with a {{layout.cheatSheet.sectionTitle}} pt `runningHead` `ink3` title |
| Search line | {{layout.cheatSheet.sectionGap}} pt below band 2, one {{layout.cheatSheet.row}} pt row: the title (`runningHead` `ink3`) in the {{layout.cheatSheet.keyColumn}} pt key column, then the search operators as typed (`snippet`, `ink`, plain text, not keycaps: they are typed, not pressed), {{layout.cheatSheet.searchTermGap}} pt apart; they take 652 of the 672 pt after the key column |
| Rows | {{layout.cheatSheet.row}} pt: large keycaps ({{layout.cheatSheet.keycap}} pt, {{layout.cheatSheet.keycapGap}} pt apart) in a {{layout.cheatSheet.keyColumn}} pt key column, then the label (`snippet`, `ink`, sentence case, §4.13; it tail-truncates in the remaining 144 pt, which every label below fits); sequences as “G then I” (“then” in `meta` `ink3`, 6 pt either side); ranges as “⌘0 … ⌘9” |

**Content**, in this order. It is the whole single-key and command vocabulary of §6 and the menus, so nothing is learnable only from a menu (the sender rules alone have no key, §5.52, and so no row); the overlay is generated from this table (`cheatSheetContent` in `tokens.json`), and the specimen draws it from the same data.

{{TABLE:cheatSheet}}

Alternates that duplicate a single key (⌘F for /, ⌘N for C, ⌘O for ↩, ⌘Z for Z, ← for esc, ⌘/ for ?, ⌘= for ⌘+) are left out to keep one key per action; menus and tooltips name them. → is no alternate: it opens the conversation in the main window's reader, where ↩ opens it in a window of its own (§5.49). Keys that undo or mirror each other share a row, as J K and N P do: → esc, E ⇧I (Archive and its inverse), # ! (the two ways out to a provider's folder), ⇧⌘I ⇧⌘O and ⌥⌘C ⌃⌘S; and ⌘0 … ⌘9, All Inboxes and then each account, one range since the Outbox's G O joined Go (§5.53). Day and week (§5.54) adds three rows and no more, keys that mirror each other again sharing one: B in Act, with the other things done to the selected conversation; [ ] T in Go, since they go to a day (or a week); D W in View, beside the rail's toggle. Band 1 now holds {{PY: cheat_sheet_band_rows(0)}} rows and band 2 {{PY: cheat_sheet_band_rows(1)}}, with every key on the sheet, {{PY: cheat_sheet_height()}} pt in all, which no longer fits the default window whole: it scrolls there under its title row (above), as merging more rows would only hide what the keys do. Every label fits its {{PY: T.LAYOUT['cheatSheet']['columnWidth'] - T.LAYOUT['cheatSheet']['keyColumn']}} pt.

**Search line**, under the bands (`cheatSheetSearch` in `tokens.json`): the operators §5.6 describes. `in:inbox`, `in:drafts` and `in:all` work too and are left off the line, which they would push past the panel; §5.6 lists them.

{{TABLE:cheatSheetSearch}}

Motion: in, the scrim fades (`dur.quick`) and the panel fades and scales from 0.98 (`dur.standard`, `ease.settle`); out, both fade in `dur.quick` (`ease.leave`). Reduce Motion: fades only.

Focus: on open the panel becomes first responder and traps focus (Tab moves only between its close button and its scroll area); nothing behind it responds to keys. Single-key shortcuts are swallowed while it is open, so learning them is safe. Dismissal: Esc, ? or ⌘/ again, ⌘W, the close button, or a click on the scrim; focus returns to where it was. VoiceOver treats it as a modal group titled “Keyboard Shortcuts”.

### 5.33 Alerts and sheets

System `NSAlert` presented as a window-modal sheet on the window where the action started (the main window, a composer or Settings), with the app icon. The one exception is the launch failure below: no window exists yet, so it is an app-modal `runModal()` alert with nothing behind it. Quire supplies:

- **Title** as a question or a fact, sentence case: “Discard this draft?”, “Couldn't send the message”, “Remove alex@contoso.test?”.
- **Body**: what happens or what went wrong, then the remedy: “The message to Ben Carter hasn't been sent. Discarding removes it from this Mac.” / “Mail from this account is removed from this Mac. Nothing changes on the server.”
- **Buttons**: verbs, title case (§4.13). The safe choice is the default (↩); the destructive button sets `hasDestructiveAction` (system red text) and is never the default; Esc cancels. Never two destructive buttons.

The app's complete alert inventory, so no screen invents its own:

| Where | Title | Body | Buttons (default first) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Composer: Message › Discard Draft… (§5.20) | Discard this draft? | The message to Ben Carter hasn't been sent. Discarding removes it from this Mac. | Keep Writing · Discard (destructive) |
| Drafts reader: Discard Draft (§5.19) | Discard this draft? (several selected: “Discard 3 drafts?”) | The message to Ben Carter hasn't been sent. Discarding removes it from this Mac. (several: “They haven't been sent. Discarding removes them from this Mac.”) | Cancel · Discard (destructive) |
| Composer: send with an empty subject (§5.20) | Send without a subject? | Messages without a subject are easy to miss. | Add Subject · Send Anyway |
| Settings: Remove… (§5.40) | Remove alex@contoso.test? | Mail from this account is removed from this Mac. Nothing changes on the server. | Cancel · Remove (destructive) |
| Settings: Remove failed (§5.40) | Couldn't remove alex@contoso.test | the reason in plain words, then “The account is still here; nothing was deleted.” | Try Again · Cancel |
| Add account: no OAuth client (§5.44) | Set up a Google OAuth client first | SuperSparrow needs your own Google OAuth client ID to add a Gmail account. | Open Settings · Cancel |
| Add account failed (§5.44) | Couldn't add the account | the reason in plain words | Try Again · Cancel |
| Add account: already added (§5.44) | alex@contoso.test is already added | Choose it in the rail or in Settings. | Show Account · Cancel |
| Reconnect failed (§5.44) | Couldn't reconnect alex@fabrikam.test | the reason in plain words (the same list as “Couldn't add the account”) | Try Again · Cancel |
| Reconnect with a different account (§5.44) | Signed in to a different account | You signed in as sam@contoso.test, but this account is alex@fabrikam.test. Nothing was changed. Try again and choose alex@fabrikam.test in your browser. | Try Again · Cancel |
| Launch: the mail database can't be opened | SuperSparrow couldn't open its mail database | the reason in plain words, then “It is stored in ~/Library/Application Support/SuperSparrow.” | Quit (the only button; ↩ and Esc). App-modal, before any window exists: no sheet and nothing behind it; the app quits when it closes |
| Leave the demo (§5.43) | Leave the demo? | The made-up mailboxes are removed from this Mac. You'll return to the welcome screen. | Leave Demo · Cancel |
| First real account in demo mode (§5.43) | Replace the demo with this account? | The made-up mailboxes are removed from this Mac when your account is added. | Continue · Cancel |
| Reader: Open on an attachment that is a program or script (§5.17) | Open “Invoice.command”? | It is a program or script, and opening it lets it change your Mac. Open it only if you trust the sender and expected it. | Cancel · Open |
| Later: Pick a Date… (§5.45) | When should this come back? (several: “When should these 3 come back?”) | It returns to the Inbox at this time, marked unread. (several: “They return…”) Below it, the accessory: the system's graphical calendar and a time field | Snooze (disabled while the time is not ahead) · Cancel |
| Send Later: Pick a Time… (§5.46) | When should this send? | It sends at this time while SuperSparrow is open. Until then it waits in Drafts. Below it, Later's accessory | Schedule (disabled while the time is not ahead) · Cancel |
| Launch: Send Later times missed while the app was closed (§5.46) | A scheduled message wasn't sent (several: “3 scheduled messages weren't sent”) | “Re: Contoso rollout” to Ben Carter was due to send at Mon 09:00, while SuperSparrow was closed. Send it now, or keep it in Drafts. (several: “They were due to send while SuperSparrow was closed. Send them now, or keep them in Drafts.”) | Keep in Drafts (↩ and Esc) · Send Now |

Closing a composer never asks (its draft is kept, §5.20), and a rejected attachment drop is a composer status, not an alert. **Unreadable OAuth settings** (today's “Your OAuth settings could not be read” alert) is not an alert either: the add or reconnect stops, Settings › OAuth opens, and a danger banner at the top of the tab reads “Couldn't read the OAuth settings.” + the reason + “Correct the fields below and click Save.” (§5.40, Notices); it stays until a save succeeds. Today's generic “OK” error alerts (`AppDelegate.showError`) map onto the rows above; no other error alert remains.

Sign-in failures from the main window use this alert (§5.44). Later's Pick a Date… is this alert with an accessory (§5.45), and so is Send Later's Pick a Time… (§5.46). The only app-drawn modal surface is the cheat-sheet overlay (§5.32).

### 5.34 Tooltips

System `toolTip` on every icon-only control and on truncated text; Quire defines no tooltip view and no tooltip metrics. Content: the action name, two spaces, the shortcut (“Archive  E”, “Reply All  A”, “All Inboxes  ⌘0”), in title case because it repeats the command's menu name (§4.13). Disabled controls explain why (“Nothing to undo”). A control whose action is a choice says the one in force: Send reads “Send and Archive  ⌘↩” or “Send  ⌘↩” (§5.23). The reader uses `title` attributes so WebKit shows the same system tooltip. Delay and look are the system's.

### 5.35 Menus

Main menu (system), with Quire content. Single-key shortcuts are handled by `KeyboardRouter` and shown as attributed shortcut text, so they never fire inside text fields.

- **SuperSparrow**: About SuperSparrow (the system About panel) · Settings… ⌘, · Services (the system's; SuperSparrow adds two, §5.20) · Hide · Quit.
- **File**: New Message ⌘N (also C) · Open in New Window ⌘O (the selected conversation in a reader window, §5.49) · Close Window ⌘W · Add Gmail Account… · Add Microsoft Account… · Save Attachments… · Download Attachments ⇧⌘D (the target message's, to Downloads, §5.17; enabled while it has any) · — · Print… ⌘P (the open conversation, §5.48) · in demo mode, Leave Demo… (§5.43).
- **Edit**: standard, plus Find… ⌘F (focuses search), Find in Conversation… ⌥⌘F (`{{icon.findInConversation}}`; the find bar over the reader, §5.50), Find Next ⌘G and Find Previous ⇧⌘G (enabled while one conversation shows and there is a query to find: the bar's, or the session's last) and Search Gmail for More (§5.10, no key equivalent: ↩ in the search field runs it, and a plain ↩ on a menu item would take Return from the list, the composer and every sheet; titled by the scope: Search Outlook for More, Search All Accounts for More). It is enabled while the search field holds words whose local results are all listed (or not in yet) and the server has not been asked for them, or failed; it is disabled while any other place to type or the reader has focus, and hidden while a composer is key. Undo is titled by its target (“Undo Archive”, “Undo Send”, “Undo Send and Archive”, “Undo Typing”), following §6.1.
- **View**: Inbox · Later (while the rail shows it) · Starred · Sent · Drafts · Outbox (while the rail shows it, §5.53) · Trash · All Mail (G-sequences) · All Inboxes ⌘0 · accounts ⌘1–9 · Check for New Mail ⇧⌘N · Show/Hide Account Rail ⌃⌘S · Compact Rows ⌥⌘C · Load Images ⇧⌘I · Original Colors ⇧⌘O (checkmark when on) · Show Quoted Text . (on a message under an invitation card it shows or hides the message, §5.14) · — · Hide Day / Show Day D · Show Week / Hide Week W · Previous Day [ · Next Day ] (Previous Week, Next Week while the week shows) · Today T (This Week while the week shows; these three enabled while the day column or the week shows, §5.54) · — · Make Text Bigger ⌘+ · Make Text Smaller ⌘− (the reader's own size, §4.15; ⌘= is taken as ⌘+, as Safari takes it; disabled at Larger and at Small)
- **Message**: Reply R · Reply All A · Forward F · — · Archive E · Later… L (the Later menu at the selection, §5.45) · Move to Trash # · Mark as Spam ! (§5.47) · Move to Inbox ⇧I · Star S · Mark as Unread U · Mute M (Unmute when every selected conversation is muted, §5.51) · Block Time B (`{{icon.blockTime}}`; enabled while one conversation is selected, §5.54) · Undo Z · — · Unsubscribe ⇧⌘U (enabled while one conversation is open and offers it, §5.14; no symbol, since SF Symbols has none for it before macOS 26) · Always Archive Mail from This Sender · Block Sender (no key; enabled while one conversation is open with mail from someone else, the rule its sender has checked and disabled, §5.52) · — · Next Message N · Previous Message P. In a reader window the same items act on its conversation (§5.49), and the context menu on its messages is the one below, acting on that conversation.
- **Format** (composer windows only): Show Format Row ⇧⌘T · — · Bold ⌘B · Italic ⌘I · Underline ⌘U · — · Bulleted List ⇧⌘8 · Numbered List ⇧⌘7 · Quote ⌘' · — · Add Link… ⌘K · Insert Picture… ⌥⌘I (no symbol; enabled whenever the composer is not sending: it puts pictures into the body from any field, §5.20 Images in the body). In a composer window the Message menu holds Send ⌘↩ (what the Send button does, §5.23) · Send and Archive ⇧⌘↩ (always; no symbol) · Send Later… ⇧⌘L (`clock`; Send Later's times hung from the drop-down, §5.46) · Attach Files… ⇧⌘A · Add Cc Field ⇧⌘C · Add Bcc Field ⇧⌘B · — · Discard Draft… (§5.20).
- **Window**, **Help** (Keyboard Shortcuts ⌘/).

Context menu on rows and messages: Reply, Reply All, Forward, —, Archive, Later (a submenu holding the Later menu's items, §5.45), Star, Mark as Unread, Mute (§5.51), Move to Inbox (disabled where it does not apply), —, Move to Trash, Mark as Spam; every item with its symbol and shortcut, title case, disabled items visible. Then, after a separator, Unsubscribe ⇧⌘U (no symbol, as in the Message menu), present only while the open conversation offers it (§5.14), since most mail is no list's, and Always Archive Mail from This Sender and Block Sender, present only while one conversation is open with mail from someone other than the user (§5.52): on a message, its sender; on a row, the sender of the open conversation's target message (§5.14), or of its newest such mail when the user sent the target; the toast names the address it took. It acts on the selection, like the Message menu, and shares its validation (Star reads Unstar when every selected conversation is starred; Move to Inbox is disabled in the Inbox; Move to Trash in Trash; Mark as Spam in Trash and Drafts). A right-click on a row outside the selection selects that row first; heads and the list tail have no menu. On a message it opens over the sheet's or collapsed row's own chrome; inside a body, which is the sender's document, the system's text menu stays, for Copy and links. The message More menu is §5.14; the address token's menu §5.21. The person menu on a recipient or the sender in a sheet header: Copy Address, Copy Name and Address, —, New Message, Show Mail from This Person; items that do not apply are hidden (§5.14). The rail's add menu is §5.44; the demo tag's menu §5.43. The highlight is the system's (Iron Gall under Multicolor, §3.1).

### 5.36 Keyboard shortcut hints (keycaps)

{{TABLE:layout:kbd}}

| Part | Spec |
|---|---|
| Keycap | min {{layout.kbd.minWidth}} × {{layout.kbd.height}} ({{layout.kbd.inField}} inside the search field), padding 0 {{layout.kbd.padX}}, radius {{layout.kbd.radius}} (`r.xs`); `raised` with a {{border.strong.width}} pt `rule` inset and a {{border.strong.width}} pt `rule` bottom inset (key depth); Increase Contrast uses `ruleStrong` |
| Legend | `kbd` (SF Pro {{typography.kbd.size}} Medium) `ink2`; modifiers in Apple order ⌃⌥⇧⌘; Return “↩”, Delete “⌫”, Escape “esc”, Space “space” |
| Sequence | two caps with “then” in `meta` `ink3` between them (cheat sheet, empty states); adjacent caps in menus and tooltips |
| On the slip | no fill, `onSlipEdge` outline; legend `onSlip2` at rest, `onSlip` while its action is hovered or pressed |
| Large | {{layout.kbd.largeHeight}} pt, radius {{layout.kbd.largeRadius}} (`r.sm`), {{layout.kbd.largeFont}} pt legend (cheat sheet) |

Where keycaps appear: search “/”, the autocomplete's “↩”, quick reply “R”, “⌘↩” beside Send, the server search's “↩” while the search field has the keyboard (§5.9, §5.10), the target message's R · A · F, the quick-reply strip's “⌘Z”, toast “Z”, undo panel “⌘Z”, the Drafts “↩”, the day column's “B” on the selected mail's time (§5.54), empty states, the HUD and the cheat sheet. Keycaps never sit on accent fills, without exception: the autocomplete's “↩” sits on the `selWash` highlight in its normal raised style (§5.21), and “⌘↩” sits beside Send, not inside it.

**Narrow reader.** When the reader is narrower than {{layout.window.readerKeycapsMin}} pt (`layout.window.readerKeycapsMin`, {{layout.reader.columnMax}} minus two {{layout.reader.columnMinMargin}} pt margins), the reader's keycaps hide: the quick reply's “R” and “⌘↩”, the strip's “⌘Z”, the target message's R · A · F (its `ruleStrong` edge stays, §5.14) and the Drafts footer's “↩”. Tooltips keep naming the shortcuts. Empty-state hints on the desk keep their keycaps and wrap instead. Keycaps outside the reader (search, toast, undo panel, HUD, cheat sheet, the day column's “B”) never hide.

### 5.37 Progress

{{TABLE:layout:progress}}

| Indicator | Spec | Use |
|---|---|---|
| Spinner (AppKit) | `QuireSpinnerView`, layer-drawn (not `NSProgressIndicator`, which has no 12 pt spinning size and no public tint): a `CAShapeLayer` circle path inset by half the stroke, `strokeStart` 0, `strokeEnd` {{layout.progress.spinnerArc}} (a ¾ arc, butt caps), stroke {{layout.progress.spinnerStroke}} pt at {{layout.progress.spinner}} pt; a `transform.rotation.z` `CABasicAnimation` of one turn per `dur.spinnerTurn`, linear, `repeatCount = .infinity`. Colour: `ink3`; inside filled buttons the label colour (`onAccent`, `onDanger`); on slips `onSlip2`; re-resolved on appearance change. It is added {{motion.duration.spinnerDelay}} ms after work starts (and never if the work ends sooner), fades in over `dur.quick`, and removes its animation when hidden or off-window | buttons, sync line, search, chips, undo panel, rail tile, list footer, Settings rows |
| Spinner (reader CSS) | `.ss-spin`: the same shape, a circle with a {{layout.progress.spinnerStroke}} pt border in `currentColor` and one quarter (`border-right-color: transparent`) left open, rotating once per `dur.spinnerTurn`, linear; same delay and fade-in | message “Sending…”, image loading |
| Determinate bar | {{layout.progress.barHeight}} pt, radius {{layout.progress.barRadius}}, track `fill`, fill `accent`; jumps forward without easing, never backwards | Settings › Accounts download status ({{layout.settings.statusBarWidth}} pt wide, §5.40) |
| Contrail | {{layout.progress.contrail}} pt line in `accent` (`onSlip` on slips) whose trailing {{layout.progress.contrailTail}} pt fades to transparent, with a {{layout.progress.contrailHead}} pt head dot at the moving end. Determinate: length = progress. Indeterminate: a segment {{layout.progress.indeterminateSegment}} of the width sweeping every `dur.contrailSweep` (Reduce Motion: a static segment {{layout.progress.reducedSegment}} of the width; the text carries the state) | sync line, attachment progress, toast life, undo countdown |

Spinners appear only after {{motion.duration.spinnerDelay}} ms and **never in rows**; the row's only status marks are the static `ss.queued` glyph and the failed triangle.

### 5.38 Semantic banners

{{layout.banner.height}} pt, radius {{layout.banner.radius}}, padding 0 {{layout.banner.padRight}} 0 {{layout.banner.padLeft}}, gap {{layout.banner.gap}}, `snippet` `ink` text, glyph {{layout.banner.glyph}} pt in the semantic colour, optional trailing link button and optional dismiss button (`xmark` {{layout.banner.dismissGlyph}} pt `ink2`, {{layout.button.minHit}} pt hit). Variants: neutral (`fill`, glyph `ink2`), info (`infoWash`, `info`), success (`successWash`, `success`), warning (`warningWash`, `warning`), danger (`dangerWash`, `danger`). Never more than one status banner per message.

**Multi-line.** Text that does not fit wraps instead of truncating where the words matter (Settings notices, sign-in errors): the banner grows from {{layout.banner.height}} pt with {{layout.banner.padY}} pt top and bottom padding, the glyph aligns to the first line, and the link sits after the text on its last line.

### 5.39 Welcome screen

Fills the main window until the first account exists; the rail and list are hidden; window background `paper`; the traffic lights stay where the empty unified toolbar puts them (§3.2).

{{TABLE:layout:welcome}}

| Part | Spec |
|---|---|
| Block | max width {{layout.welcome.blockWidth}}, centred horizontally; vertically its centre sits at {{layout.welcome.blockCenterY}} of the content height below the title-bar zone (`welcome.blockCenterY`: optically centred, a little high). It re-centres, instantly, whenever a banner or a state changes its height |
| Mark | `ss.paperPlane` in a {{layout.welcome.mark}} pt square in the brand's black and white, `{{layout.brand.ink}}` on a light page and `{{layout.brand.paper}}` on a dark one, like the wordmark under it, whatever the theme (§4.11); the plane spans the square's width |
| Wordmark | the drawn wordmark (`layout.brand.wordmark`, the outlines of docs/brand/source/wordmark.svg), never the name set in a font, in the mark's black or white, its cap height {{layout.welcome.wordmarkCap}} pt, {{layout.welcome.titleGap}} pt below the mark's frame, centred on its ink. Its frame runs from the cap height to the p's descender (the letters are straight-edged and have no overshoot), the baseline on a whole point. VoiceOver reads it as an image, “SuperSparrow”; the screen itself is a group described “Welcome to SuperSparrow”, the greeting the screen no longer prints |
| Tagline | “Your mail, quietly fast.” in `aside` `ink2`, {{layout.welcome.taglineGap}} pt below the wordmark's descender |
| Actions | {{layout.welcome.actionsGap}} pt below: two large secondary buttons, {{layout.welcome.buttonWidth}} wide, {{layout.welcome.buttonGap}} apart: `{{icon.addAccountProvider}}` + “Add Gmail account” (trailing note “Google” in `snippet` **`ink2`**), “Add Microsoft account” (trailing note “365 · Outlook”). The note is `ink2`, not `ink3`, because it sits on `raised` and on its hover and pressed washes, where `ink3` falls to 4.24:1 in Dark (§7 tests the note on all three). Two equal choices, so neither is primary; the first has initial focus |
| Demo | ghost “Try the demo”, {{layout.welcome.demoGap}} pt below (tooltip: “Explore SuperSparrow with two made-up mailboxes. Nothing leaves this Mac.”); it opens the main window in demo mode (§5.43) |
| Footnote | {{layout.welcome.footGap}} pt below, `meta` `ink3`, max {{layout.welcome.footWidth}} wide, with `lock`: “Your mail is stored on this Mac and travels only between this Mac and Google or Microsoft. No analytics, ever.” |
| Link | “Set up OAuth clients…”: a link button (§5.24) in `label` `accent`, centred, {{layout.welcome.linkGap}} pt below the footnote (`welcome.linkGap`); it opens Settings › OAuth |
| Banner slot | between the tagline and the actions: when a banner shows it takes the actions' place, {{layout.welcome.actionsGap}} pt below the tagline, and the buttons follow {{layout.welcome.bannerGap}} pt below it (`welcome.bannerGap`); full block width, multi-line (§5.38). One banner at a time, the newest winning: sign-in error, demo failed, or no OAuth client |

| State | Look |
|---|---|
| Rest | as above; the first button has initial focus |
| Hover, pressed, focused | the secondary-button states of §5.24; the trailing note stays `ink2` in every one |
| Waiting for the browser | the chosen button goes back to its **rest** face (it does not keep the pressed face) and becomes inert: a {{layout.welcome.spinner}} pt `ink2` spinner replaces `{{icon.addAccountProvider}}`, the label reads “Finish signing in in your browser…”, the trailing note hides, focus stays on it. The other provider button and the OAuth link are disabled, and the demo slot shows ghost “Cancel” in place of “Try the demo” |
| Preparing the demo (after “Try the demo”) | the ghost button becomes inert and reads “Preparing the demo…” with a {{layout.progress.spinner.0}} pt `ink2` spinner {{layout.button.glyphGap}} pt before the label (after {{motion.duration.spinnerDelay}} ms, so a fast seed shows no spinner); both large buttons and the OAuth link are disabled. On success the window cross-fades to the demo inbox (`dur.slow`, §5.43) |
| Demo failed | everything re-enables and the ghost button reads “Try the demo” again; a multi-line danger banner in the banner slot: “Couldn't start the demo. The made-up mailboxes couldn't be created on this Mac.” + link “Try again” |
| Sign-in error | “Couldn't add the account” + the reason, as a multi-line danger banner in the banner slot |
| No OAuth client configured (after choosing a provider) | an info banner in the banner slot: `info.circle` + “Add your Google OAuth client in Settings › OAuth first.” + the trailing link “Open Settings” |
| Success | `dur.slow` cross-fade to the inbox with the skeleton |
 Adding accounts later, from the main window or Settings, follows §5.44 with the same copy.

### 5.40 Settings window

SwiftUI `Settings { TabView { … } }` with the **system toolbar tab style**, {{layout.settings.width}} pt wide, background `canvas`. Height fits the current tab's content, never taller than the screen's visible frame; the account group is capped (below), so the tallest tab, Accounts with a two-line notice, six visible rows, the +/− buttons and a full detail form, is 800 pt of content ({{layout.settings.padTop}} + a 46 pt banner + {{layout.settings.noticeGap}} + 336 + {{layout.settings.plusMinusTop}} + {{layout.settings.plusMinus}} + {{layout.settings.detailTop}} + 310 for six detail rows with a three-line error and the 82 pt Signature row + {{layout.settings.padBottom}}), 888 pt with the window's 88 pt title bar and toolbar (macOS 26). A shorter visible frame stops the window at its height and cuts the foot of the form, which takes all of that at once: six accounts, a notice and a three-line error. Tabs: **Accounts** (`person.crop.circle`), **OAuth** (`key`), **General** (`gearshape`), **Appearance** (`{{icon.appearanceTab}}`; `circle.lefthalf.filled` is Original colors'), appended so no tab a user knows moves. The toolbar and its tabs are system chrome: macOS draws their size, material, selection and focus (the selected tab's symbol takes the accent asset, §3.1), so Quire specifies no tab metrics and asserts no tab contrast (§7). Everything **inside** the content area is Quire-drawn with the custom SwiftUI styles of §8.3, so every edge and every focus ring there meets 3:1. Content padding {{layout.settings.padTop}} {{layout.settings.padX}} {{layout.settings.padBottom}}.

{{TABLE:layout:settings}}

**Accounts.** An optional notice banner (below), then the account group (`sheet`, radius {{layout.settings.groupRadius}}, hairline edge) of {{layout.settings.accountRow}} pt rows, then the +/− buttons {{layout.settings.plusMinusTop}} pt below the group, then, {{layout.settings.detailTop}} pt below the buttons (`settings.detailTop`), the detail of the selected account.

**Group capacity.** The group shows at most {{layout.settings.groupMaxRows}} rows ({{layout.settings.groupMaxRows}} × {{layout.settings.accountRow}} = 336 pt, `settings.groupMaxRows`) and scrolls beyond that, exactly like the rail's stack (§5.2): a `rule` hairline marks each clipped edge (bottom, and top once scrolled), the scroller shows only while scrolling, and ↑/↓ scroll the selected row into view instantly. The group's corner radius clips its content; the +/− buttons stay below it.

| Account row part | Spec |
|---|---|
| Face | the account's rail face ({{layout.settings.accountFace}} pt, {{border.accountRing.gap}} pt gap in the row's fill, {{border.accountRing.width}} pt ring): the face's leading edge at x = {{layout.settings.accountFaceX}} (`layout.settings.accountFaceX`), so the ring's outer edge runs from x = 12 to x = 50; vertically centred |
| Name line | from x = {{layout.settings.accountHairlineX}}: the name in `bodyStrong` `ink`, then {{layout.settings.tagGap}} pt and the provider tag “Gmail” / “Microsoft 365” (“Demo” in demo mode) on the same line, sharing its centre; the name truncates, the tag never does |
| Address line | `snippet` `ink2` under the name; for Sign in required it is replaced by the status words in `danger` (below) |
| Status | a trailing block, {{layout.settings.statusTrailing}} pt from the group's trailing edge (`statusTrailing`), centred on the row: glyph {{layout.settings.statusGlyph}} pt, {{layout.settings.statusGlyphGap}} pt, the words in `snippet`. When the state has an action, a small secondary button takes the trailing position and the glyph and words sit {{layout.settings.statusButtonGap}} pt before it. The name and address block ends at least {{layout.settings.statusGap}} pt before the status block (`statusGap`) and truncates first |
| Separators | hairlines from the text column (x = {{layout.settings.accountHairlineX}}); hidden around the selected row |
| Selected | `selWash` + a {{border.marginRule.width}} pt `selRule` margin rule flush with the group's leading edge, full row height (clipped by the group's corner radius on the first and last rows) while Settings is key; `selWashIdle` + `selRuleIdle` when it is not. The wash is a scan cue (`selWash` ≥ 1.25:1 on `sheet`); the rule is the state (≥ 3:1 on the wash and on `sheet`, both keys, §7) |
| Hover, pressed | **none.** Rows take no hover and no pressed wash: selection applies on mouse-down, as in native Mac source lists, and a wash under the account ring would need its own pairs (Jade's ring on `sheet + pressed` falls to 3.41:1 in Dark IC, below the 3.5 IC floor of §4.2). The ring therefore only ever sits on `sheet`, `selWash` and `selWashIdle`, all tested. Buttons inside a row (Reconnect, Retry, Cancel) keep their own states (§5.24) |
| Detail | the form below the +/− buttons (next table) |

**Detail form**, on the same grid as OAuth and General: labels `body` `ink`, right-aligned in the {{layout.settings.labelColumn}} pt column; controls start {{layout.settings.labelGap}} pt after it; rows {{layout.settings.detailRow}} pt (`settings.detailRow`), each control centred vertically in its row. It describes the selected account and is replaced by nothing (no rows) when the group is empty.

| Row | Label | Control |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Colour | popup (§5.27), {{layout.popup.height}} pt high, {{layout.settings.colourPopupWidth}} pt wide (`settings.colourPopupWidth`): the {{layout.settings.swatch}} pt swatch on its {{layout.popup.inkHalo}} pt `raised` halo, 8 pt, the ink's name (“Persimmon”); the menu lists the ten inks with swatches, a checkmark on the current one |
| 2 | Monogram | text field {{layout.settings.monogramFieldWidth}} × {{layout.settings.fieldHeight}} pt (`settings.monogramFieldWidth`), max {{layout.settings.monogramMaxLength}} characters; placeholder = the derived monogram in `ink3`, so clearing the field returns to it. No caption |
| 3 | Signature | a {{layout.settings.signatureLines}}-line field: the composer's own editor (§5.20) in the text-field container (§5.25: `sheet`, radius {{layout.textField.radius}}, `ruleStrong` edge, `ink3` hovered, the focus ring while it edits), {{layout.settings.fieldWidth}} × {{layout.settings.signatureFieldHeight}} pt (`settings.signatureFieldHeight` = {{layout.settings.fieldHeight}} + ({{layout.settings.signatureLines}} − 1) × {{typography.writing.lineHeight}}), its top 8 pt into the row (({{layout.settings.detailRow}} − {{layout.settings.fieldHeight}}) / 2, as a one-line field's), so the row is 82 pt and the label, centred in the row's first {{layout.settings.detailRow}} pt, sits level with the first line. Text in `writing` from {{layout.textField.padX}} pt in, the first line where a one-line field's text would sit (({{layout.settings.fieldHeight}} − {{typography.writing.lineHeight}}) / 2 pt from the top); past three lines it scrolls inside (scroller only while scrolling). Bold, italic, underline and links through the composer's shortcuts (⌘B ⌘I ⌘U, and ⌘K for its link popover, which closes, as in the composer, when focus goes anywhere else or another account is selected); lists and quotes are off (⇧⌘8, ⇧⌘7 and ⌘' do nothing there); pasting is plain, as in the composer. Empty by default, no placeholder and no caption. Saved as the HTML the composer sends (anything else dropped) when editing ends: focus leaves the field, another account is selected, another window or app comes forward, the window closes, a composer opens while Settings is in front (⌘N, a notification's Reply), or the app quits (before the store closes); never per keystroke. Emptied, the account has no signature |
| 4 | Rules | Senders… (small secondary, at the field column's leading edge): the Senders sheet, the one place sender rules are listed (§5.52) |
| 5 | Last error (only when there is one) | the error in `meta` `ink2` (“Gmail returned an unexpected response.”), wrapping in the field column ({{layout.settings.fieldWidth}} pt) up to {{layout.settings.lastErrorMaxLines}} lines (`settings.lastErrorMaxLines`; the row grows by {{typography.meta.lineHeight}} pt per extra line), the full text in its tooltip |
| 6 | — | Remove… (small secondary, at the field column's leading edge; confirms with the destructive alert of §5.33). In demo mode it reads Leave Demo… |

**Seven accounts.** When a {{layout.badge.autoInitialsAt}}th account is added and **Show account initials in unified views** turns on by itself (§4.2), Settings says why in two places. In **Accounts**, an info banner in the notice slot: `info.circle` + “Rows now show each account's initial. With seven or more accounts, colours alone can look alike.” + the link “Change in General…” and a dismiss button; it appears once, stays (across launches) until dismissed or followed, and is not shown again. In **General**, a caption under the Rows checkbox, in the field column {{layout.settings.captionGap}} pt below it (`meta` `ink3`): “Turned on when you added a seventh account.”, shown while the setting is still in that automatic state; once the user changes the checkbox the caption goes and the choice is kept (an eighth account does not turn it back on).

**Account status.** The same words as the sync line's tooltip (§5.11), one per account phase:

| Status | Glyph | Words (`snippet`) | Action |
|---|---|---|---|
| Up to date (idle) | `checkmark.circle.fill` `success` | “Up to date” `ink2`; tooltip “Checked 23:41” | — |
| Up to date, downloading older mail (new mail is current; the older mail downloads behind it) | `checkmark.circle.fill` `success` | “Up to date · older mail 3,412 of 8,120” `ink2` (“Up to date · older mail 3,412” while the total is unknown, “Up to date · older mail…” before the first count); while the total is known, the Downloading row's determinate bar under the words; tooltip “Checked 23:41” | — |
| Up to date, older mail paused (the provider is limiting the older-mail download) | `checkmark.circle.fill` `success` | “Up to date · older mail paused until 10:42” `ink2`, no bar; the tooltip adds the cause on its own line (“Gmail is limiting requests.”). Never Retrying: new mail still arrives | — |
| Up to date, downloading messages (the older mail is listed; the bodies the backfill still lacks arrive behind it) | `checkmark.circle.fill` `success` | “Up to date · downloading messages…” `ink2`, no bar; tooltip “Checked 23:41”. A message opened before its body arrives fetches it at once. A throttle on these downloads keeps these words, never “paused until” | — |
| Waiting to sync (added; the engine has not reached it yet) | `clock` `ink3` | “Waiting to sync” `ink3` | — |
| Downloading (a new account's first page of newest mail, before its first check; the older mail after it reads as Up to date) | {{layout.progress.spinner.0}} pt `ink3` spinner | “Downloading · 3,412 of 8,120” `ink2` (“Downloading · 3,412 messages” while the total is unknown); while the total is known, a {{layout.settings.statusBarWidth}} × {{layout.progress.barHeight}} pt determinate bar (§5.37) {{layout.settings.statusBarGap}} pt under the words, trailing-aligned with them | — |
| Checking (a check the user asked for) | spinner | “Checking for mail…” `ink2` | — |
| Retrying (rate limited, or unreachable while other accounts are online) | `clock` `warning` | “Retrying in 30 s” `ink2`, or “Retrying shortly” when the server gave no time; the tooltip names the cause (“Gmail is limiting requests.”, “Couldn't reach Gmail.”) | — |
| Offline | `wifi.slash` `ink2` | “Offline” `ink2` | — |
| Sign in required | `exclamationmark.triangle.fill` `danger` | “Sign in required” in `danger`, **in place of the address line**; the status block holds only the button | small secondary “Reconnect” |
| Calendar permission needed (mail is up to date; an invitation answered on this Mac waits for permission to update the calendar, §5.14) | `calendar` `ink2` | “Calendar permission needed” `ink2`; tooltip “Answer invitations: SuperSparrow needs permission to update your calendar.” The address line stays: the account still syncs. The sync line keeps Up to date and its tooltip carries the same words | small secondary “Reconnect”, which asks for the permission beside mail (§5.44) |
| Reconnecting (waiting for the browser, §5.44) | {{layout.progress.spinner.0}} pt `ink3` spinner | “Sign in required” stays in `danger` in place of the address (it is still true); the status block reads “Waiting for your browser” `ink2` | small secondary “Cancel”, in Reconnect's place |
| Error | `exclamationmark.triangle.fill` `danger` | “Couldn't sync” `danger`; the reason in plain words in the tooltip and the detail | small secondary “Retry” |
| Demo account | `info.circle` `ink3` | “Made up, nothing syncs” `ink3` | — |

Every glyph, word and bar meets its pair on `sheet`, `selWash` and `selWashIdle` (§7; `ink2` and `ink3` words through the row pairs).

The group is **not** a `List(selection:)`: SwiftUI's list selection paints an accent block with white text that cannot be restyled. It is a `VStack` of rows inside a `.focusable()` container: click selects; ↑/↓ move the selection (`onMoveCommand`); ⌫ asks to remove; Tab enters and leaves the group as one stop, drawing the focus ring around the selected row inset {{border.focusRing.width}} pt; each row reports `.isSelected` to accessibility.

**+/−.** Two joined square buttons, {{layout.settings.plusMinus}} × {{layout.settings.plusMinus}} each, `raised`, {{border.strong.width}} pt `ruleStrong` edge with a shared middle edge, outer radius `r.sm`, glyphs `plus` / `minus` {{layout.settings.plusMinusGlyph}} pt `ink2`. Hover `raised + hover`, glyph `ink`; pressed `raised + pressed`; focused ring around the focused half (each half is its own Tab stop); − disabled (`inkDisabled`, no fill change) when nothing is selected. + opens the add-account menu (§5.44) under the button; − is Remove….

| Accounts state | Look |
|---|---|
| Accounts listed | as above |
| No accounts | the group holds one {{layout.settings.accountRow}} pt row: “No accounts yet” (`body` `ink2`) over “Add one to start syncing.” (`snippet` `ink3`), centred; − disabled; + enabled |
| Adding (waiting for the browser) | a pending row at the end of the group: {{layout.settings.accountFace}} pt `fill` face with a dashed `ruleStrong` ring and a {{layout.progress.spinner.1}} pt `ink3` spinner, “Finish signing in in your browser…” (`body` `ink`) over “Gmail” (`snippet` `ink2`), trailing small secondary “Cancel”; + is disabled until it resolves |
| Add failed | the pending row leaves; a danger banner above the group: “Couldn't add the account” + the reason (multi-line) + link “Try again” |
| Reconnecting | the account's row shows the Reconnecting status above; + and every other Reconnect and Retry are disabled until it resolves |
| Demo mode | an info banner above the group (§5.43) |

**Notices.** Banners at the top of a tab's content, {{layout.settings.noticeGap}} pt above the first control, multi-line (§5.38): info for guidance, danger for failures. A confirmation of a button press is not a notice: it sits beside the button that caused it (OAuth “Saved”, below). The OAuth tab's incoming notice, shown when an add-account attempt found no client, is an **info** banner (`info.circle`): “To add a Gmail account, SuperSparrow needs your own Google OAuth client ID. Enter it below and click Save, then try again.” with the link “How to create one…” (opens `docs/OAUTH-SETUP.md`); it stays until Save succeeds or the tab is left. When the OAuth settings file exists but cannot be read, the tab opens with a **danger** banner instead: “Couldn't read the OAuth settings.” + the reason + “Correct the fields below and click Save.”; it stays until a save succeeds (§5.33). The seven-accounts notice (above) is the Accounts tab's only info banner besides demo mode; when both apply, demo mode's shows.

**OAuth.** One form, top to bottom (the incoming notice above it when present). Labels `body` `ink`, right-aligned in the {{layout.settings.labelColumn}} pt column; fields start {{layout.settings.labelGap}} pt after it and fill to the content's trailing padding ({{layout.settings.fieldWidth}} pt at the {{layout.settings.width}} pt window: `layout.settings.fieldWidth`), {{layout.settings.fieldHeight}} pt high, on {{layout.settings.row}} pt rows; section titles in `title` `ink` at the content's leading edge, {{layout.settings.sectionGap}} pt above their first row.

| Row | Label | Control | Placeholder (`ink3`) | Font |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Section | “Google” | — | — | `title` |
| 1 | Client ID | text field | “….apps.googleusercontent.com” | `mono` |
| 2 | Client secret | secure field (§5.25: bullets, reveal button on hover) | “GOCSPX-…” | `mono` |
| Section | “Microsoft” | — | — | `title` |
| 3 | Client ID | text field | “00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000” | `mono` |
| 4 | Tenant | text field | “common” | `mono` |
| Caption | — | in the field column, {{layout.settings.captionGap}} pt under the last field, max {{layout.settings.captionMaxWidth}} pt wide, `meta` `ink3`: “Register a Desktop app client in Google Cloud Console with the Gmail API enabled, and a Mobile and desktop app in Microsoft Entra with http://localhost as its redirect URI. Leave Tenant as common to allow both work and personal Microsoft accounts.” followed by the link “How to create these…” (`meta`, `accent`; opens `docs/OAUTH-SETUP.md`) | — | — |
| Buttons | — | {{layout.settings.sectionGap}} pt under the caption: “Show File in Finder” (small secondary, at the field column's leading edge; disabled until the file exists) and, trailing at the content padding, “Save” (small primary, the default button, enabled only when a field changed) | — | — |

**“Saved”** is a status beside Save, not a banner: `checkmark.circle.fill` `success` {{layout.settings.statusGlyph}} pt + “Saved” (`snippet` `ink2`), {{layout.settings.savedGap}} pt before the Save button, from a successful save until the next edit (VoiceOver announces “Saved”). A failed save is a danger banner at the top of the tab (“Couldn't save the OAuth settings.” + the reason). Validation errors sit under their field (§5.25: “That doesn't look like a Google client ID.”).

**General** (a two-column grid: labels right-aligned in {{layout.settings.labelColumn}} pt, rows {{layout.settings.row}} pt, a hairline between groups; nine controls, ten once the roadmap's update check lands, under its cap of twelve): List density (radio: Standard, Compact) · Rows (checkbox: Show account initials in unified views; the seven-accounts caption under it, above) · Undo send (popup: 5, 10, 20 seconds) · — · Remote images (toggle “Load by default”, caption “Off: images load only when you ask, per message.”) · Notifications (toggle “New mail”; checkbox “Show sender and subject”; while macOS keeps them from SuperSparrow, its line, below) · — · Keep mail on this Mac (below) · — · At login (below) · Mail links (below). Font and Text size moved to Appearance in 1.8. General never scrolls and is never cut where Accounts is not: at its tallest (the seven-accounts caption, Notifications' line with its caption, At login's tallest line and Mail links' failure line, all at once) it stays shorter than Accounts at its tallest (760 pt of content).

**Keep mail on this Mac.** How far back messages keep their full content on this Mac: a popup, Everything (the default), 1 year, 3 months, and {{layout.settings.mailboxSizeGap}} pt after it the space the mailbox takes in plain words (“292 MB”: the mail database, less the pages it keeps free for reuse after a clean-up, and the attachment cache, decimal units as Finder counts, `meta` `ink2`; VoiceOver reads “292 MB on this Mac”). Under the row, in the field column {{layout.settings.captionGap}} pt below it (`meta` `ink3`): “Older mail stays in the list and in search, and downloads again when you open it.” The size is measured when the tab appears, when the window reopens on it, and after each clean-up while the window is open; never on a timer, and until the first answer the row holds the popup alone, never a placeholder number. A message received before the window keeps its row, its snippet and its text for search; its full content goes, and opening it fetches it again as for mail the download has not reached yet (§5.14: the skeleton while it loads, the offline preview without a network), after which it stays until the app next starts; so does mail shown in the reader, so a clean-up never empties a conversation on screen or its attachments. Older mail the download brings in arrives already cleaned up, so the mailbox never takes its full size first. Choosing a shorter window cleans up at once, in the background, and the size follows when it is done; choosing a longer one, or Everything, removes nothing more and downloads nothing back (those messages fill in again as they are opened). Drafts, messages waiting to send and messages with an action still syncing are never touched. Attachments opened on this Mac are kept up to 1 GB, the least recently used going first.

**Notifications' line.** macOS, not the app, decides whether SuperSparrow's notifications show, and with them the Dock badge (§5.28): with notifications off for it in System Settings › Notifications (or Don't Allow answered to its prompt) macOS shows none and draws no badge, though the New mail toggle reads on and the app sets the count. So under the Notifications rows, whatever the toggle says (the badge shows with New mail off), a {{layout.settings.row}} pt row in the field column says so, in At login's approval shape: `{{icon.notificationsOff}}` `ink2` {{layout.settings.statusGlyph}} pt, {{layout.settings.statusGlyphGap}} pt, the words (`snippet` `ink2`), {{layout.settings.statusButtonGap}} pt, a small secondary button. macOS keeps the answer and is asked whenever the tab appears and whenever the app comes to the front while it shows; until the first answer, and while nothing stands in the way, there is no line.

| macOS's answer | Line |
|---|---|
| Allowed, with Badge application icon on; or no notification center (a bare build) | none |
| Off for SuperSparrow | “Off in System Settings”, “Open Notifications”, which opens System Settings at Notifications on SuperSparrow's page; under it the caption “No notifications and no Dock badge until they're allowed there.” |
| Allowed, with Badge application icon off | “Dock badge off in System Settings”, “Open Notifications” |
| Never asked | “Not allowed yet”, “Allow…”, which puts macOS's prompt up; the line follows its answer |

Allowed there, the line goes as the app comes back to the front. No alert, no key, no menu item.

**At login.** Whether SuperSparrow opens when the user logs in, so new mail notifies from then on (§5.41): a toggle “Open SuperSparrow” (VoiceOver reads “Open SuperSparrow at login”), the first row of the last group, which is SuperSparrow's place in macOS. macOS keeps the answer (the app as a login item, `SMAppService`), not the app's settings: it is asked whenever the tab appears (the window reopening on it included) and whenever the app comes to the front while it shows, since the user can approve or remove the item in System Settings meanwhile; until the first answer the row holds no toggle, rather than one that may flip. The toggle applies at once: it moves as pressed, asks macOS once and ignores presses until macOS answers, then shows what macOS says, which wins over any error on the way. No key, no menu item: it is a setting like the rest of General.

| At login state | Look |
|---|---|
| Off, on | the toggle, off or on. Off includes a copy macOS has not met yet (it answers not found until the first register, which a press makes): the toggle stays usable |
| Needs approval (asked for and waiting for the user in System Settings › General › Login Items & Extensions, or turned off there since) | the toggle on; under it a second {{layout.settings.row}} pt row in the field column: `{{icon.loginApproval}}` `ink2` {{layout.settings.statusGlyph}} pt, {{layout.settings.statusGlyphGap}} pt, “Needs approval in System Settings” (`snippet` `ink2`), {{layout.settings.statusButtonGap}} pt, “Open Login Items” (small secondary), which opens System Settings at Login Items & Extensions. Approved there, the line goes as the app comes back to the front; turning the toggle off withdraws the request |
| Unavailable (a build run outside an app bundle, or macOS still cannot find this copy after a press asked it to add it, until it answers anything else) | the toggle off and disabled (§5.26); under it, in the field column {{layout.settings.captionGap}} pt below the control and at most {{layout.settings.captionMaxWidth}} pt wide, a caption (`meta` `ink3`): “Not available for this copy of SuperSparrow. Move it to the Applications folder and open it from there.” |
| The change failed (macOS did not end where the user asked) | the toggle where macOS says it is, enabled; under it, Mail links' error help line (below) in the same place: “Couldn't set SuperSparrow to open at login. You can add it in System Settings › General › Login Items & Extensions.”, or for turning it off “Couldn't stop SuperSparrow opening at login. You can remove it in System Settings › General › Login Items & Extensions.” It stays until macOS is found where the user asked, through a retry too, and gives way to Unavailable's caption. One line under the toggle at a time: a turn-off that fails while macOS waits for approval shows these words, which name Login Items themselves, in the approval line's place. Never an alert |

**Mail links.** The last row says which app opens `mailto:` links (§5.20). LaunchServices is asked whenever the tab appears (the window reopening on it included) and whenever the app comes to the front while it shows, since the user can choose another mail app in Mail's settings meanwhile; until the first answer the row holds nothing, rather than a button that may vanish.

| Mail links state | Look |
|---|---|
| SuperSparrow opens mail links | `checkmark.circle.fill` `success` {{layout.settings.statusGlyph}} pt, {{layout.settings.statusGlyphGap}} pt, “SuperSparrow is your default mail app.” (`snippet` `ink2`) at the field column's leading edge: the same status as OAuth's “Saved”. No button |
| Another app opens them | “Set as Default Mail App” (small secondary, at the field column's leading edge). It asks macOS to make SuperSparrow the default; macOS confirms in its own dialog, and the button is disabled until it answers. The row then shows what LaunchServices says: the words above, or the button again when the user kept the other app, with no message (a choice, not a failure) |
| The change failed | the button stays, enabled; under it, in the field column {{layout.settings.captionGap}} pt below the control and at most {{layout.settings.captionMaxWidth}} pt wide, a field's error help line (§5.25: `exclamationmark.triangle.fill` {{layout.textField.helpGlyph}} pt, {{layout.textField.helpGlyphGap}} pt, `meta` `danger`): “Couldn't make SuperSparrow the default mail app. You can also choose it in Mail › Settings › General.” It stays until an attempt succeeds or SuperSparrow is found to be the default: through a retry too, under the disabled button while macOS asks and after the user keeps the other app. Never an alert |

States per control as §5.24–5.27, Quire-drawn; validation errors inline under the field.

**Appearance** (§4.16), on General's grid: labels `body` `ink` right-aligned in the {{layout.settings.labelColumn}} pt column, controls {{layout.settings.labelGap}} pt after it, {{layout.settings.row}} pt rows, captions `meta` `ink3` {{layout.settings.captionGap}} pt under their control and at most {{layout.settings.captionMaxWidth}} pt wide, a hairline between the groups. Font and Text size moved here from General: they are how mail looks and belong beside the theme. The tab has no buttons, so no new verb, and it never scrolls (about 374 pt of content, far below Accounts').

| Row | Label | Control | Under it |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Theme | the five theme tiles (below) in a row at the field column, {{layout.settings.themeTileGap}} pt apart (`settings.themeTileGap`), each with its name {{layout.settings.themeNameGap}} pt below in `label`; row height {{layout.settings.themeRow}} (`settings.themeRow`) | the chosen theme's caption (§4.16), {{layout.settings.themeCaptionLines}} lines reserved (`settings.themeCaptionLines`) so a switch never moves the rows below |
| 2 | Appearance | radio group in one line (§5.26): Automatic · Light · Dark. VoiceOver reads the first “Automatic, follows macOS” | — |
| 3 | Dark Mode | checkbox “Adapt mail that sets its own colours” (on). Disabled, its caption kept, while Appearance is Light, where it would change nothing; with Automatic it stays enabled, since night will come | “Off: it opens in the sender's colours, on a white page. ⇧⌘O switches one message.” |
| — | hairline | | |
| 4 | Font | popup: New York, SF Pro (§4.4) | — |
| 5 | Text size | popup: Small, Default, Large, Larger (§4.15) | — |

**The theme tile** (`QuireThemeTile`, inside the `QuireThemePicker` radio group): a {{layout.settings.themeTile.0}} × {{layout.settings.themeTile.1}} pt miniature of the main window (`settings.themeTile`), radius `r.md`, drawn from **that theme's** tokens in the **current** mode with Increase Contrast and Graphite applied, so a Graphite user sees what they will get: a {{layout.settings.themeTileSpine}} pt `spine` strip; a {{layout.settings.themeTilePaper}} pt `paper` column with a {{layout.settings.themeTilePip}} pt `accent` pip and two {{layout.settings.themeTileLine}} pt `ink3` lines; and `canvas` holding a `sheet` card (hairline `rule`) with two `ink` lines and one `accent` line, {{layout.settings.themeTileInset}} pt in. Its edge is a {{border.strong.width}} pt `ruleStrong` of the **current** theme (the pair “Secondary button edge vs desk”). Five tiles and four gaps fill the {{layout.settings.fieldWidth}} pt field column exactly. The choice is marked as the active tab is (§4.14), by weight and a mark under the name, never by a ring: a ring is the focus ring's alone.

| Tile state | Look |
|---|---|
| Chosen | name in `labelStrong` `ink`, with a {{layout.settings.themeUnderline}} pt `selRule` underline {{layout.settings.themeUnderlineGap}} pt below it, as wide as the name (`selRuleIdle` while Settings is not key, as the margin rule does); the picture keeps its edge |
| Not chosen | name in `label` `ink2`; no underline |
| Hover | the name turns `ink`; the picture never takes a wash |
| Pressed | the underline shows in `ruleStrong` under the pressed tile's name until mouse-up, then the choice applies |
| Focused | the group is one Tab stop; ← → (and ↑ ↓) move the choice and apply it at once, stopping at the ends, as a radio group does; the Quire focus ring (§4.1) goes round the chosen tile's picture and name. It is the only ring the picker ever draws |
| VoiceOver | a radio group “Theme”; each tile reads as “Study, 1 of 5, selected”; the picture is hidden from accessibility; the caption is the group's help text |

Every change applies at once, Settings included: the window re-themes in place, keeping keyboard focus on the tiles and every field's state (§8.3). There is no hover preview and no Reset button: choosing Study is the reset. The values live in `UserDefaults` (`theme` = `study`, `appearance` = `automatic`, `adaptsMailColours` = true), and a value this version does not know reads as the default, so a fresh install, and every existing one, opens in Study, Automatic, adapted. The underline is two pairs of §7 (“Theme name underline, chosen, Settings key / not key”, `selRule` and `selRuleIdle` on `canvas`); the name, caption, edge and focus ring are existing pairs.

### 5.41 Notifications

`UNUserNotificationCenter`; macOS draws them, Quire supplies content. **Permission**: macOS's prompt is asked for while macOS has no answer and New mail is on, when an account is added (so it comes with an obvious reason) and at launch while there are accounts, the demo's included; macOS keeps the answer, not the app's defaults, and once answered only System Settings changes it, which Settings › General then says (§5.40, Notifications' line). One notification per new conversation; `threadIdentifier` = account id so they group; `targetContentIdentifier` = conversation id, so clicking opens and selects it.

| Field | Preview on | Preview off (Settings) |
|---|---|---|
| Title | sender display name (“Ben Carter”), else the address, else “No sender” | “New message” |
| Subtitle | subject, else “No subject” | account name |
| Body | first line of the snippet, max {{layout.notification.bodyMaxChars}} characters | none |
| Summary (more than {{layout.notification.summaryThreshold}} within {{layout.notification.summaryWindowSeconds}} s) | “3 new messages” · account name | same |

Sound: system default, off while the app is frontmost. No notification for mail that arrives while its conversation is open in a key window, or during an account's first sync (its first page of newest mail, including mail of that page whose rows land just after it). New mail notifies while older mail downloads behind it; the older mail itself never does, since it predates the first page. Nor does a conversation coming back from Later (§5.45): it is not new mail, and the Inbox badge and its row's Back tag say it came back. Nor does mail a mute or a sender rule took out of the Inbox as it arrived (§5.51, §5.52): it never reaches the Inbox, a muted conversation never notifies even where the user moved it back, and a row whose newest message is not itself in the Inbox (a rule's archive in a conversation the Inbox still holds for older mail, the user's own reply) stays quiet.

**Actions.** A notification for one conversation offers three actions (one category, registered at launch), which macOS lists in this order. The summary offers none: it names no conversation to act on, and a click on it only brings the app forward.

| Action | Copy | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| Archive | “Archive” | archives the conversation, as E does; the app stays where it is |
| Mark as Read | “Mark as Read” | marks every message of the conversation read; the app stays where it is |
| Reply | “Reply” | brings the app forward with a reply composer (§5.20) to the conversation's latest message, from the conversation's account, as R opens one; a button, not a text field in the notification |

Archive and Mark as Read are the window's own changes, applied on this Mac at once and queued to sync exactly as the keys apply them. Taken from a notification they show no toast and leave nothing for Z or ⌘Z: the undo history belongs to the window (§6.1), and the user acted elsewhere. The row leaves the inbox, or loses its unread mark, as a change synced from another device would. An action on a conversation that is gone, on one the change would leave as it is (already archived, already read), or on an account that is not open (a demo notification once the real accounts are back, or the other way round) does nothing, silently. An action that launches the app waits until the main window has read its accounts.

### 5.42 App icon

The brand mark, the paper plane, in black and white: a white plane on a near-black tile, by default and in dark alike, and white on black for the system to tint. On the macOS {{layout.appIcon.canvas}} template (y down) the body is the {{layout.appIcon.body}} square centred on the canvas, its continuous corners {{layout.appIcon.corner}} of the body (185.4, Apple's corner curve). The plane is drawn on the template's 128 × 128 construction grid of 8 px units, with an isometric lattice laid over it (verticals every 80 px and lines at 1:2 both ways, anchored on the tail point S): the glyph's {{GLYPH:ss.paperPlane:grid}} square fills the {{layout.appIcon.markSize}} square at ({{layout.appIcon.markX}}, {{layout.appIcon.markY}}), one unit to 8 px, so every vertex is a grid point: N (840, 296), WF (200, 456), T (328, 520), S (360, 536), WN (520, 616), Sk (360, 560), X (504, 632), K (360, 776). The plane is 640 × 480, from (200, 296) to (840, 776), placed optically rather than by its box: the point midway between its box's centre (520, 536) and its ink's centroid (500, 493) lies within 3 px of the canvas's centre on each axis (1.8 px left, 2.6 px low), so the wings sit up and right of centre and the keel sits low. Its ink covers about 13% of the body. The nose clears the body's edge by about 83, the far wing's tip by 100 and the keel's foot by 148, outside Icon Composer's rim.

| Part | Default | Dark | Tinted and clear |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tile (the document's fill) | `{{layout.appIcon.tile}}` | `{{layout.appIcon.tileDark}}` | `{{layout.appIcon.tileTinted}}`, which the system tints |
| Plane (one layer) | `{{layout.appIcon.plane}}` | `{{layout.appIcon.planeDark}}` | `{{layout.appIcon.planeTinted}}` |

**Why a white plane on black.** The plane is the colour of paper, and the two folds read as shade. A black tile is rare in the Dock, so the icon is easy to find, and it has the build of Spark's icon (a solid tile, a white plane) without the blue. It is the highest contrast available, and the same two colours as the welcome screen's mark, the wordmark and the lockup, so the icon and the interface read as one drawing. The dark appearance keeps it as it is; the tinted one hands the system the white plane on black, so the plane is the luminance the system tints. There is no other hue, no gradient, no texture and no sheen: glass, specular highlights, shadow and translucency are all off in the document, and any grey the icon shows is the system's (Liquid Glass's edge, the tinted and clear looks, the Dock's reflections), never drawn.

**Small sizes.** The 16 and 32 px images are pixel-fitted masters, not reductions (`layout.appIcon.fit16`, `fit32`, drawn in pixels): the body snaps to whole pixels ({{layout.appIcon.fit16.body}} = 12 px, 2 px in from each side; {{layout.appIcon.fit32.body}} = 26 px, 3 px in), and the plane is redrawn on that size's own pixel grid as straight-edged polygons, every vertex on a pixel corner, and rendered anti-aliased like every other size, so its diagonals stay straight lines and the nose a sharp point. At 32 px it is 21 × 15 px: the two wings are one contour, the fold between them kept as a short dark notch from (10, 16) to (20, 12), and the keel is its own triangle under a gap that opens from 1 to 1.5 px, so no parallel-sided cut breaks into two grey stripes. At 16 px it is 10 × 10 px: the wings are one triangle, the nose raised a pixel so it still climbs, and the keel is its own triangle under a 1 px gap, its tip stepped 1:1 to stay on whole pixels. Every other size, 64 to 512 px, is the 1024 px master reduced with Core Graphics' high-quality resampling, which is how the approved images were made (`sips -Z`): drawn from the paths at each size instead, the edges come out softer, up to 56 levels apart at 64 px.

**How it is built.** `swift scripts/tools/app-icon.swift` draws everything above from `tokens.json` alone (the `ss.paperPlane` path and `layout.appIcon`). It writes `Resources/AppIcon.icon`, an Icon Composer document that `actool` compiles for macOS 26 and later: the tile is the document's fill, specialized for dark and tinted; the plane is one layer, `plane.svg`, drawn on the document's canvas (the body), its solid fill specialized for dark and tinted, in one group with glass, specular, shadow and translucency off. Its colours live only in `icon.json`; there are no per-appearance copies of the artwork to drift. It also writes `Resources/AppIcon.icns`, the default appearance on the macOS 15 template (the same continuous corners, and the template's shadow, {{layout.appIcon.shadowY}} down, {{layout.appIcon.shadowBlur}} blurred, black at {{layout.appIcon.shadowAlpha}}, outside the body only), packed by `iconutil` from 16 to 1024 px; inside the body every size is the approved image, pixel for pixel, the 16 and 32 px masters included. The shadow is the macOS 15 template's convention, outside the tile, never part of the mark. The `.icns` is the bundle's icon only where the asset catalog is not read: a build without Xcode's `actool`, and tools that open `CFBundleIconFile`. Once `actool` has compiled the document, the system reads `CFBundleIconName` from `Assets.car` on every supported macOS, so macOS 15 shows `actool`'s own flat renderings of the document (16 to 512 pt at 2× and 1024 px), which carry Liquid Glass's edge and reduce the plane rather than drawing the pixel-fitted masters. `actool` ignores an `AppIcon.appiconset` that shares the document's name (checked with Xcode 27.2), and an image set cannot carry dark or tinted images, so the approved pixels on macOS 15 and the document's appearances on macOS 26 cannot both ship; the build keeps the document. `--preview <png>` renders the document's six system appearances through Xcode's `ictool` beside the raster sizes, and the icon in the Dock at 64 and 32 px among Apple's white-tile apps and Mail, for review only. `--check` writes nothing: it renders into a temporary folder, compares byte for byte with `Resources/AppIcon.icon` and `Resources/AppIcon.icns`, and exits 1 listing every stale file; `scripts/test.sh` runs it, and `brand-assets.swift --check` for `docs/brand`, beside `quire-tokens.swift --check`.

The Dock badge stays system red. The icon shares its mark with the welcome screen, About, inbox zero, alerts and notifications, and nowhere else.

**Distinct from Apple Mail and from other planes.** Mail is a white envelope on a blue tile; this icon has no envelope, no blue and no circle, in any appearance (App Review Guideline 5.2.5). Spark's plane is rounded, upright and symmetric, on blue. Telegram's is also a white plane heading up and to the right with a fold, but it sits in a blue circle and has a notched tail and a keel that hangs below; this one sits on a black squircle, with a straight trailing edge and a flush keel. Stock send icons and Font Awesome's `paper-plane` have a separate tail piece that sticks out below the wing; here the keel stays inside the outline. It does not reproduce SF Symbols' `paperplane` or `paperplane.fill`, which Apple does not allow in app icons or logos: that is a symmetric 45° arrowhead with rounded joins and a slotted notch, where this has three triangles, sharp corners and a vertical keel back, and heads up at 26.6°. A look-alike sheet (kept outside the repository, comparison only) sets it beside each of them.

**Brand marks.** The symbol, the wordmark and the lockup are in `docs/brand` (SVG and PNG, with their use and clear space), built by `swift scripts/tools/brand-assets.swift` from the mark's path, `layout.brand`, `layout.appIcon` and the wordmark's outlines (`docs/brand/source/wordmark.svg`, drawn, never set in a font: straight segments, mitred joins and 45° chamfers, cap height {{layout.brand.capHeight}}, x-height 520, descender 190, stroke 100, the S's spine at the plane's 1:2 slope). Two colours only, both the icon's: ink `{{layout.brand.ink}}` and paper `{{layout.brand.paper}}`. The palette is written once (`BRAND` in `docs/design/source/tokens_def.py`), and `layout.brand` and the icon's colours are read from it. Beside the word the plane is {{layout.brand.lockupMark}} wordmark units tall (1.75 cap heights), its box centred on the cap height (y {{layout.brand.lockupCentre}}, baseline 0) and {{layout.brand.lockupGap}} (0.55 cap heights) before the word's origin. The interface's one use of the word, under the welcome screen's mark (§5.39), draws `layout.brand.wordmark`, which `tokens_def.py` reads from the same master, so the two never part.

**Alignment with the interface.** A bundle has one icon, so the icon is black and white in every theme and appearance; the interface draws the same mark as a template in the brand's black and white (welcome) and the system's secondary label grey (inbox zero). A theme never recolours the icon or the mark, and the icon never colours the rest of the interface. The mark is `ss.paperPlane` from the token file, so the welcome screen, inbox zero and the icon are one drawing, and the welcome screen's name is the wordmark's own outline.

### 5.43 Demo mode

“Try the demo” opens the main window on two made-up mailboxes stored only on this Mac (`demo.sqlite`). Everything behaves as with real accounts except that nothing syncs or sends; the demo must never be mistaken for real mail, so it is marked in words in four places.

| Where | Marker | States |
|---|---|---|
| Masthead | a “Demo” tag ({{layout.tag.height}} pt, `tag` role, `ink2`, `ruleStrong` edge, radius `r.xs`) {{layout.listHeader.demoTagGap}} pt after the masthead (after its chevron in minimal mode, or after the minimal-mode dot, §5.3), in every view and in minimal mode while the header line keeps search's minimum beside it; on a line too crowded for both (minimal mode at the default list width, any list at its minimum width) it steps off the line and the other three markers carry the demo | it is a pull-down button: hover `hover` wash and `ink` text; pressed / menu open `pressed`; focused ring; tooltip “These mailboxes are made up and live only on this Mac.” Its menu: “Add a Real Account…” (submenu: Add Gmail Account…, Add Microsoft Account…) · — · “Leave Demo…” |
| Window title | “SuperSparrow Demo” (Window menu, Mission Control, VoiceOver; the title bar itself hides titles, §3.2) | — |
| Sync line | `info.circle` `ink3` + **Demo** · made-up mail, nothing syncs | replaces Up to date; no action |
| Settings › Accounts | an info banner above the group: `info.circle` + “Demo mode: these mailboxes are made up and live only on this Mac.” + link “Leave demo…” (sentence case in a banner, §4.13) | the demo accounts list as usual, with the tag “Demo” in place of the provider; Reconnect and Colour work, Remove is replaced by Leave Demo |

**Send Later in the demo.** The first time the demo's mailboxes are made, one message waits in the outbox for Monday 09:00 (Tomorrow's on a Sunday), so Drafts shows a row reading “Sends Mon 09:00” (§5.46) from the start, and the Outbox lists it too, its rail tile showing from launch until the message has gone or its schedule is cancelled (§5.53). It keeps no draft: opening it makes one from the message.

**The demo's calendar.** The demo accounts each have a calendar of made-up events for this week, a few a day in their inks, with an invitation in the Inbox whose event overlaps one of them (hatched, and named on its card), a snoozed conversation coming back later this week and the Send Later message above, so the day column, the week and their marks show from the start (§5.54). Its blocks are kept on this Mac, as everything in the demo is.

**Leaving.** Leave Demo… (tag menu and File menu; “Leave demo…” in the Settings banner) asks with a sheet alert: “Leave the demo?” / “The made-up mailboxes are removed from this Mac. You'll return to the welcome screen.” / buttons “Leave Demo” (default) and “Cancel”. Nothing real is lost, so the alert has no destructive button. Confirming wipes the demo store and shows the welcome screen (`dur.slow` cross-fade).

**Adding a real account from the demo.** “Add a Real Account…” starts the normal flow (§5.44). When the first real account is added, the demo leaves with it: the alert “Replace the demo with this account?” / “The made-up mailboxes are removed from this Mac when your account is added.” / “Continue” (default), “Cancel” comes first, then the browser sign-in; on success the window shows the real inbox.

### 5.44 Adding an account from the main window

Rail +, File › Add Gmail Account… / Add Microsoft Account… and Settings › Accounts + share one flow. The provider is chosen in a menu, never an alert.

| Step | Spec |
|---|---|
| Choose | rail + (and Settings +) opens a system pull-down menu {{layout.rail.addMenuOffsetY}} pt below the button, leading-aligned: `{{icon.addAccountProvider}}` “Add Gmail Account…” · `{{icon.addAccountProvider}}` “Add Microsoft Account…”. The File menu has both items directly |
| Kind of Microsoft account | before a Microsoft sign-in, an alert sheet: “Which kind of Microsoft account?” / “An address can be both a work or school account and a personal Microsoft account. Choose the one that holds your mail.” / “Work or School” (default), “Personal”, “Cancel”. Work or school signs in through `organizations` (or the one organisation Settings › OAuth names), personal through `consumers`, and the account renews and reconnects through it from then on, so Microsoft never picks the other mailbox of the same address. A work and a personal account with one address are two accounts |
| No OAuth client for that provider | an alert sheet: “Set up a Google OAuth client first” / “SuperSparrow needs your own Google OAuth client ID to add a Gmail account.” / “Open Settings” (default), “Cancel”; Open Settings shows Settings › OAuth with the info notice of §5.40 |
| Waiting for the browser | the browser opens; the rail gets a pending tile at the end of the stack (§5.2); the sync line reads “**Finish signing in in your browser** · Gmail” with the link “Cancel” (it joins the Tab order, §5.11); Settings › Accounts shows the pending row. Only one sign-in runs at a time: both + buttons and the File items are disabled meanwhile |
| Cancel | the pending tile and row leave at once; the sync line returns to its previous state; no message |
| Success | the pending tile becomes the account's tile (instant), the new account is selected, the list shows the skeleton (§5.10), the sync line reads “Downloading mail · Contoso…” |
| Failure | the pending tile leaves; an alert sheet on the main window (or Settings when started there): “Couldn't add the account” / the reason in plain words (“The browser sign-in was cancelled.”, “Google refused the sign-in. Check the client ID in Settings › OAuth.”, “Couldn't reach Google. Check your internet connection.”) / “Try Again” (default), “Cancel” |
| Already added | alert “alex@contoso.test is already added” / “Choose it in the rail or in Settings.” / “Show Account” (default), “Cancel” |

The welcome screen keeps its own in-place waiting state (§5.39) because it has no rail or sync line.

**Reconnecting for a permission** (the invitation card's “Reconnect”, or the Settings row's when it reads Calendar permission needed) is the same reconnect, with every step below, asking the provider for the permission beside mail (incremental authorization): the provider's own consent screen names the calendar access, in its own words. A reconnect for any other reason keeps a permission the account already granted. Success sends the answers that waited and takes the notice and the Settings status away; declining the permission in the browser leaves them as they were, with no message.

**Reconnecting an account** (the sync line's “Reconnect” with one account, or a Settings row's “Reconnect”) runs the same browser sign-in for an account that already exists:

| Step | Rail tile | Sync line | Settings › Accounts row |
|---|---|---|---|
| Waiting for the browser | the attention badge **stays** (the account still needs sign-in until the browser finishes); tooltip “Finish signing in in your browser…”; no spinner on the tile | state 1 of the precedence (§5.11): “**Finish signing in in your browser** · Fabrikam”, the account's name rather than the provider, with the link “Cancel” | “Sign in required” stays in place of the address; the status block shows a {{layout.progress.spinner.0}} pt `ink3` spinner and “Waiting for your browser” with small secondary “Cancel” in Reconnect's place (§5.40) |
| While it runs | one sign-in at a time: both + buttons, the File › Add items and every other Reconnect and Retry are disabled | — | — |
| Cancel (sync line or Settings) | unchanged: still needs sign-in | back to the state the precedence picks (Sign in required) | back to Sign in required with Reconnect; no message |
| Success | the attention badge leaves in the same frame; the unread badge, if any, takes its place | Checking for mail… (a check the user asked for, §5.11), then Up to date | Checking for mail…, then Up to date; the address line returns. VoiceOver announces “Fabrikam reconnected.” No toast: the badge leaving is the confirmation |
| Failure | unchanged | Sign in required | unchanged; an alert sheet on the window where it started: “Couldn't reconnect alex@fabrikam.test” / the reason / “Try Again” (default), “Cancel” (§5.33) |
| Signed in to a different account | unchanged | Sign in required | unchanged; the alert “Signed in to a different account” / “You signed in as sam@contoso.test, but this account is alex@fabrikam.test. Nothing was changed. Try again and choose alex@fabrikam.test in your browser.” / “Try Again” (default), “Cancel”. Nothing is replaced or added |

### 5.45 Later (snooze)

L puts the selection away until a time: it is archived now and comes back to the Inbox then, its latest message unread. Both halves are the ordinary Archive and Move to Inbox, so the phone and the web agree; only the timer lives on this Mac. One key, one conditional tile (§5.2), four presets and a date, no setting.

**The Later menu.** A system `NSMenu`, so its highlight, keyboard (↑/↓, ↩, Esc, type-to-select) and states are the system's. L pops it on the selection (one conversation or several, in the list or with the conversation open), hung from the lead row: its top-leading corner at the row's text column (x = {{layout.row.textX}}, {{layout.rowCompact.textX}} in compact rows), {{layout.menu.titleMenuOffsetY}} pt below the row, as the title menu hangs from the masthead (`menu.titleMenuOffsetY`); when focus is not in the list (the reader has it), from the reader header's Archive button, at its leading edge, the same distance below.
